\chapter{Atiyah--Spencer Calculus and Higher Brave-New Lie Operations}
\label{ch:atiyah-spencer}

\section{Purpose, continuity, and the operation-comparison problem}
\label{sec:as-purpose}

The preceding two chapters constructed the nonlinear and differentiated sides
of brave-new Lie theory.  On the nonlinear side, a brave-new formal Lie
groupoid
\[
    \mathfrak G_\bullet\rightrightarrows X
\]
has its effective formal quotient
\[
    q_{\mathfrak G}:X\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G},
\]
its canonical nonlinear anchor
\[
    \phi_{\mathfrak G}:
    \mathfrak G_\bullet\longrightarrow R_{X/S,\bullet},
\]
its formal isotropy group
\[
    I^{\mathrm{for}}_{\mathfrak G}\longrightarrow X,
\]
its descended formal adjoint group
\[
    \operatorname{Ad}^{\mathrm{for}}(\mathfrak G)
    \longrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G},
\]
formal adjoint transport, the formal-disk monad $T_{\mathfrak G}$, the jet
comonad $J_{\mathfrak G}$, and effective Cartan descent.  Effectivity gives
\[
    \mathfrak G_\bullet
    \simeq
    \check C_\bullet
    \left(
        X\xrightarrow{q_{\mathfrak G}}Q_{\mathfrak G}
    \right).
\]

On the differentiated side, the preceding chapter constructed the intrinsic
stable package
\[
    \mathbb A^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathfrak G},
    \qquad
    \mathbb I^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathfrak G}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb F^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathfrak G},
    \qquad
    \mathfrak l^{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak G},
\]
and, on coherent spectral coefficient charts, the spectral partition-Lie
algebroid
\[
    \mathbb A^{\pi,E_\infty}_{\mathfrak G}
    \in
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{X/S}.
\]
It is obtained from the globally constructed spectral formal leaf
\[
    \mathcal L^{\mathrm{fm,sp}}_{\mathfrak G}
    \in
    \operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{X/S}
\]
by the global differentiation equivalence
\[
    \operatorname{Diff}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{X/S}:
    \operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{X/S}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{X/S}.
\]
In rational coherent coefficients the same formal leaf rectifies to the dg
formal leaf and the spectral controller rectifies to
\[
    \mathbb A^{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathfrak G}.
\]

The purpose of the present chapter is to construct the operation calculus
relating these nonlinear and differentiated objects.  Four resolution
mechanisms occur, and they are kept separate.

\begin{enumerate}
    \item The \emph{geometric Cech scalar object} is obtained by applying
    additive scalar functions to the actual groupoid nerve.  Its degree zero is
    the scalar coefficient on $X$, its degree one sees $\mathfrak G_1$, and its
    normalized degree-one term has a constructed first-neighbourhood cotangent
    comparison.

    \item The \emph{Cartan comonadic cobar} is attached to an actual
    $J_{\mathfrak G}$-coalgebra.  Its cosimplicial degree $n$ is
    $J_{\mathfrak G}^{n+1}E$.  The coalgebra coaction supplies the terminal
    coface which a bare comonad does not supply.  The augmented cobar is
    canonically split and therefore totalizes to the Cartan coefficient.

    \item The \emph{cofree cobar} is the Cartan cobar of the cofree coalgebra
    $(J_{\mathfrak G}E,\delta_E)$.  It exists for every coefficient and is the
    canonical object on which arbitrary co-Kleisli differential operators act.

    \item The \emph{Artinian cotangent-primitive operation system} is constructed
    on coherent spectral Artinian tests.  The nonlinear support-marked Cech
    geometry is retained as a separate raw/formalized comparison layer.  The
    higher algebraic operations are extracted instead from directional
    square-zero cubes which switch the independent derivation directions on
    and off.  The exact scalar diagonal is then compared, through the canonical
    cotangent--square-zero comonadic resolution, with the multi-filtered
    cotangent cobar.  Its continuous pro-coherent dual is the integral spectral
    partition-Lie operation system.
\end{enumerate}

The first three objects live in parameterized stable coefficients and encode
relation descent.  The fourth lives in the coherent Artinian deformation
context and encodes the algebraic operations detected by formal-moduli
differentiation.  The geometric scalar Cech object and the scalar Cartan
cobar are related by the source-fixed decalage theorem below; they are not
defined to be the same object.

The same separation is retained after Artinian formalization.  Write
\[
    \ell_{\mathfrak G}:
    Q_{\mathfrak G}\longrightarrow Q^{\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G}
\]
for quotient formalization.  It induces a simplicial morphism
\[
    \lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}:
    \mathfrak G_\bullet
    \longrightarrow
    R^{\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}
    :=
    \check C_\bullet
    \left(
        X\to Q^{\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G}
    \right).
\]
The corresponding right-adjoint jet comparison has the opposite variance.
Since
\[
    q^{\mathrm{fm}}=\ell_{\mathfrak G}q_{\mathfrak G},
\]
there is a canonical equivalence
\[
    J^{\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G}
    \simeq
    q_{\mathfrak G}^*\ell_{\mathfrak G}^*
    \Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}\Pi_{q_{\mathfrak G}},
\]
and the counit of
$\ell_{\mathfrak G}^*\dashv\Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}$ gives a canonical
morphism of comonads
\[
\boxed{
    \kappa^J_{\mathfrak G}:
    J^{\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G}
    \longrightarrow
    J_{\mathfrak G}.
}
\]
Thus relation objects are covariant from raw to formalized, whereas scalar
functions and right-correspondence jet comonads are contravariant.  The
formalized scalar Cartan cobar consequently maps to the raw scalar Cartan
cobar, in the same direction as formalized scalar restriction after
source-fixed decalage.

The integral algebraic comparison is carried out on spectral Artinian cells.
For a coherent affine coefficient map $R\to B$ and a complete almost-finite
augmentation $A\to B$, put
\[
    P_{A/B}:=B\otimes_A B.
\]
The Cech diagonal has normalized primitive cotangent coefficient
\[
    L_{B/P_{A/B}}[-2]
    \simeq
    L_{B/A}[-1].
\]
It is useful to regard the left side as a functor
$\operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}$ on complete almost-finite arrows.
The displayed equivalence is a natural equivalence
\[
    \eta^{\mathrm{bar}}:
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{cot}_{B/R}.
\]
For a square-zero object the comonad counit is transported through this
natural equivalence from the actual cotangent--square-zero adjunction; it is
not supplied by a Cech degeneracy.  Naturality with respect to the adjunction
unit identifies the Cech-unit primitive cooperation with the genuine comonad
comultiplication.  Hence the resulting Artinian Spencer coLie comonad is
canonically equivalent, as a comonad, to the already constructed
cotangent--square-zero coLie comonad.

The geometric-to-algebraic comparison has two genuinely different
linearization stages.  The first is geometric formalization,
\[
\boxed{
\text{raw pointed Cech geometry}
\longrightarrow
\text{formalized pointed Cech geometry}.}
\]
A coherent Artinian point
$\widetilde q:\operatorname{Spec}A\to Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$ does not
identify the formalized relation with the affine Cech nerve of $A\to B$.
Instead it canonically supplies a simplicial test map
\[
    \check C_\bullet
    (\operatorname{Spec}B/\operatorname{Spec}A)
    \longrightarrow
    R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
\]
Relative reduced stabilization is covariant along this map.  Applying
internal scalar Hom reverses the variance and gives a map from the
\emph{scalar dual} of formalized support-marked geometry to the corresponding
Artinian scalar support bar.  The geometric stable object itself is not
silently identified with its scalar dual.

The second stage is cotangent linearization.  On a finite set $I$ of
independent square-zero directions one forms the multi-filtered square-zero
test $A_I\to B$ and the multi-filtered cotangent--square-zero comonad $G_I$.
Two constructions are then kept separate.

First, the actual multi-derivative is obtained from the \emph{directional
Artinian cube}: for every $J\subseteq I$ one switches off the directions outside
$J$ before forming the Cech self-intersection.  The total fibre of this cube is
canonically the all-positive multiweight part of the source-fixed Cech
primitive.  This construction is independent of the support-marked geometric
cells; no identification of the two cubes is made.

Second, the canonical cotangent--square-zero adjunction cobar of $A_I$
produces an augmented scalar-diagonal cobar
\[
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
\]
and the cofree cotangent cobar
\[
    \mathsf{CotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
\]
The relative scalar Hurewicz morphism and the Cech-diagonal cotangent
equivalence give an actual morphism of augmented cosimplicial objects
\[
    h_I^{\mathrm{comon},\bullet}:
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathsf{CotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
\]
Its augmented degree $-1$ is the exact multiweight scalar diagonal comparison.
The cofree cotangent cobar is correctly indexed by
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^{n+2}(D_I)$ in cosimplicial degree $n$.

Thus the geometric and algebraic ladders are
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\text{raw marked geometry}
&\longrightarrow
\text{formalized marked geometry},\\
\text{formalized scalar support dual}
&\xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{supp,prim}}_{\widetilde q}\ }
\mathbf{RedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A/B}(I),
\end{aligned}}
\]
and, independently on the universal directional square-zero test,
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\left(\mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)\right)^0
&\longrightarrow
\mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{-1}_{B/R}(I;D_I),\\
N^\bullet\mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
&\xrightarrow{\ h_I^{\mathrm{comon}}\ }
\mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I),\\
\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}\mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
&\xrightarrow{\sim}
\operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)\\
&\xrightarrow{\sim}
\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)
\xrightarrow{\ \vee\ }
\text{spectral partition-Lie operations}.
\end{aligned}}
\]
The first line of the second display is the canonical quotient from the
all-positive directional scalar coefficient to exact multiweight.  The two
cosimplicial directions---Cech descent and the cotangent--square-zero
comonadic resolution---meet at this diagonal coefficient but are not
identified.  The equivalences begin only after the independently constructed
primitive cotangent cobar has been totalized.

An arbitrary Artinian point of $Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$ need not lift
to $Q_{\mathfrak G}$, so no raw Artinian primitive object is postulated.  The
full PD substitution system is constructed on the Artinian multiweight side
from multi-filtered square-zero geometry and comonad composition; no
substitution law is imposed on raw geometric cells.

A second raw/formalized distinction is required for adjoint coefficients.
The descended nonlinear formal adjoint group lives over
$Q_{\mathfrak G}$.  Right direct image along $\ell_{\mathfrak G}$ and
pullback along $q^{\mathrm{fm}}$ construct a formalized adjoint group over
$X$ and a canonical counit comparison to actual formal isotropy.  Tangent
differentiation therefore gives a canonical representation on the
\emph{formalized adjoint coefficient} together with a comparison to the
independently constructed differentiated actual-isotropy controller.  An
equivalence between those coefficients is asserted only when the comparison
is separately proved invertible; in particular it is an equivalence when
quotient formalization is an equivalence.

\paragraph{Non-conflation convention.}
The geometric scalar Cech object, Cartan cobar, cofree cobar, first-order
principal-parts object, formalized support-marked stable object, its scalar
dual, reduced scalar Artinian bars, directional Artinian Cech cross-effects,
source-fixed directional primitives, comonadic scalar-diagonal cobars,
primitive cotangent cobars, multi-filtered diagonal cotangent primitives,
Artinian Spencer coLie coalgebra, multiweight operation system, tangent enveloping
monad, formalized adjoint coefficient, actual-isotropy controller
$\mathfrak i^{\pi,E_\infty}_{\mathfrak G}$, homotopy anchor fibre
$\mathbb F^{\pi,E_\infty}_{\mathfrak G}$, Hopf-derived primitive spectral Lie
algebra $\mathfrak l^{\mathrm{sp}}_{\mathfrak G}$, spectral partition-Lie
algebroid $\mathbb A^{\pi,E_\infty}_{\mathfrak G}$, and rational dg
controller $\mathbb A^{\mathrm{Lie}}_{\mathfrak G}$ remain distinct
constructions.  Identifications are made only by comparison theorems.

\paragraph{No hidden operation package.}
No finite jet order, reduced-jet factorization, bar decomposition, PBW
presentation, enveloping presentation, Spencer presentation, representation
datum, connection, splitting, curvature tensor, pronilpotence datum,
admissibility class, formal-moduli recognition datum, affine presentation,
operator-algebra augmentation, scalar-Hurewicz equivalence,
actual-isotropy ambient representation, or spectral-to-animated comparison is
part of a formal groupoid or its differentiated algebroid.  The Cech objects,
cobars, normalized Spencer objects, totalization towers, stable Artinian
section localizations, tangent representation categories, tangent enveloping
monads, support-marked geometric cells, scalar duals, scalar Artinian bars,
directional Artinian square-zero cubes, comonadic scalar-diagonal Hurewicz
comparison, primitive cotangent cobars, multi-filtered diagonal cotangent
primitives, multiweight Artinian operation systems,
principal-parts filtrations, one-sided kernel module actions, continuous
source coduals, iterated completed Cech corings, distribution free-module
bars, connection spaces, rooted-tree attachment objects, obstruction towers,
convolution completions, and comparison morphisms are constructed
functorially.

\paragraph{Coefficient-domain convention.}
The raw relation, geometric scalar Cech object, stable normalization, Cartan
cobar, cofree cobar, co-Kleisli calculus, common-base support-marked Cech
constructions, bi-reduced nonlinear commutator, and Cartan-transport
constructions are intrinsic in the native big spectral Nisnevich
$\infty$-topos.  The first structured neighbourhood, first-jet Atiyah
extension, and quasi-coherent principal-parts formulas are constructed on the
represented first-order range.  The directional mixed square-zero commutator
and the higher cotangent-primitive operation system are constructed on
coherent spectral Artinian charts.  Completed principal parts, the two-sided
pro-coherent kernel algebra, its one-sided coefficient action, continuous
source coduality, and the distribution free-module bar are constructed on
coherent spectral Artinian charts.  The pro-coherent partition-Lie comparison
is formed on coherent affine coefficient charts and globalized by the
two-variable spectral-etale descent of the preceding chapter.  When a
represented arrow is unitwise formalized, the completed arrow need not remain
represented; its split first-order probe is nevertheless canonically
corepresented by the original represented cotangent coefficient.
Representability is used only on the stated represented first-order,
quasi-coherent cotangent, principal-parts, or ordinary tangent interfaces.
Perfectness or ordinary dualizability is used only when a continuous dual of
a symmetric power is replaced by the corresponding ordinary symmetric power
of the dual.  Rationality is used only for dg rectification, PBW/CE formulas,
and the additive Maurer--Cartan equation.  These are theorem domains, not
fields of structure on $\mathfrak G$.

\paragraph{Chapter-level output.}
The chapter constructs the iterated jet correspondence; geometric scalar
Cech cochains; Cartan and cofree cobars and their source-fixed decalage
comparison; normalized Spencer objects and complete totalization filtrations;
co-Kleisli differential operators and their cofree Spencer action; first-jet
Atiyah extensions and quasi-coherent principal parts; tangent representation
categories and stable Artinian section linearization; the formalized relation
and correctly varianced formalized-to-raw jet/cobar maps; affine and global
tangent enveloping monads; the bar--cotangent equivalence; the transported
Artinian Spencer coLie comonad and its integral partition-Lie comparison; raw
and formalized support-marked Cech cells and their discrepancy; the canonical
Artinian Cech test map; formalized scalar support duals; reduced and primitive
support scalar Artinian bars; directional Artinian square-zero cubes and their
all-positive Cech cross-effects; the comonadic scalar-diagonal Hurewicz
comparison; primitive cotangent cobars; multi-filtered diagonal cotangent
primitives and the Artinian multiweight PD operation system; the filtered tangent enveloping
monad; coefficient connections, the corolla--latching extension theorem,
curvature, Bianchi, and the complete flatness obstruction tower; the
right-normalized nonlinear-isotropy commutator and its integral arity-two
cotangent comparison; completed adic principal parts and finite-order operator
kernels; the one-sided pro-coherent kernel action, continuous source coduality,
iterated completed Cech corings, and the distribution free-module-bar
reconstruction of completed Artinian Spencer cochains; Artinian-cell
Kan-extension reconstruction; the formalized adjoint representation and its
comparison to differentiated actual isotropy; rational PBW--Spencer and
relative $R$-linear CE--Spencer comparisons; complete Cartan transport; the
integral monadic twisting system; the rational convolution $L_\infty$
structure and its canonically complete convolution target; and the one-object,
action, gauge, and classical smooth specializations.

\section{The groupoid jet correspondence and its iterates}
\label{sec:as-jet-correspondence}

Fix
\[
    \mathfrak G_\bullet
    \in
    \operatorname{FormLieGpd}(X/S)
\]
and abbreviate
\[
    q:=q_{\mathfrak G}:X\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G}.
\]
Effectivity gives
\[
    \mathfrak G_n
    \simeq
    \underbrace{
        X\times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}}\cdots\times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}}X
    }_{n+1}.
\]
Let
\[
    p_i^{(n)}:\mathfrak G_n\longrightarrow X,
    \qquad
    0\leq i\leq n,
\]
be the vertex projections.  Retain
\[
    \mathcal E_X
    :=
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal X_{/X})
\]
and the stabilized dependent adjoint triple
\[
    \Sigma_f\dashv f^*\dashv\Pi_f.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Degree-one jet correspondence]
\label{prop:as-degree-one-jet}
There is a canonical equivalence of exact endofunctors
\[
\boxed{
    J_{\mathfrak G}
    =q^*\Pi_q
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \Pi_s t^*,
}
\]
where
\[
    s=p_0^{(1)},
    \qquad
    t=p_1^{(1)}:
    \mathfrak G_1\rightrightarrows X.
\]
Under this equivalence the comonad counit is induced by the unit
$u:X\to\mathfrak G_1$, and the comultiplication is the mate of groupoid
composition on
\[
    \mathfrak G_2
    \simeq
    \mathfrak G_1\times_{t,X,s}\mathfrak G_1.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
\mathfrak G_1 \arrow[r,"t"] \arrow[d,"s"'] &
X \arrow[d,"q"]\\
X \arrow[r,"q"'] &
Q_{\mathfrak G}
\end{tikzcd}
\]
is Cartesian.  Right Beck--Chevalley gives
$q^*\Pi_q\simeq\Pi_s t^*$.  The counit and comultiplication are those of the
adjunction $q^*\dashv\Pi_q$.  Under the Cech presentation, their mates are
respectively the diagonal and the two-arrow composition map.  Coherence is
the coherent Beck--Chevalley pasting of the complete Cech nerve.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Iterated jet correspondence]
\label{thm:as-iterated-jet}
For every $n\geq1$ there is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    J_{\mathfrak G}^{\,n}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \Pi_{p_0^{(n)}}
    \left(p_n^{(n)}\right)^*.
}
\]
These equivalences are natural under the face and degeneracy maps which are
generated by composition, insertion of units, and deletion of composable
relation factors.  All identities and higher simplicial coherences agree with
the corresponding comonad coherences.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $n=1$ this is Proposition~\ref{prop:as-degree-one-jet}.  Let
\[
    r_n:\mathfrak G_{n+1}\longrightarrow\mathfrak G_n
\]
forget the final vertex, and let
\[
    v_{n,n+1}:\mathfrak G_{n+1}\longrightarrow\mathfrak G_1
\]
retain the final arrow.  There is a Cartesian square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
\mathfrak G_{n+1}
  \arrow[r,"v_{n,n+1}"]
  \arrow[d,"r_n"'] &
\mathfrak G_1
  \arrow[d,"s"]\\
\mathfrak G_n
  \arrow[r,"p_n^{(n)}"'] &
X.
\end{tikzcd}
\]
Hence right Beck--Chevalley gives
\[
    (p_n^{(n)})^*\Pi_s
    \simeq
    \Pi_{r_n}v_{n,n+1}^*.
\]
Using the inductively constructed equivalence and Fubini,
\[
\begin{aligned}
J^{n+1}
&\simeq
\Pi_{p_0^{(n)}}(p_n^{(n)})^*\Pi_s t^*\\
&\simeq
\Pi_{p_0^{(n)}}\Pi_{r_n}
v_{n,n+1}^*t^*\\
&\simeq
\Pi_{p_0^{(n+1)}}
(p_{n+1}^{(n+1)})^*.
\end{aligned}
\]
Every equivalence is a canonical mate of a Cartesian square.  Pasting
coherence for mates identifies the unit and composition relations with the
simplicial identities.  Since the face and degeneracy maps of the Cech nerve
are generated by these operations, the assertion follows.
\end{proof}


\section{Geometric Cech cochains, Cartan cobars, and cofree cobars}
\label{sec:as-cobar}

Write
\[
    p:X\longrightarrow S
\]
for the structure map and
\[
    a_n:=p\,p_i^{(n)}:\mathfrak G_n\longrightarrow S.
\]
The expression is independent of the chosen vertex $i$ because every vertex
lies over the same point of $S$.

\subsection{Geometric scalar Cech cochains}

\begin{definition}[Geometric scalar Cech object]
\label{def:as-geometric-cech}
For $n\geq0$ define
\[
\boxed{
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{n}
    :=
    \Pi_{a_n}a_n^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
    \in\mathcal E_S.
}
\]
For a simplicial operator
\[
    \alpha^*:\mathfrak G_n\longrightarrow\mathfrak G_m
\]
the identity $a_n=a_m\alpha^*$ and the adjunction unit
\[
    a_m^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
    \longrightarrow
    \Pi_{\alpha^*}(\alpha^*)^*
    a_m^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
\]
give a morphism
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{m}
    \longrightarrow
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{n}.
\]
Thus
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}
    \in
    \operatorname{Fun}
    \left(
        \Delta,
        \operatorname{CAlg}(\mathcal E_S)
    \right)
\]
is a cosimplicial commutative algebra object.
\end{definition}

\begin{proof}
The equality of structural maps makes the adjunction-unit construction
functorial in composable simplicial operators.  Pullback is strong symmetric
monoidal and dependent product is lax symmetric monoidal, so every term is a
commutative algebra and every cosimplicial structure map is multiplicative.
The simplicial identities imply the cosimplicial identities.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Spherical geometric Cech object]
\label{def:as-geometric-spherical}
Define
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathbb S}^{n}
    :=
    \Pi_{a_n}\mathbb S_{\mathfrak G_n}.
\]
The same construction gives
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathbb S}^{\bullet}
    \in
    \operatorname{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal E_S).
\]
\end{definition}

The scalar coefficient on $X$ is
$p^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}\simeq\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}$, so
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{0}
    \simeq
    \Pi_p\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]
In particular, normalized degree one sees the actual arrow relation
$\mathfrak G_1$ and not $\mathfrak G_2$.

\subsection{Cartan comonadic cobar}

A bare comonad does not supply the terminal coface in the cosimplicial cobar
used here.  That coface is supplied by a coalgebra coaction.

\begin{definition}[Cartan cobar]
\label{def:as-cartan-cobar}
Let
\[
    (E,\rho_E)
    \in
    \operatorname{Coalg}_{J_{\mathfrak G}}(\mathcal E_X).
\]
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{n}(E,\rho_E)
    :=
    J_{\mathfrak G}^{\,n+1}E,
    \qquad
    n\geq0.
}
\]
There are $n+2$ cofaces from degree $n$ to degree $n+1$: the first
$n+1$ are obtained by inserting
$\delta:J\to J^2$ into one of the $J$-factors and the final coface is
\[
    J^{n+1}\rho_E:
    J^{n+1}E\longrightarrow J^{n+2}E.
\]
For $n\geq1$ there are exactly $n$ ordinary codegeneracies from degree $n$
to degree $n-1$, obtained by inserting the counit
$\varepsilon:J\to\operatorname{id}$ between adjacent $J$-factors.  The
outer counit used below to split the augmented cobar is an \emph{extra}
codegeneracy and is not one of these ordinary cosimplicial structure maps.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Cosimplicial identities for the Cartan cobar]
\label{prop:as-cartan-cobar-cosimplicial}
Definition~\ref{def:as-cartan-cobar} canonically defines a cosimplicial
object.  Its cosimplicial identities are exactly the coassociativity and
counit identities of the comonad together with the two coalgebra identities
for $\rho_E$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Two insertions of $\delta$ agree by comonad coassociativity.  An insertion of
$\delta$ followed by the terminal coface agrees with the terminal coface
followed by $\delta$ by
\[
    \delta_E\rho_E
    \simeq
    J(\rho_E)\rho_E.
\]
Insertion of $\varepsilon$ gives the codegeneracies.  The mixed identities
are the two comonad counit identities together with
$\varepsilon_E\rho_E\simeq\operatorname{id}_E$.  The coherent versions of
these identities supply all higher cosimplicial coherence.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Geometric realization of the Cartan cobar]
\label{thm:as-jet-cech-cobar}
Let $(E,\rho_E)$ be a Cartan coefficient and let
\[
    \theta:s^*E\xrightarrow{\sim}t^*E
\]
be its equivalent coherent groupoid transport.  For every $n\geq0$ there is
a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{n}(E,\rho_E)
    \simeq
    \Pi_{p_0^{(n+1)}}
    (p_{n+1}^{(n+1)})^*E.
}
\]
Under these equivalences the first $n+1$ cofaces are induced by composition
of adjacent relation arrows, the final coface uses the Cartan endpoint
transport of $E$, and the codegeneracies are induced by units.  The
comparison is coherent in every simplicial degree.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The degreewise equivalence is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-iterated-jet}.  The comultiplication of $J$ is the mate of
composition in the Cech relation, so insertion of $\delta$ is composition of
adjacent arrows.  Under effective Cartan descent, the coaction $\rho_E$ is
the mate of the coherent endpoint transport, giving the final coface.  The
counit is the unit-section restriction.  Beck--Chevalley pasting and the
Cartan cocycle identities identify all higher simplicial relations.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Cartan coaugmentation]
\label{def:as-cart-coaugmentation}
The coaction
\[
    \rho_E:E\longrightarrow J_{\mathfrak G}E
\]
defines the augmentation-degree map
\[
\boxed{
    E\longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}(E,\rho_E).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Canonical splitting of the augmented Cartan cobar]
\label{prop:as-cartan-cobar-split}
The augmented cosimplicial object
\[
    E\longrightarrow
    J E\rightrightarrows J^2E\rightrightarrows\cdots
\]
admits canonical extra codegeneracies.  Consequently it is a split augmented
cosimplicial object.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In cosimplicial degree $n\geq0$ define the extra codegeneracy by applying the
outer counit,
\[
    h_n:
    J^{n+1}E
    \xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{J^nE}}
    J^nE,
\]
where degree $-1$ is interpreted as $E$.  The identity
$h_0\rho_E\simeq\operatorname{id}_E$ is the coalgebra counit identity.  The
remaining extra-degeneracy identities are obtained by moving
$\varepsilon$ past an insertion of $\delta$ or past the terminal coaction.
They are respectively the comonad counit identities and the naturality of
$\varepsilon$ together with $\varepsilon_E\rho_E\simeq\operatorname{id}_E$.
Thus the augmented cobar has the standard coherent splitting.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cobar descent for Cartan coefficients]
\label{thm:as-cobar-descent}
For every Cartan coefficient,
\[
\boxed{
    E
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname*{Tot}
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}(E,\rho_E).
}
\]
The equivalence is natural in Cartan morphisms and compatible with the
fixed-object and arbitrary-base-change variance of the raw jet calculus.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A split augmented cosimplicial object is a limit diagram.  Apply
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-cartan-cobar-split}.  Naturality follows from
naturality of the extra codegeneracies.  Exact pullback carries the comonad,
coalgebra structure, and the counit splitting to the corresponding
base-changed data, giving base-change compatibility.  Effective Cartan
descent is used separately to identify
$\operatorname{Coalg}_{J_{\mathfrak G}}(\mathcal E_X)$ with coefficients over
$Q_{\mathfrak G}$; it is not needed to prove this totalization identity.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Cofree cobar and co-Kleisli coefficients}

\begin{definition}[Cofree cobar]
\label{def:as-cofree-cobar}
For an arbitrary $E\in\mathcal E_X$, the cofree coalgebra is
\[
    (J_{\mathfrak G}E,\delta_E).
\]
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Cobar}^{\mathrm{cofr},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    :=
    \operatorname{Cobar}^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}
    (J_{\mathfrak G}E,\delta_E).
}
\]
Thus
\[
    \operatorname{Cobar}^{\mathrm{cofr},n}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    =
    J_{\mathfrak G}^{\,n+2}E.
\]
\end{definition}

This object exists for every coefficient and is the canonical recipient of
co-Kleisli operator actions.  No Cartan structure on $E$ is required.

\subsection{Source-fixed scalar decalage}

\begin{definition}[Source-fixed decalage]
\label{def:as-source-decalage}
Let
\[
    \sigma_s:\Delta\longrightarrow\Delta
\]
be the ordinal-sum functor
\[
    \sigma_s([n])=[0]\star[n]\simeq[n+1],
    \qquad
    \sigma_s(\alpha)=\operatorname{id}_{[0]}\star\alpha.
\]
For a cosimplicial object $C^\bullet$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Dec}_sC^\bullet
    :=C^\bullet\circ\sigma_s.}
\]
Thus
\[
    (\operatorname{Dec}_sC)^n=C^{n+1},
\]
with
\[
    d_{\operatorname{Dec}}^i=d_C^{i+1},
    \qquad 0\leq i\leq n+1,
\]
and
\[
    s_{\operatorname{Dec}}^i=s_C^{i+1},
    \qquad 0\leq i\leq n-1.
\]
Equivalently, vertex $0$ is held fixed: the source-changing coface $d^0$ and
the matching source codegeneracy $s^0$ are omitted.  This convention is fixed
for the rest of the chapter.
\end{definition}

The additive scalar coefficient has a canonical Cartan structure because
\[
    q^*\mathcal O_{Q_{\mathfrak G}}^{\mathrm{add}}
    \simeq
    \mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]
Let $\rho_{\mathcal O}$ denote its induced $J_{\mathfrak G}$-coaction.

\begin{theorem}[Scalar Cech--cobar decalage]
\label{thm:as-scalar-decalage}
There is a canonical equivalence of cosimplicial objects
\[
\boxed{
    \Pi_p
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}},\rho_{\mathcal O})
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Dec}_s
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}.}
\]
Equivalently, in degree $n\geq0$,
\[
    \Pi_p
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{n}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}},\rho_{\mathcal O})
    \simeq
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{n+1}.
\]
The same statement holds for the spherical coefficient.  Under this
identification the outer counit
\[
    h_n:J^{n+1}\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}\to
    J^n\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
\]
which splits the augmented Cartan cobar corresponds to the omitted
source codegeneracy $s^0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:as-jet-cech-cobar},
\[
    \operatorname{Cobar}^{n}_{\mathfrak G}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})
    \simeq
    \Pi_{p_0^{(n+1)}}
    (p_{n+1}^{(n+1)})^*
    \mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]
Base change of the additive coefficient identifies the pullback on the right
with $a_{n+1}^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}$, and Fubini gives
\[
    \Pi_p\Pi_{p_0^{(n+1)}}
    (p_{n+1}^{(n+1)})^*
    \mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
    \simeq
    \Pi_{a_{n+1}}a_{n+1}^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]
The $n+1$ cofaces obtained by inserting $\delta$ are the Cech cofaces
$d^1,\ldots,d^{n+1}$ which preserve the source vertex.  The Cartan endpoint
coaction supplies the remaining source-fixed coface $d^{n+2}$.  The $n$
ordinary counit insertions are the Cech codegeneracies
$s^1,\ldots,s^n$.  Hence the degreewise equivalences assemble exactly along
the ordinal-sum functor $\sigma_s$.  The omitted $s^0$ is the outer counit,
which is therefore the extra codegeneracy of the augmented cobar.
\end{proof}

\section{Stable normalization and complete Spencer filtrations}
\label{sec:as-spencer-normalization}

Stable Dold--Kan is applied separately to the geometric, Cartan, and cofree
cosimplicial objects.

\begin{definition}[Geometric scalar Spencer cochains]
\label{def:as-spencer}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{geom},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}
    :=
    N^\bullet C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}(\mathcal E_S).
}
\]
Likewise define
\[
    \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{geom},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G,\mathbb S}
    :=
    N^\bullet C_{\mathfrak G,\mathbb S}^{\bullet}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Cartan and cofree Spencer cochains]
\label{def:as-cartan-cofree-spencer}
For a Cartan coefficient $(E,\rho)$ put
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{Cart},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}(E,\rho)
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}(E,\rho).
}
\]
For arbitrary $E\in\mathcal E_X$ put
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{cofr},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \operatorname{Cobar}^{\mathrm{cofr},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}(E).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Dold--Kan totalization convention]
\label{def:as-dk-totalization}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a stable $\infty$-category admitting the relevant limits.
Stable Dold--Kan gives an equivalence
\[
    N^\bullet:
    \operatorname{Fun}(\Delta,\mathcal C)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}(\mathcal C).
\]
For a coherent nonnegative cochain object $K^\bullet$, define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}K^\bullet
    :=
    \operatorname{Tot}
    \left(
        (N^\bullet)^{-1}K^\bullet
    \right).
}
\]
Thus, whenever $K^\bullet=N^\bullet C^\bullet$,
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}N^\bullet C^\bullet
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet.
}
\]
The notation $\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}$ will be used only when the
normalized coherent cochain object, rather than its inverse Dold--Kan
cosimplicial object, is being displayed.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[No first-order collapse]
The geometric scalar Spencer cochains are normalized functions on the full
formal Cech nerve.  They are not exterior powers of a cotangent complex,
symmetric coalgebras, Chevalley--Eilenberg complexes, or partition complexes
by definition.  Their first-order cotangent quotient is constructed below.
The Cartan and cofree Spencer complexes are decalage-related descent
resolutions and are not substituted for the geometric degree-one object.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Ordered Alexander--Whitney structure]
\label{prop:as-spencer-multiplicative}
The geometric scalar Cech object is a cosimplicial commutative algebra.
Consequently its normalized terms carry the natural ordered
Alexander--Whitney maps
\[
    N^pC_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}
    \otimes
    N^qC_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}
    \longrightarrow
    N^{p+q}C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}.
\]
After passage to the homotopy category these maps are associative and unital
and satisfy the graded Leibniz identity for the normalized differential.
Every partial and complete totalization is canonically a commutative algebra
as a limit of cosimplicial commutative algebras.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This is the ordered Alexander--Whitney construction of Chapter~10 applied to
the cosimplicial commutative algebra
$C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}$.  The statement deliberately retains
the theorem strength established there: no separate homotopy-coherent
$E_1$-lift on the un-totalized normalized object is inferred.
\end{proof}

\section{The complete Spencer totalization filtration}
\label{sec:as-spencer-filtration}

For any one of the cosimplicial objects $C^\bullet$ constructed above write
\[
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\leq r}C^\bullet
    :=
    \lim_{\Delta_{\leq r}}C^\bullet.
\]

\begin{definition}[Partial-totalization filtration]
\label{def:as-partial-totalization}
Set
\[
    F_{\mathrm{Sp}}^0\operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
    :=
    \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
\]
and, for $r\geq0$,
\[
\boxed{
    F_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{r+1}\operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
        \longrightarrow
        \operatorname{Tot}_{\leq r}C^\bullet
    \right).
}
\]
Define
\[
    \operatorname{gr}_{\mathrm{Sp}}^r
    \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(
        F_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{r+1}
        \longrightarrow
        F_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{r}
    \right).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Complete Spencer filtration]
\label{thm:as-complete-spencer-filtration}
The preceding construction is a functorial complete descending filtration and
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{gr}_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{r}
    \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
    \simeq
    N^rC^\bullet[-r].
}
\]
Moreover
\[
    \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \lim_r\operatorname{Tot}_{\leq r}C^\bullet.
\]
For a Cartan coefficient,
\[
    E
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Tot}
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}(E,\rho_E),
\]
so the filtration gives a canonical complete descent filtration of the
Cartan coefficient itself.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The simplex category is the filtered union of the finite truncations
$\Delta_{\leq r}$.  Limits over a filtered union of full indexing
subcategories are computed as the inverse limit of the corresponding finite
partial limits, giving
\[
    \operatorname{Tot}C^\bullet
    \simeq
    \lim_r\operatorname{Tot}_{\leq r}C^\bullet.
\]
Stable Dold--Kan identifies the fibre of
\[
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\leq r}C^\bullet
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\leq r-1}C^\bullet
\]
with $N^rC^\bullet[-r]$.  Comparing the two fibre sequences defining
$F_{\mathrm{Sp}}^r$ and $F_{\mathrm{Sp}}^{r+1}$ gives the displayed
associated-graded equivalence.  The Cartan statement is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-cobar-descent}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[No numerical jet-order filtration]
The partial-totalization filtration records cosimplicial descent depth.  It is
not a filtration by a chosen number of reduced jet factors.  Finite-order
differential operators are constructed later from the functorial derived
adic filtration of principal parts.
\end{remark}

\section{Spectral co-Kleisli differential operators}
\label{sec:as-differential-operators}

\begin{definition}[Spectral groupoid differential operators]
\label{def:as-diff-operators}
For $E,F\in\mathcal E_X$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Diff}_{\mathfrak G}(E,F)
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{\mathcal E_X}
    (J_{\mathfrak G}E,F).
}
\]
For
$f:JE\to F$ and $g:JF\to H$, define
\[
    g\star f:
    JE
    \xrightarrow{\delta_E}
    J^2E
    \xrightarrow{Jf}
    JF
    \xrightarrow{g}
    H.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Spectral co-Kleisli category]
\label{prop:as-cokleisli}
The preceding formula defines a spectral-enriched co-Kleisli category.
For every $E$,
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal D^{\mathrm{coKl}}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{\mathcal E_X}(JE,E)
}
\]
is an $E_1$-ring spectrum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Associativity is the comonad coassociativity identity and the counit supplies
the unit.  The coherent higher homotopies are inherited from the comonad.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Cofree lift]
\label{con:as-cofree-operator-lift}
For $f:JE\to E$ define
\[
\boxed{
    f^\sharp:
    JE
    \xrightarrow{\delta_E}
    J^2E
    \xrightarrow{Jf}
    JE.
}
\]
The map $f^\sharp$ is a morphism of cofree $J$-coalgebras.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Universal cofree Spencer operator action]
\label{thm:as-universal-spencer-action}
The cofree lift induces a canonical morphism of $E_1$-ring spectra
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal D^{\mathrm{coKl}}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{End}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{cofr},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    \right).
}
\]
The action preserves the complete partial-totalization filtration of the
cofree cobar.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The cofree cobar is the Cartan cobar of $(JE,\delta_E)$.  Since
$f^\sharp$ is a coalgebra morphism, functoriality of the Cartan cobar gives a
cosimplicial endomorphism.  Normalization gives the displayed action.
Composition of cofree lifts agrees with co-Kleisli composition, hence the map
is $E_1$-multiplicative.  Cosimplicial endomorphisms commute with every
partial totalization.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Cartan versus cofree operator action]
An arbitrary co-Kleisli operator need not preserve a specified Cartan
coaction on $E$.  Accordingly the universal action is on the cofree Spencer
object.  If a co-Kleisli operator is induced by a Cartan endomorphism of
$(E,\rho_E)$, functoriality gives in addition its action on
$\operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{Cart},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}(E,\rho_E)$.
\end{remark}


\section{First jets and intrinsic Atiyah extensions}
\label{sec:as-atiyah-extensions}

Work in the represented first-order range of the preceding chapter.  Retain
\[
    X\xrightarrow{i_u}D^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}
    \xrightarrow{\nu_u}\mathfrak G_1
\]
and the restricted source and target
\[
    s_1,t_1:D^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}\rightrightarrows X.
\]
The completed formal arrow object need not itself remain represented:
Theorem~\ref{thm:bnla-first-neighbourhood-formalization} gives the canonical
pointed lift of this first structured neighbourhood to the unitwise
completion.

\begin{definition}[First-jet functor]
\label{def:as-first-jet}
Define
\[
    J^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}:=\Pi_{s_1}t_1^*.
\]
The unit section gives
\[
    \varepsilon_E^{(1)}:
    J^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}E\longrightarrow E.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Intrinsic Atiyah extension and connection space]
\label{def:as-atiyah-extension}
Put
\[
    \Omega^1_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        J^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}E
        \xrightarrow{\varepsilon_E^{(1)}}E
    \right).
\]
The fibre sequence
\[
\boxed{
    \Omega^1_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    \longrightarrow
    J^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}E
    \longrightarrow
    E
}
\]
is the intrinsic first-jet Atiyah extension and its boundary
\[
    \operatorname{at}_{\mathfrak G}(E):
    E\longrightarrow\Omega^1_{\mathfrak G}(E)[1]
\]
is the intrinsic Atiyah class.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak G}(E)
    :=
    \operatorname{Map}_{(\mathcal E_X)_{/E}}
    \left(E,J^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}E\right).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Connection--Atiyah equivalence]
\label{prop:as-connection-atiyah}
A point of $\operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak G}(E)$ is a section of
$\varepsilon_E^{(1)}$, equivalently a specified nullhomotopy of
$\operatorname{at}_{\mathfrak G}(E)$.  No connection is part of the input
groupoid.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This is the standard section--boundary equivalence in a stable
$\infty$-category.
\end{proof}

\section{Represented principal parts and first-order scalar linearization}
\label{sec:as-principal-parts}

Suppose the displayed first-neighbourhood geometry is represented by spectral
schemes.

\begin{definition}[Quasi-coherent first principal parts]
\label{def:as-qc-principal-parts}
For $M\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)$ define
\[
\boxed{
    P^1_{\mathfrak G}(M)
    :=
    (s_1)_*t_1^*M.
}
\]
Restriction along $i_u$ gives
\[
    P^1_{\mathfrak G}(M)\longrightarrow M.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Square-zero bimodule coincidence]
\label{lem:as-square-zero-bimodule}
Let $B'\to B$ represent the first structured unit neighbourhood on an affine
chart and put
\[
    K:=\operatorname{fib}(B'\to B).
\]
The two $B$-module structures on $K$ induced by source and target are
canonically equivalent.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The source and target maps differ by the structured first-order derivation.
Their difference acts on the square-zero fibre through multiplication of two
elements of the square-zero ideal, which is canonically zero.  The
structured square-zero classification identifies the compatible
identifications through a contractible space.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Represented first-principal-parts sequence]
\label{thm:as-principal-parts-cotangent}
For every $M\in\operatorname{QCoh}(X)$ there is a canonical fibre sequence
\[
\boxed{
    \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}
    \otimes_{\mathcal O_X}M
    \longrightarrow
    P^1_{\mathfrak G}(M)
    \longrightarrow
    M,
}
\]
where
\[
    \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}
    =
    u^*L_{\mathfrak G_1/X,s}.
\]
If $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ is represented, then
\[
    \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}
    \simeq
    L_{X/Q_{\mathfrak G}}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Affine-locally the first structured neighbourhood is a square-zero extension
$B'\to B$ with fibre
$K\simeq\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}$.  Exactness of tensor product gives
\[
    K\otimes_BM
    \longrightarrow
    B'\otimes_{B,t_1}M
    \longrightarrow
    M.
\]
Restriction through source and
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-square-zero-bimodule} identify the first term with
$\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}\otimes_BM$.  Naturality under affine
restriction glues the sequence.  The quotient formula is
Theorem~\ref{thm:bnla-relative-cotangent-identification}.
\end{proof}

The geometric scalar Cech object has degree
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^1
    =
    \Pi_{a_1}a_1^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]
Let
\[
    a_1^{(1)}:
    D^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}\longrightarrow S
\]
be the structure map.  Restriction along
$\nu_u:D^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}\to\mathfrak G_1$ gives
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^1
    \longrightarrow
    \Pi_{a_1^{(1)}}(a_1^{(1)})^*
    \mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]
Both objects map to
$C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^0=\Pi_p\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}$
by restriction along the unit.

\begin{definition}[Stabilized first-order scalar coefficient]
\label{def:as-stabilized-first-scalar}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal L^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathfrak G/S}
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        \Pi_{a_1^{(1)}}(a_1^{(1)})^*
        \mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
        \longrightarrow
        \Pi_p\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
    \right)
    \in\mathcal E_S.
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Scalar first-order linearization]
\label{cor:as-scalar-first-order-linearization}
There is a canonical morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \lambda^1_{\mathfrak G}:
    N^1C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal L^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathfrak G/S}.
}
\]
On a represented affine chart,
$\mathcal L^{\mathrm{st}}_{\mathfrak G/S}$ is the parameterized stable
realization of
$\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}$ after pushforward to $S$; if
$Q_{\mathfrak G}$ is represented, its quasi-coherent coefficient is
$L_{X/Q_{\mathfrak G}}$.  No equivalence between the full normalized
one-cochain and its first-order linearization is asserted.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Stable normalization in degree one is the fibre of the unique
codegeneracy
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^1
    \longrightarrow
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^0.
\]
Restriction to $D^{(1)}_{\mathfrak G}$ commutes with the unit restriction, so
there is a morphism between the two fibre sequences.  Taking fibres gives
$\lambda^1_{\mathfrak G}$.  The represented coefficient calculation is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-principal-parts-cotangent} together with the Chapter~10
identification of the additive stable square-zero fibre with the
quasi-coherent coefficient.
\end{proof}


\section{Tangent representations of spectral formal moduli}
\label{sec:as-tangent-representations}

Fix a coherent affine spectral coefficient map $R\to B$ and write
\[
    \mathcal F_{B/R}
    :=
    \operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R},
    \qquad
    \mathcal A_{B/R}
    :=
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}.
\]
Let
\[
    D_{B/R}:
    \mathcal F_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathcal A_{B/R}
\]
be the affine spectral differentiation equivalence.

\begin{definition}[Formal-moduli and partition-Lie representation categories]
\label{def:as-representation-categories}
For $F\in\mathcal F_{B/R}$ and $\mathfrak a=D_{B/R}(F)$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)
    :=
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \bigl((\mathcal F_{B/R})_{/F}\bigr),
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}(\mathfrak a)
    :=
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \bigl((\mathcal A_{B/R})_{/\mathfrak a}\bigr).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Tangent differentiation equivalence]
\label{thm:as-tangent-differentiation}
Differentiation induces a canonical exact equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    D_F^{\mathrm{lin}}:
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \left(D_{B/R}(F)\right).
}
\]
It is natural in morphisms of formal-moduli problems and compatible with the
one-variable and two-variable coherent spectral-etale transport of the
preceding chapter.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
An equivalence of $\infty$-categories induces an equivalence on slices over
corresponding objects.  Stabilization is functorial and preserves
equivalences.  Apply these operations to the affine differentiation
equivalence and to its coherent spectral-etale naturality squares.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Global spectral algebroid representation category]
\label{def:as-global-representation-categories}
For a global spectral partition-Lie algebroid
\[
    \mathfrak a
    \in
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{X/S}
\]
define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}(\mathfrak a)
    :=
    \lim_{(U,V)\in
    \operatorname{AffEt}^{\mathrm{coh}}(X/S)^{\mathrm{op}}}
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \left(\mathfrak a|_{U/V}\right).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Etale descent of tangent representations]
\label{prop:as-representation-etale-descent}
The affine tangent representation categories satisfy coherent
spectral-etale descent.  More precisely, if an affine-etale Cech diagram gives
\[
    \mathcal A
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname*{Tot}_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathcal A^n
\]
and $\mathfrak a$ corresponds to a compatible system $\mathfrak a_n$, then
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal A_{/\mathfrak a}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname*{Tot}_{[n]\in\Delta}
    \mathcal A^n_{/\mathfrak a_n}
}
\]
and therefore
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal A_{/\mathfrak a})
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname*{Tot}_{[n]\in\Delta}
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal A^n_{/\mathfrak a_n}).
}
\]
Consequently Definition~\ref{def:as-global-representation-categories} is
independent of any chosen affine-etale basis.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The preceding chapter proves that the affine algebroid coefficient system is
an etale stack.  Slicing over an object commutes with limits of
$\infty$-categories: an object over $\mathfrak a$ in the limit is exactly a
compatible system of objects over $\mathfrak a_n$.  Formation of spectrum
objects also commutes with limits, because pointed objects and the loop
condition are defined by finite limits.  Apply these two observations to the
algebroid descent equivalence.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Stable Schlessinger reflection and universal Artinian sections}

The linearization of a stable coefficient on the nonlinear formalized target
will be constructed directly from its sections on the actual Artinian test
category.  This avoids inserting an additional recognition theorem equating
all stable coefficients on the nonlinear target with stabilized formal-moduli
coefficients.

Fix
\[
    F\in\operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R}
\]
and put
\[
    \mathcal P_F
    :=
    \operatorname{Fun}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Art}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{R/B},
        \mathcal S
    \right)_{/F}.
\]

\begin{construction}[Relative stable Schlessinger generators]
\label{con:as-relative-stable-schlessinger-generators}
For every Artinian Schlessinger Yoneda generator
\[
    w:K\longrightarrow L
\]
of the preceding chapter and every morphism $L\to F$, regard $w$ as a
morphism in the slice $\mathcal P_F$.  Let
\[
    \mathcal W_F^{\mathrm{rel}}
\]
be the resulting set of relative generators.  In
$\operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal P_F)$ let
$\mathcal W_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}$ be the stable strongly saturated class
generated by
\[
    \Sigma^m\Sigma^\infty w,
    \qquad
    w\in\mathcal W_F^{\mathrm{rel}},\quad m\in\mathbb Z.
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{definition}[Stabilized Schlessinger reflector]
\label{def:as-stable-schlessinger-reflector}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}:
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal P_F)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \left((\operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R})_{/F}\right)}
\]
to be localization at
$\mathcal W_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Existence, identification of local objects, and etale exchange]
\label{prop:as-stable-schlessinger-exists}
The localization $L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}$ exists, is accessible and exact, and
its local objects are precisely the spectrum objects whose every shifted
infinite-loop object is Schlessinger-local in the slice.  Consequently the
local subcategory is canonically
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \left((\operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R})_{/F}\right).}
\]
The construction is natural under coherent spectral-etale change of centre
and base.  If $F'$ is the transported formal-moduli problem, then
\[
\boxed{
    f^*L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    L_{F'}^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}f^*.}
\]
These equivalences satisfy identity, composition, and higher pullback
coherences.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The slice $\mathcal P_F$ is presentable and the chosen universe makes the
relative generating family a set.  Hence the stable localization exists.
The class is closed under suspension and desuspension, so its local
subcategory is stable and the reflector is exact.

For a spectrum object $E$, locality against all
$\Sigma^m\Sigma^\infty w$ is equivalent by adjunction to locality of every
$\Omega^\infty\Sigma^{-m}E$ against $w$.  Thus every spectrum level is a
Schlessinger-local object over $F$.  Conversely a spectrum object in the
slice of Schlessinger-local objects is local against all stable generators.
This identifies the local stable category with the displayed stabilization.

The two-variable Artinian transport of the preceding chapter preserves the
identity Artinian object and every structured square-zero Schlessinger
pullback.  It therefore carries the relative generating set over $F$ to the
relative generating set over $F'$.  The universal property of stable
localization produces the exchange equivalence.  The identity, composition,
and higher-pasting coherences are inherited from Artinian transport and the
uniqueness of localization factorizations.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Universal stable Artinian sections]
\label{con:as-stable-artinian-sections}
Let $Y\to S$ be a nonlinear target carrying a centre
$U=\operatorname{Spec}B\to Y$ over
$V=\operatorname{Spec}R\to S$, and let
\[
    F(A\to B)
    =
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Map}_S(\operatorname{Spec}A,Y)
        \to
        \operatorname{Map}_S(U,Y)
    \right)
\]
be the resulting Artinian leaf.  For a stable coefficient
$E\in\mathcal E_Y$ and an Artinian arrow $A\to B$, define a functor
\[
    \mathscr S_{A,k}(E):F(A)\longrightarrow\mathcal S,
    \qquad
    x\longmapsto
    \Omega^\infty\Sigma^k\Gamma_A^{\mathrm{st}}(x^*E).
\]
Unstraighten this functor.  Its Grothendieck construction is an object over
$F(A)$,
\[
\boxed{
    \int_{x\in F(A)}
    \Omega^\infty\Sigma^k
    \Gamma_A^{\mathrm{st}}(x^*E)
    \longrightarrow F(A).
}
\]
As $k$ varies, the fibrewise spectrum structure on stable sections makes these
objects into a spectrum object in the slice.  As $A\to B$ varies, pullback of
Artinian points together with stable-section Beck--Chevalley gives the
presheaf structure.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sec}^{\mathrm{Art}}_F(E)
    \in
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal P_F)
}
\]
to be the resulting spectrum-valued Artinian presheaf over $F$.

By construction, the fibre over
$x:\operatorname{Spec}A\to Y$ is canonically the spectrum
\[
    \Gamma_A^{\mathrm{st}}(x^*E).
\]
No ordinary colimit over $F(A)$ is used: such a colimit would forget the map
to the parameter space and would not have the stated fibres.  No choice of a
prorepresenting Artinian algebra is made.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Functoriality of Artinian sections]
\label{prop:as-stable-artinian-sections-functorial}
Construction~\ref{con:as-stable-artinian-sections} is functorial in $E$, in
maps of nonlinear centred targets, and in coherent spectral-etale change of
centre and base.  Under etale transport it commutes with the corresponding
pullback of spectrum-valued Artinian presheaves.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For every Artinian test, the value is the unstraightening of the functor of
stable section spectra parametrized by the Artinian mapping space.  A map of
coefficients or centred targets gives a natural transformation of these
parametrized functors.  For coherent spectral-etale change of centre and base,
Beck--Chevalley for stable sections identifies the two possible orders of
pullback and section formation.  Unstraightening carries these natural
transformations to maps in the slice over the transported Artinian leaf.  The
identity, composition, and higher coherence are the corresponding
Beck--Chevalley and Grothendieck-construction pasting coherences.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Differentiation of Cartan coefficients}

A formal Cartan coefficient is intrinsically an object over
$Q_{\mathfrak G}$.  In stable coefficients write
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Cart}}(\mathfrak G)
    :=
    \mathcal E_{Q_{\mathfrak G}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Coalg}_{J_{\mathfrak G}}(\mathcal E_X).
}
\]

Let
\[
    \ell_{\mathfrak G}:
    Q_{\mathfrak G}\longrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
be quotient formalization.

\begin{construction}[Artinian linearization of a Cartan coefficient]
\label{con:as-artdef-cartan-coefficient}
For
$\overline E\in\mathcal E_{Q_{\mathfrak G}}$, first form the right direct
image
\[
    \Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}\overline E
    \in
    \mathcal E_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}.
\]
On a coherent affine-etale formal leaf
\[
    F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}
    =
    \mathcal L_{\mathfrak G,U/V}^{\mathrm{fm,sp}},
\]
define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Lin}_{F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}}(E)
    :=
    L_{F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}}^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}
    \operatorname{Sec}^{\mathrm{Art}}_{F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}}
    \left(
        \Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}\overline E
    \right).
}
\]
This is an object of
$\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F_{\mathfrak G,U/V})$.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Differentiation of Cartan representations]
\label{thm:as-cart-rep-differentiation}
The affine objects
\[
    D^{\mathrm{lin}}_{F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Lin}_{F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}}(E)
    \right)
\]
form a coherent etale cocartesian section.  Hence they define a canonical
functor
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{DiffRep}_{\mathfrak G}:
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Cart}}(\mathfrak G)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \left(
        \mathbb A^{\pi,E_\infty}_{\mathfrak G}
    \right).
}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-stable-artinian-sections-functorial} gives coherent
etale transport of the raw stable Artinian section presheaves.
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-stable-schlessinger-exists} identifies the
Schlessinger localizations after transport.  The formal leaves themselves
are etale cocartesian by the preceding chapter, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-tangent-differentiation} is etale natural.  These
compatibilities identify the differentiated affine representations on every
overlap and all higher intersections, so they glue to the displayed global
functor.
\end{proof}

\section{The Artinian-formalized relation and raw/formalized Spencer maps}
\label{sec:as-formalized-relation}

Retain
\[
    q:X\to Q_{\mathfrak G},
    \qquad
    q^{\mathrm{fm}}:X\to Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}},
    \qquad
    q^{\mathrm{fm}}\simeq\ell_{\mathfrak G}q.
\]

\begin{definition}[Formalized relation and jet comonad]
\label{def:as-formalized-relation}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    R_{\mathfrak G,n}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    :=
    \underbrace{
        X\times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}
        \cdots
        \times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}X
    }_{n+1}
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
    J_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    :=
    (q^{\mathrm{fm}})^*\Pi_{q^{\mathrm{fm}}}.
}
\]
No effectivity of $q^{\mathrm{fm}}$ is used to construct the relative Cech
nerve or the comonad.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary-map jet correspondence and source-fixed scalar decalage]
\label{thm:as-arbitrary-map-decalage}
Let
\[
    v:X\longrightarrow Y
\]
be an arbitrary morphism in the native $\infty$-topos, let
$b:Y\to S$, and put $p=bv$.  Define
\[
    R_{v,n}
    :=
    \underbrace{X\times_Y\cdots\times_YX}_{n+1},
    \qquad
    p_{v,i}^{(n)}:R_{v,n}\to X,
\]
and
\[
    J_v:=v^*\Pi_v.
\]
Then, without any effectivity hypothesis on $v$, the following statements
hold canonically.

\begin{enumerate}
    \item For every $n\geq1$ there is a natural equivalence
    \[
    \boxed{
        J_v^{\,n}
        \xrightarrow{\sim}
        \Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(n)}}
        \left(p_{v,n}^{(n)}\right)^*.
    }
    \]
    The comonad counit and comultiplication correspond respectively to the
    diagonal and to composition in the relative Cech relation.

    \item If $\overline E\in\mathcal E_Y$ and $E=v^*\overline E$, the
    adjunction unit gives the canonical $J_v$-coalgebra structure on $E$, and
    its Cartan cobar satisfies
    \[
    \boxed{
        \operatorname{Cobar}_v^n(E)
        \simeq
        \Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(n+1)}}
        \left(p_{v,n+1}^{(n+1)}\right)^*E,
        \qquad n\geq0.
    }
    \]
    Under this identification the first $n+1$ cofaces are relation
    composition, the final coface is the canonical endpoint identification
    induced from $\overline E$, and the codegeneracies are induced by units.

    \item Put
    \[
        a_{v,n}:=p\,p_{v,i}^{(n)}:R_{v,n}\to S
    \]
    and
    \[
        C_{v,\mathcal O}^n
        :=
        \Pi_{a_{v,n}}a_{v,n}^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}.
    \]
    Then the additive scalar coefficient
    $\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}\simeq v^*b^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}$
    has its canonical $J_v$-coalgebra structure and there is a canonical
    equivalence of cosimplicial objects
    \[
    \boxed{
        \Pi_p
        \operatorname{Cobar}_v^\bullet
        (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})
        \xrightarrow{\sim}
        \operatorname{Dec}_s C_{v,\mathcal O}^\bullet.
    }
    \]
    The extra outer counit of the augmented cobar corresponds to the omitted
    source codegeneracy $s^0$.
\end{enumerate}

All three constructions are natural in commutative squares of morphisms over
$S$, with the usual mate variance for the jet comonad.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The degree-one square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
R_{v,1} \arrow[r,"p_{v,1}^{(1)}"] \arrow[d,"p_{v,0}^{(1)}"'] &
X \arrow[d,"v"]\\
X \arrow[r,"v"'] & Y
\end{tikzcd}
\]
is Cartesian by the definition of the relative fibre product.  Right
Beck--Chevalley therefore gives
\[
    v^*\Pi_v
    \simeq
    \Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(1)}}(p_{v,1}^{(1)})^*.
\]
For the inductive step, let
$r_n:R_{v,n+1}\to R_{v,n}$ forget the final vertex and let
$v_{n,n+1}:R_{v,n+1}\to R_{v,1}$ retain the final arrow.  The square with
$r_n$, $v_{n,n+1}$, $p_{v,n}^{(n)}$, and $p_{v,0}^{(1)}$ is Cartesian.
Right Beck--Chevalley followed by Fubini gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
J_v^{n+1}
&\simeq
\Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(n)}}
(p_{v,n}^{(n)})^*\Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(1)}}(p_{v,1}^{(1)})^*\\
&\simeq
\Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(n+1)}}
(p_{v,n+1}^{(n+1)})^*.
\end{aligned}
\]
Every comparison is a canonical mate of a Cartesian square, so mate-pasting
identifies the comonad structure with the relative Cech composition and unit
maps, including all higher simplicial coherence.  No use has been made of
the realization of the relative Cech nerve or of effectivity of $v$.

If $E=v^*\overline E$, the unit
$\overline E\to\Pi_vv^*\overline E$ induces the canonical coaction
$E\to J_vE$.  Applying the iterated correspondence just proved to
$J_v^{n+1}E$ gives the displayed cobar formula.  The coaction is the mate of
the canonical identification of the two endpoint pullbacks of
$\overline E$, while the comonad maps are the relation composition maps;
therefore the degreewise equivalences identify every coface and
codegeneracy.

For $E=\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}$, the endpoint pullback is
$a_{v,n+1}^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}$.  Applying $\Pi_p$ and Fubini gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Pi_p\operatorname{Cobar}_v^n(\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})
&\simeq
\Pi_p\Pi_{p_{v,0}^{(n+1)}}
(p_{v,n+1}^{(n+1)})^*\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}\\
&\simeq
\Pi_{a_{v,n+1}}a_{v,n+1}^*\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
=
C_{v,\mathcal O}^{n+1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The first source-changing coface and the matching source codegeneracy are
omitted, exactly as in the ordinal-sum definition of
$\operatorname{Dec}_s$; the outer counit is the omitted $s^0$.  Naturality is
again mate-pasting for the defining Cartesian squares.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Raw relation and formalized-to-raw jet comparison]
\label{prop:as-raw-formalized-jet}
There is a canonical simplicial morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}:
    \mathfrak G_\bullet
    \longrightarrow
    R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}
}
\]
and a canonical morphism of comonads in the opposite direction
\[
\boxed{
    \kappa^J_{\mathfrak G}:
    J_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    \longrightarrow
    J_{\mathfrak G}.
}
\]
Both are functorial for fixed-object groupoid morphisms and compatible with
the spectral-etale varying-object variance of the preceding chapter.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The simplicial map is induced degreewise by
$Q_{\mathfrak G}\to Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$.
For the jet comparison, Fubini gives
\[
    J_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    \simeq
    q^*\ell_{\mathfrak G}^*
    \Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}\Pi_q.
\]
Apply the counit
\[
    \ell_{\mathfrak G}^*\Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{id}
\]
to $\Pi_q$ and then pull back along $q$.  This gives
\[
    q^*\ell_{\mathfrak G}^*
    \Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}\Pi_q
    \longrightarrow
    q^*\Pi_q.
\]
It is the canonical morphism from the comonad of the composite adjunction
$q^*\ell^*\dashv\Pi_\ell\Pi_q$ to the comonad of
$q^*\dashv\Pi_q$.  The triangle identities and mate-pasting identify the
counits and comultiplications, so it is a morphism of comonads with all higher
coherence.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Variance]
The relation map and jet map necessarily have opposite directions.  Geometric
relations are covariant under postcomposition of quotient maps.  Right
correspondence functors are contravariant through the counit of the
intermediate adjunction.  This variance will also govern Cartan cobars and
co-Kleisli operators below.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Contravariant scalar restriction}

Let
\[
    a_n^{\mathrm{fm}}:
    R_{\mathfrak G,n}^{\mathrm{fm}}\longrightarrow S
\]
be the structure map and define
\[
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{fm},n}
    :=
    \Pi_{a_n^{\mathrm{fm}}}
    (a_n^{\mathrm{fm}})^*
    \mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Formalized-to-raw scalar restriction]
\label{prop:as-formalized-raw-scalar}
Pullback of scalar functions along
$\lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}$ gives a canonical morphism of cosimplicial
commutative algebras
\[
\boxed{
    r_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathcal O}:
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \longrightarrow
    C_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\bullet}.
}
\]
Stable normalization gives
\[
\boxed{
    r_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}:
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{fm,geom},\bullet}
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{geom},\bullet}.
}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Scalar functions are contravariant in the geometric relation.  The
simplicial map $\lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}$ commutes with every face and
degeneracy, so the degreewise restriction maps assemble cosimplicially.
Normalization is functorial.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Formalized-to-raw Cartan cobar comparison}

The scalar coefficient $\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}$ has canonical coalgebra
structures for both jet comonads, since it is pulled back from the additive
scalar coefficient on the respective quotient target.

\begin{proposition}[Formalized-to-raw Cartan cobar comparison]
\label{prop:as-raw-formalized-cobar}
The comonad morphism $\kappa^J_{\mathfrak G}$ and the canonical scalar
coactions induce
\[
\boxed{
    \kappa^{\mathrm{Cobar}}_{\mathfrak G}:
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}).
}
\]
After normalization,
\[
\boxed{
    \kappa^{\mathrm{CartSp}}_{\mathfrak G}:
    \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{Cart,fm},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Sp}^{\mathrm{Cart},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}).
}
\]
After applying $\Pi_p$, the source-fixed decalage equivalences identify this
map with
$\operatorname{Dec}_s(r_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathcal O})$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A morphism of comonads $K\to J$ carries a compatible $K$-coalgebra to a
$J$-coalgebra by composition of the coaction with $K\to J$ and therefore
induces a map of the corresponding Cartan cobars.  For the scalar coefficient,
the two coactions are obtained from the same quotient-factorization diagram,
and the counit defining $\kappa^J$ identifies their mates.  The last statement follows from
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-arbitrary-map-decalage}, applied to both $q$ and
$q^{\mathrm{fm}}$.  Its naturality identifies the two source-fixed decalage
maps with the right-correspondence mates of the same simplicial relation
map; no effectivity of $q^{\mathrm{fm}}$ is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Stable scalar formalization discrepancy]
\label{def:as-spencer-formalization-discrepancy}
Define the Cartan scalar discrepancy by
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb C_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{CartSp,Art}}
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(
        \kappa^{\mathrm{CartSp}}_{\mathfrak G}
    \right).
}
\]
Define the geometric scalar discrepancy by
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb C_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{geomSp,Art}}
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(
        r_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}
    \right).
}
\]
After applying $\Pi_p$ and source-fixed decalage, the two discrepancy
sequences are canonically equivalent.  Neither discrepancy is identified
with an algebraic partition-Lie discrepancy before Artinian cotangent-primitive
linearization.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Represented quotient collapse]
\label{prop:as-raw-formalized-spencer-local}
If $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ is represented, then
\[
    \lambda^R_{\mathfrak G},
    \qquad
    \kappa^J_{\mathfrak G},
    \qquad
    r_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}},
    \qquad
    \kappa^{\mathrm{CartSp}}_{\mathfrak G}
\]
are equivalences and both stable scalar discrepancies vanish.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:bnla-represented-quotient-artdef-local} gives
\[
    Q_{\mathfrak G}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
\]
All statements follow by finite limits, dependent products, the adjunction
counit, normalization, and source-fixed decalage.
\end{proof}

\section{The universal Artinian-formalized Spencer and adjoint representations}
\label{sec:as-universal-spencer-representation}

Fix a coherent affine-etale pair $(U,V)$ with coordinate map $R\to B$, and
write
\[
    F
    :=
    \mathcal L_{\mathfrak G,U/V}^{\mathrm{fm,sp}}
    \in
    \operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R}.
\]
For an Artinian point
\[
    \widetilde q:
    T=\operatorname{Spec}A\longrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
centred at $U=\operatorname{Spec}B$, let
\[
    i:U\longrightarrow T,
    \qquad
    u:U\longrightarrow X
\]
be the Artinian thickening and centre map, together with the specified
homotopy
\[
    q^{\mathrm{fm}}u\simeq\widetilde q\,i.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Artinian Cech test map into the formalized relation]
\label{prop:as-artinian-cech-test-map}
For every $n\geq0$ there is a canonical morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \beta_{\widetilde q,n}:
    \underbrace{U\times_T\cdots\times_TU}_{n+1}
    \longrightarrow
    \underbrace{
    X\times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}\cdots
    \times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}X
    }_{n+1}
    =R_{\mathfrak G,n}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
}
\]
These maps assemble to a simplicial morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \beta_{\widetilde q}:
    \check C_\bullet(U/T)
    \longrightarrow
    R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
}
\]
On coordinate algebras the source Cech nerve is
\[
    [n]\longmapsto
    \underbrace{B\otimes_A\cdots\otimes_A B}_{n+1}.
\]
Scalar functions and stable scalar sections restrict in the opposite
direction along $\beta_{\widetilde q}$.  No equality or base-change
identification between the Artinian Cech nerve and the formalized relation is
asserted.

If the Artinian point admits a lift
$T\to Q_{\mathfrak G}$ compatible with the centre, then
$\beta_{\widetilde q}$ factors canonically through the raw relation
$\mathfrak G_\bullet$.  Without such a lift no raw Artinian factorization is
postulated.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let
\[
    C_n:=U\times_T\cdots\times_TU
\]
and let $p_j:C_n\to U$ be the vertex projections.  The maps
$up_j:C_n\to X$ have canonically identified composites to
$Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$, because
\[
    q^{\mathrm{fm}}up_j
    \simeq
    \widetilde q\,ip_j
\]
and all composites $ip_j:C_n\to T$ are identified by the defining homotopies
of the iterated fibre product.  The universal property of
$X^{\times_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}(n+1)}$ therefore gives
$\beta_{\widetilde q,n}$.

Deletion and repetition of vertices commute with the projections $p_j$, so
the degreewise maps satisfy every simplicial identity.  The affine coordinate
formula is the usual tensor-product description of the Cech nerve of
$\operatorname{Spec}B\to\operatorname{Spec}A$.  Contravariance of scalar
functions gives the scalar restriction.  A lift to $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ gives the
same construction with $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ in place of its formalization, and
postcomposition with
$Q_{\mathfrak G}\to Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$ recovers
$\beta_{\widetilde q}$.
\end{proof}

Applying additive scalar sections to the represented Artinian Cech test gives
a cosimplicial spectrum.  We construct the corresponding object over the
Artinian leaf before normalizing it.  For an Artinian arrow $A\to B$ put
\[
    C_A^n
    :=
    \underbrace{
        \operatorname{Spec}B
        \times_{\operatorname{Spec}A}\cdots
        \times_{\operatorname{Spec}A}
        \operatorname{Spec}B
    }_{n+1}
    =
    \operatorname{Spec}P_A^{[n]},
\]
where
\[
    P_A^{[n]}
    :=
    \underbrace{B\otimes_A\cdots\otimes_A B}_{n+1}.
\]
Let $a_{A,n}:C_A^n\to V=\operatorname{Spec}R$ be the structure map.  For every
$k\in\mathbb Z$ define
\[
    \mathscr C^n_{F,A,k}:F(A)\longrightarrow\mathcal S,
    \qquad
    x\longmapsto
    \Omega^\infty\Sigma^k
    \Gamma_{C_A^n}^{\mathrm{st}}
    \left(
        a_{A,n}^*\mathcal O_V^{\mathrm{add}}
    \right).
\]
Unstraightening gives an object over $F(A)$.  As $k$ varies, the fibrewise
spectrum structure makes these objects a spectrum object in the slice over
$F(A)$.  As $A\to B$ varies, Artinian pullback and stable-section
Beck--Chevalley give a presheaf over $F$, and as $n$ varies the Cech maps give
a cosimplicial object
\[
\boxed{
    \mathscr C_F^\bullet
    \in
    \operatorname{Fun}
    \left(
        \Delta,
        \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal P_F)
    \right).
}
\]
On every represented affine test, the scalar-section formula identifies the
fibre in Cech degree $n$ with the underlying spectrum of $P_A^{[n]}$.
The adjective ``Artinian'' below refers to this test bar; it does not mean
that the formalized relation itself is represented by the tensor-product
algebras in every degree.

\begin{definition}[Artinian test scalar bar cochains]
\label{def:as-raw-artinian-bar-coefficient}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal S_F^{\mathrm{bar},\bullet}
    :=
    N^\bullet\mathscr C_F^\bullet
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Sp}
        \left(
            \operatorname{Fun}
            (\operatorname{Art}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{R/B},\mathcal S)_{/F}
        \right)
    \right).
}
\]
For a fixed Artinian arrow $A\to B$, write
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{Sp}_{A}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \left(
        [n]\longmapsto P_A^{[n]}
    \right)
}
\]
for its represented Artinian test-bar model, with each $P_A^{[n]}$ regarded
as its underlying spectrum.  This is the fibrewise model used below before
degreewise stabilized Schlessinger localization; no prorepresenting algebra
for $F$ is chosen or retained.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Formal-leaf scalar Spencer representation cochains]
\label{def:as-formal-leaf-spencer-representation}
Apply the stabilized Schlessinger reflector degreewise and define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{Sp}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    :=
    L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}
    \left(
        \mathcal S_F^{\mathrm{bar},\bullet}
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)
    \right).
}
\]
Here and below an exact functor between stable categories is applied
degreewise to a cochain object unless totalization is explicitly written.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Construction and etale compatibility]
\label{prop:as-linear-spencer-reflector}
The cochain object
$\mathbf{Sp}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}$ is functorial in morphisms of formal
leaves.  Under coherent spectral-etale change of centre and base, the
two-variable Artinian transport carries it to the corresponding Spencer
representation cochain object.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The affine Cech bar commutes with Artinian base change.  The transported
Schlessinger generators are the corresponding Schlessinger generators by the
preceding chapter, so
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-stable-schlessinger-exists} gives the exchange
equivalence for $L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}$.  Exactness makes the exchange
degreewise and compatible with the normalized differential.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Universal formalized scalar Spencer representation]
\label{thm:as-universal-spencer-representation}
Tangent differentiation, applied degreewise, gives
\[
\boxed{
    D_F^{\mathrm{lin}}
    \left(
        \mathbf{Sp}_F^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}
        \left(
            \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}|_{U/V}
        \right)
    \right).
}
\]
Equivalently, the controller acts coherently on every cochain degree and the
normalized differential is equivariant.  These affine cochain
representations are etale cocartesian and glue to a global cochain
representation
\[
\boxed{
    \rho_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}:
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \curvearrowright
    \mathbf{Sp}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}.
}
\]
No representation is supplied as input structure on the algebroid.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Definition~\ref{def:as-formal-leaf-spencer-representation} places every
cochain degree in $\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)$.  Apply
