Theorem~\ref{thm:as-tangent-differentiation} degreewise.  Etale compatibility
follows from Proposition~\ref{prop:as-linear-spencer-reflector} and the
two-variable naturality of tangent differentiation.
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-representation-etale-descent} then glues the affine
representations, and exactness preserves the normalized differential.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The formalized adjoint coefficient}

The scalar Spencer coefficient and actual isotropy are different objects.
There is an additional raw/formalized distinction on the adjoint side.  The
formal-groupoid chapter constructed the descended formal adjoint group
\[
\boxed{
    K_{\mathfrak G}
    :=
    \operatorname{Ad}^{\mathrm{for}}(\mathfrak G)
    \longrightarrow
    Q_{\mathfrak G}
}
\]
and a canonical equivalence of group objects
\[
    q_{\mathfrak G}^*K_{\mathfrak G}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}.
\]
Under this equivalence the Cech descent transport of $K_{\mathfrak G}$ is
nonlinear conjugation.

\begin{construction}[Formalized adjoint group]
\label{con:as-formalized-adjoint-group}
Let
\[
    \ell=\ell_{\mathfrak G}:
    Q_{\mathfrak G}\longrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
\]
Dependent product preserves finite limits.  It therefore preserves internal
group objects, and we define
\[
\boxed{
    K_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    :=
    \Pi_\ell K_{\mathfrak G}
    \longrightarrow
    Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
}
\]
Pulling back along $q^{\mathrm{fm}}$ gives the formalized adjoint group on
$X$,
\[
\boxed{
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm}}
    :=
    (q^{\mathrm{fm}})^*
    K_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
}
\]
Using $q^{\mathrm{fm}}=\ell q$ and the counit
$\ell^*\Pi_\ell\to\operatorname{id}$ gives a canonical homomorphism
\[
\boxed{
    \chi_{\mathfrak G}^{I}:
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm}}
    \longrightarrow
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}.
}
\]
All three constructions are functorial for fixed-object groupoid morphisms
and for the spectral-etale varying-object variance of the global coefficient
system.
\end{construction}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion follows because $\Pi_\ell$ is a right adjoint and internal
groups are defined by finite-limit diagrams.  For the comparison,
\[
\begin{aligned}
I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm}}
&=
(q^{\mathrm{fm}})^*\Pi_\ell K_{\mathfrak G}\\
&\simeq
q^*\ell^*\Pi_\ell K_{\mathfrak G}\\
&\longrightarrow
q^*K_{\mathfrak G}\\
&\simeq
I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The arrow is the adjunction counit.  Naturality and all higher coherence are
the corresponding mate-pasting coherences.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Formalized stable adjoint coefficient]
\label{def:as-formalized-stable-adjoint}
Let $e^{\mathrm{fm}}:Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}\to
K_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$ be the identity section.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \overline{\mathbb I}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm,st}}
    :=
    \widetilde\Sigma^\infty_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}
    \left(
        K_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}},e^{\mathrm{fm}}
    \right)
    \in
    \mathcal E_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}.
}
\]
Its pullback to $X$ is denoted
$\mathbb I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm,st}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Formalized-to-actual stable adjoint comparison]
\label{prop:as-formalized-actual-adjoint-stable}
The counit comparison
$\chi_{\mathfrak G}^{I}$ induces a canonical morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \chi_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}}:
    \mathbb I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm,st}}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}}.
}
\]
If $\ell_{\mathfrak G}$ is an equivalence, then
$\chi_{\mathfrak G}^{I}$ and
$\chi_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}}$ are equivalences.  In particular this
holds whenever $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ is represented.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Reduced stabilization is functorial in pointed objects and exact pullback.
If $\ell$ is an equivalence, its adjunction counit is an equivalence.  The
represented case follows from
Proposition~\ref{prop:bnla-represented-quotient-artdef-local}.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Artinian linearization of the formalized adjoint coefficient]
\label{con:as-formalized-adjoint-linearization}
On the affine formal leaf $F$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{Ad}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    :=
    L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}
    \operatorname{Sec}^{\mathrm{Art}}_F
    \left(
        \overline{\mathbb I}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm,st}}
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F).
}
\]
This construction uses the formalized quotient itself, so every Artinian
point of $F$ is a legitimate test.  No lift of that point to the raw quotient
is chosen or required.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Formalized differentiated adjoint representation]
\label{thm:as-differentiated-adjoint-representation}
Tangent differentiation gives a canonical affine representation
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Ad}^{\pi,\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G,U/V}:
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}|_{U/V}
    \curvearrowright
    D_F^{\mathrm{lin}}
    \left(
        \mathbf{Ad}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    \right).
}
\]
The affine representations are spectral-etale cocartesian and therefore glue
to a global representation
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Ad}^{\pi,\mathrm{fm}}_{\mathfrak G}:
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \curvearrowright
    \mathbf{Ad}_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,\mathrm{fm}}.
}
\]
If quotient formalization is an equivalence, this representation is the
Artinian differentiation of the actual descended nonlinear conjugation
coefficient.  No unconditional identification of its coefficient with the
independently constructed actual-isotropy controller
$\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$ is asserted.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The object
$\overline{\mathbb I}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm,st}}$ is a stable
coefficient on $Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$.  Construction~\ref{con:as-stable-artinian-sections} gives its spectrum-valued Artinian
section object over $F$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-stable-schlessinger-exists} reflects it into
$\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)$.  Apply tangent differentiation.
The two-variable spectral-etale compatibility of stable Artinian sections,
Schlessinger reflection, and tangent differentiation gives the cocartesian
transition equivalences, so
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-representation-etale-descent} glues the result.

If $\ell$ is an equivalence, then
$K_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}\simeq K_{\mathfrak G}$ and the coefficient
comparison of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-formalized-actual-adjoint-stable} is an equivalence.
The final non-identification records that the actual-isotropy controller was
constructed independently by differentiating
$B_\bullet I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}$; no comparison theorem between
that partition-Lie controller and the present stabilized Cartan coefficient
has been used.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three adjoint objects]
The nonlinear group
$I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}$, the formalized stable adjoint
representation coefficient
$\mathbf{Ad}_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,\mathrm{fm}}$, and the spectral
actual-isotropy controller
$\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$ are distinct constructions.
The first maps from its formalized adjoint approximation by
$\chi_{\mathfrak G}^{I}$; the second is obtained by stable Artinian
linearization over the formalized quotient; the third is obtained by
spectral formal-moduli differentiation of the one-object formal-isotropy
groupoid.  Equality of the latter two is not an axiom or a package datum.
\end{remark}

\section{The tangent enveloping monad}
\label{sec:as-enveloping-monad}

Fix a coherent affine coefficient map $R\to B$.  Put
\[
    \mathcal C_B:=\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B,
    \qquad
    \mathcal A:=\operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R},
\]
and let
\[
    \operatorname{Free}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    \dashv U
\]
be the free--forgetful adjunction over
$\operatorname{Anch}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{B/R}$.

\begin{lemma}[Stabilization of a sliced monadic adjunction]
\label{lem:as-stabilized-monadicity}
Let
\[
    F:\mathcal C\rightleftarrows\mathcal D:U
\]
be a monadic adjunction between presentable $\infty$-categories whose
associated monad preserves sifted colimits.  Fix $d\in\mathcal D$.  Assume
that finite limits commute with sifted colimits in the source slice
$\mathcal C_{/Ud}$.  Then the sliced and stabilized adjunction
\[
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal C_{/Ud})
    \rightleftarrows
    \operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal D_{/d})
\]
is monadic.  Its right adjoint is conservative, preserves sifted colimits,
and creates split geometric realizations.  Consequently the induced tangent
monad preserves sifted colimits.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Monadicity of $U$ implies that the sliced right adjoint is conservative and
creates limits.  Because the original monad preserves sifted colimits, $U$
also creates sifted colimits, and the same is true after slicing.

A spectrum object in either slice is a pointed sequence satisfying loop
equivalences defined by finite limits.  By the hypothesis on
$\mathcal C_{/Ud}$, sifted colimits of spectrum objects in the source slice
are computed levelwise and again satisfy the loop equivalences.  The sliced
right adjoint creates the corresponding sifted colimits in the target slice;
conservativity then detects the loop equivalences there.  Thus the stabilized
right adjoint preserves sifted colimits.

Now let a simplicial spectrum object become split after the stabilized right
adjoint.  Evaluation in each spectrum degree gives a $U$-split simplicial
object.  The original monadic right adjoint creates its geometric
realization.  A split geometric realization is absolute, and hence commutes
with the finite limits defining the spectrum structure.  Therefore the
levelwise realization is again a spectrum object and is created by the
stabilized right adjoint.  Barr--Beck--Lurie applies.  Finally the tangent
monad is the composite of the stabilized left adjoint with this
sifted-colimit-preserving right adjoint.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Sliced free--forgetful tangent adjunction]
\label{con:as-sliced-free-forgetful}
For $\mathfrak a\in\mathcal A$, write
\[
    A:=U\mathfrak a\longrightarrow
    \mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1]
\]
for the underlying anchor.  Slicing the free--forgetful adjunction over
$\mathfrak a$ gives a source slice
\[
    \left(
        \operatorname{Anch}_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}
    \right)_{/A}
    \simeq
    (\mathcal C_B)_{/A}.
\]
Since $\mathcal C_B$ is stable,
\[
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \left((\mathcal C_B)_{/A}\right)
    \simeq
    \mathcal C_B
\]
by the fibre-at-$A$ equivalence.  Stabilization therefore gives
\[
\boxed{
    F_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{lin}}:
    \mathcal C_B
    \rightleftarrows
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}(\mathfrak a):
    U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{lin}}.
}
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Tangent monadicity]
\label{thm:as-tangent-monadicity}
The right adjoint $U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{lin}}$ is monadic.  Therefore
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
    :=
    U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{lin}}
    F_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{lin}}
}
\]
is an accessible sifted-colimit-preserving monad on
$\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$, and
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}(\mathfrak a)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B
    \right).
}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The spectral partition-Lie forgetful functor is monadic and its monad
preserves sifted colimits by the preceding chapter.  Its source slice at $A$
is $(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)_{/A}$.  This slice is not asserted to be
stable.  Instead, both finite limits and sifted colimits in the slice are
created in the stable presentable category $\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$.
Finite limits in the latter are finite colimits, and finite colimits commute
with sifted colimits.  Hence finite limits commute with sifted colimits in the
slice, which is exactly the hypothesis of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-stabilized-monadicity}.  The fibre-at-$A$ equivalence
\[
    \operatorname{Sp}\bigl((\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)_{/A}\bigr)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B
\]
was constructed above.  Apply the lemma and
Construction~\ref{con:as-sliced-free-forgetful}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Enveloping monad rather than an imposed enveloping object]
The integral enveloping construction is the monad
$\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}$.  No assertion that every colimit-preserving
endofunctor of $\operatorname{QC}_B^\vee$ is tensoring with its value on the
unit is used.  An ordinary associative enveloping algebra appears only after
rational dg rectification.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Etale descent of the tangent monad}

The preceding chapter constructs the pro-coherent coefficient category and
the partition-Lie algebroid category as coherent spectral-etale
global-section categories.  The tangent monad inherits the same descent.

\begin{definition}[Global pro-coherent coefficient category]
\label{def:as-global-procoherent-coefficients}
On the locally coherent qcqs globalization domain define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_{X/S}
    :=
    \lim_{(U,V)\in
    \operatorname{AffEt}^{\mathrm{coh}}(X/S)^{\mathrm{op}}}
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_{B},
}
\]
where $(U,V)=(\operatorname{Spec}B,\operatorname{Spec}R)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Global tangent enveloping monad]
\label{thm:as-global-tangent-monad}
Let
\[
    \mathfrak a
    \in
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{X/S}.
\]
The affine tangent monads
\[
    \mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a|_{U/V}}
\]
and their units and multiplications are compatible with coherent
spectral-etale transport.  Consequently there is a canonically descended
sifted-colimit-preserving monad
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a}:
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_{X/S}
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_{X/S}
}
\]
characterized by
\[
    \left(
        \mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a}M
    \right)|_{U/V}
    \simeq
    \mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a|_{U/V}}
    \left(M|_{U/V}\right).
\]
Moreover
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}(\mathfrak a)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_{X/S}
    \right).
}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The relative partition-Lie free--forgetful adjunction is natural under the
two-variable spectral-etale transport of the preceding chapter.  Slicing at
the cocartesian section $\mathfrak a$ and stabilizing preserves these
naturality squares, so the affine adjunctions
\[
F_{\mathfrak a|_{U/V}}^{\mathrm{lin}}
\dashv
U_{\mathfrak a|_{U/V}}^{\mathrm{lin}}
\]
satisfy coherent Beck--Chevalley equivalences.  Their composites therefore
give a cocartesian family of monads.  Taking global sections defines the
displayed endofunctor, and the affine units and multiplications glue because
the Beck--Chevalley equivalences are compatible with identity, composition,
and higher pasting.

Finally, Eilenberg--Moore categories of algebras for a coherently varying
monad are defined by limits of the usual action diagrams.  Hence
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a}}
(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_{X/S})
&\simeq
\lim_{(U,V)}
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb U^\pi_{\mathfrak a|_{U/V}}}
(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)\\
&\simeq
\lim_{(U,V)}
\operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}
(\mathfrak a|_{U/V})\\
&=
\operatorname{Rep}^{\pi,E_\infty}(\mathfrak a),
\end{aligned}
\]
where the second equivalence is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-tangent-monadicity}.
\end{proof}

\section{Affine Cech bars and the bar--cotangent theorem}
\label{sec:as-bar-cotangent}

Fix a coherent connective map $R\to B$ and a complete almost-finite
augmentation
\[
    A\longrightarrow B.
\]
Put
\[
\boxed{
    P_{A/B}:=B\otimes_A B
}
\]
with multiplication
\[
    \mu:P_{A/B}\longrightarrow B.
\]

\begin{construction}[Cech bar coalgebra]
\label{con:as-cech-bar-coalgebra}
The affine Cech nerve has coordinate algebras
\[
    P_{A/B}^{(n)}
    :=
    \underbrace{
        B\otimes_A\cdots\otimes_A B
    }_{n+1}.
\]
Insertion of a middle vertex gives the usual correspondence comultiplication
\[
    \Delta_A:
    P_{A/B}
    \longrightarrow
    P_{A/B}\otimes_BP_{A/B}
    \simeq
    B\otimes_A B\otimes_A B,
\]
and the unit degeneracy gives $P_{A/B}\to B$.  All higher coassociativity is
the simplicial coherence of the complete Cech nerve.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Cotangent coefficient of the Cech diagonal]
\label{lem:as-cech-diagonal-cotangent}
There is a canonical natural equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \vartheta_A:
    L_{B/P_{A/B}}[-2]
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    L_{B/A}[-1].
}
\]
It is natural in augmented arrows and in coherent spectral base change.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Cotangent base change gives
\[
    B\otimes_{P_{A/B}}L_{P_{A/B}/A}
    \simeq
    L_{B/A}\oplus L_{B/A}.
\]
In the transitivity triangle for
\[
    A\longrightarrow P_{A/B}\xrightarrow{\mu}B,
\]
the first morphism after base change is the fold
\[
    L_{B/A}\oplus L_{B/A}
    \xrightarrow{(1,1)}
    L_{B/A}.
\]
The sequence
\[
    L_{B/A}
    \xrightarrow{(1,-1)}
    L_{B/A}\oplus L_{B/A}
    \xrightarrow{(1,1)}
    L_{B/A}
\]
is a fibre sequence in the stable $B$-module category.  Hence
\[
    L_{B/P_{A/B}}
    \simeq
    L_{B/A}[1].
\]
Shift by $[-2]$.  Every map used is natural in $A\to B$, and cotangent base
change carries the fold/anti-diagonal sequence to the corresponding sequence
after coherent base change.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The diagonal augmentation is complete almost-finite]
\label{lem:as-diagonal-complete-aft}
If $A\to B$ is spectral Artinian, then
\[
    P_{A/B}\longrightarrow B
\]
lies in
$\operatorname{CAlg}^{\mathrm{sp},\wedge\mathrm{aft}}_{B/B}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Because $\pi_0(A)\twoheadrightarrow\pi_0(B)$,
\[
    \pi_0(B\otimes_A B)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \pi_0(B),
\]
so the degree-zero augmentation ideal is zero and derived completeness is
automatic.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-diagonal-cotangent},
\[
    L_{B/P_{A/B}}
    \simeq
    L_{B/A}[1],
\]
and the spectral Artinian conditions make the latter almost perfect.  The
connective surjective spectral finiteness criterion and the complete
almost-finite criterion of the preceding chapter therefore apply.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Normalized Cech primitive derivation]
\label{def:as-cech-primitive-derivation}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(A)
    :=
    \left(
        L_{B/R}[-1]
        \longrightarrow
        L_{B/P_{A/B}}[-2]
    \right),
}
\]
where the anchor is cotangent transitivity
\[
    L_{B/R}[-1]\longrightarrow L_{B/A}[-1]
\]
transported through $\vartheta_A^{-1}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Bar--cotangent equivalence]
\label{thm:as-bar-cotangent}
There is a canonical natural equivalence of relative derivations
\[
\boxed{
    \eta_A^{\mathrm{bar}}:
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(A)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{cot}_{B/R}(A)
    =
    \left(
        L_{B/R}[-1]\to L_{B/A}[-1]
    \right).
}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The underlying equivalence is $\vartheta_A$ from
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-diagonal-cotangent}.  Compatibility with the anchor is
part of Definition~\ref{def:as-cech-primitive-derivation}.  Naturality is the
naturality of $\vartheta_A$ and cotangent transitivity.
\end{proof}

Let
\[
    L:=\operatorname{cot}_{B/R},
    \qquad
    R_0:=\operatorname{sqz}_{B/R},
    \qquad
    G:=LR_0
\]
on connective almost-perfect relative derivations.  Thus
\[
    G=\operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}.
\]
Write
\[
    \eta:\operatorname{id}\longrightarrow R_0L,
    \qquad
    \epsilon:LR_0\longrightarrow\operatorname{id}
\]
for the unit and counit of $L\dashv R_0$.

\begin{definition}[Cech primitive endofunctor]
\label{def:as-cech-primitive-endofunctor}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}
    :=
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}R_0.
}
\]
The bar--cotangent equivalence gives a natural equivalence of endofunctors
\[
\boxed{
    \theta:
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    G.
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Transported Cech primitive comonad]
\label{con:as-transported-cech-comonad}
Transport the comonad structure of $G$ through $\theta$.  Thus the counit is
\[
\boxed{
    \epsilon_D^{\check C}:
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}(D)
    \xrightarrow{\theta_D}
    G(D)
    \xrightarrow{\epsilon_D}
    D,
}
\]
and the comultiplication is the unique morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \delta_D^{\check C}:
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}(D)
    \longrightarrow
    \left(G^{\check C}_{B/R}\right)^2(D)
}
\]
whose image under the iterated natural equivalence
\[
    \theta_D^{(2)}:
    \left(G^{\check C}_{B/R}\right)^2(D)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    G^2(D)
\]
is
\[
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}(D)
    \xrightarrow{\theta_D}
    G(D)
    \xrightarrow{\delta_D}
    G^2(D).
\]
Then $\theta$ is, by construction, an equivalence of comonads.  In
particular the Cech primitive counit is the transported adjunction counit; no
Cech degeneracy is used to manufacture a map to the input derivation.
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Cech-unit primitive cooperation]
\label{con:as-cech-unit-cooperation}
For every complete almost-finite arrow $A\to B$, the adjunction unit gives
\[
    u_A:A\longrightarrow R_0L(A)
\]
over $B$.  On coordinate algebras it induces a cosimplicial Cech-bar map
\[
    B^{\otimes_A(n+1)}
    \longrightarrow
    B^{\otimes_{R_0L(A)}(n+1)}
\]
in every degree $n$.  The associated geometric Cech-nerve morphism has the
opposite direction.  Applying the normalized primitive construction in degree
one gives
\[
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(A)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(R_0L(A)).
\]
For $A=R_0D$ this gives a natural transformation
\[
\boxed{
    \Delta^{\mathrm{Sp}}:
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}
    \longrightarrow
    \left(G^{\check C}_{B/R}\right)^2.
}
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Cech-unit mate comparison]
\label{lem:as-cech-unit-mate}
Under the bar--cotangent equivalence, the square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
\operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(A)
  \arrow[r]
  \arrow[d,"\eta_A^{\mathrm{bar}}"'] &
\operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(R_0L(A))
  \arrow[d,"\eta_{R_0L(A)}^{\mathrm{bar}}"]\\
L(A)
  \arrow[r,"L(\eta_A)"'] &
LR_0L(A)
\end{tikzcd}
\]
commutes canonically.  In particular, for $A=R_0D$, the upper map is exactly
the transported comonad comultiplication $\delta_D^{\check C}$ of
Construction~\ref{con:as-transported-cech-comonad}.  The transported counit
$\epsilon_D^{\check C}$ corresponds under $\theta_D$ to the genuine
adjunction counit $\epsilon_D$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The upper map is induced functorially from the augmented-arrow morphism
$u_A:A\to R_0L(A)$.  The bar--cotangent equivalence is natural in augmented
arrows, so the square commutes.  When $A=R_0D$, the lower map is
\[
    LR_0D
    \xrightarrow{L\eta_{R_0D}}
    LR_0LR_0D,
\]
which is the comultiplication of the comonad induced by $L\dashv R_0$.
Transport through $\theta$ therefore identifies the Cech-unit map with
$\delta_D^{\check C}$.  The counit statement is immediate from the definition
\[
    \epsilon_D^{\check C}=\epsilon_D\theta_D.
\]
All homotopies are the canonical naturality homotopies of the adjunction unit,
counit, and the natural equivalence $\eta^{\mathrm{bar}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Artinian Spencer coLie comonad]
\label{def:as-spencer-colie}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{coSp}^{\mathrm{Art}}_{B/R}
    :=
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}
}
\]
with the transported counit and comultiplication of
Construction~\ref{con:as-transported-cech-comonad}.  Equivalently, for a
connective almost-perfect relative derivation
\[
    \alpha:L_{B/R}[-1]\longrightarrow M,
\]
\[
    \operatorname{coSp}^{\mathrm{Art}}_{B/R}(M,\alpha)
    =
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(B_\alpha).
\]
By Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-unit-mate}, its comultiplication is the
Cech-unit primitive cooperation of
Construction~\ref{con:as-cech-unit-cooperation}.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Spencer coLie equals cotangent--square-zero coLie]
\label{thm:as-spencer-colie-equals-colie}
The natural equivalences
$\eta_{B_\alpha}^{\mathrm{bar}}$ assemble to an equivalence of comonads
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{coSp}^{\mathrm{Art}}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}.
}
\]
The equivalence is natural under coherent spectral-etale change of centre and
base.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-bar-cotangent} identifies the underlying endofunctors.
Construction~\ref{con:as-transported-cech-comonad} defines the source comonad
precisely by transport through that natural equivalence, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-unit-mate} identifies the transported comultiplication
with the geometric Cech-unit primitive cooperation.  Hence the displayed
natural equivalence preserves counit, comultiplication, and all higher
comonad coherence.  Cotangent base change, structured square-zero base change,
and naturality of the adjunction unit and counit give the spectral-etale
statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Square-zero Cech primitive is cofree]
\label{cor:as-square-zero-bar-colie}
For $B_\alpha\to B$,
\[
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(B_\alpha)
\]
is the cofree
$\operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}$-coalgebra generated by
$(M,\alpha)$.  Its continuous dual is the free spectral partition-Lie
algebroid on
\[
    M^\vee
    \xrightarrow{\alpha^\vee}
    \mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1].
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The object $B_\alpha$ lies in the image of the right adjoint $R_0$.  Under a
comonadic adjunction, applying the left adjoint to an object in the image of
the right adjoint gives the cofree coalgebra for the induced comonad.  Apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-spencer-colie-equals-colie} and then continuous duality.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Two Cech structures]
The Cech-unit cooperation used to identify the comonad should not be
confused with the ordinary correspondence comultiplication
$P_{A/B}\to P_{A/B}\otimes_BP_{A/B}$.  The former iterates the universal
square-zero approximation $A\to R_0L(A)$ and controls the coLie comonad.  The
latter composes relation arrows and controls Cech descent and principal-parts
convolution.  The higher arity/substitution calculus is extracted separately
from the multi-filtered cotangent--square-zero comonad and its exact
multiweights.  The bar--cotangent theorem relates the diagonal coefficient to
the same cotangent primitive, but these structures play different categorical
roles.
\end{remark}

\section{The Artinian Spencer partition-Lie monad}
\label{sec:as-higher-operations}

\begin{definition}[Spencer partition-Lie monad]
\label{def:as-spencer-partition-monad}
On dually almost-perfect anchored pro-coherent objects, continuously dualize
the opposite of
$\operatorname{coSp}^{\mathrm{Art}}_{B/R}$.  Extend the resulting monad by
the same pro-coherent sifted-completion theorem used for
$\operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}$.  Denote it by
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Lie}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}_{B/R}.
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Integral monad comparison]
\label{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison}
There is a canonical equivalence of sifted-colimit-preserving monads
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Lie}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}.
}
\]
It is natural under coherent spectral-etale change of centre and base.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On the dense dually almost-perfect subcategory this is continuous duality
applied to
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-spencer-colie-equals-colie}.  Both monads are the unique
sifted-colimit-preserving extensions satisfying the pro-coherent completion
relations of the preceding chapter.  Hence the equivalence extends uniquely,
including unit and multiplication.  Etale naturality follows from cotangent
base change, Cech base change, and finite-Tor pro-coherent
Beck--Chevalley.
\end{proof}


\section{Raw marked Cech geometry and Artinian higher operations}
\label{sec:as-marked-operations}

The geometric and Artinian operation systems live in different coefficient
categories and are constructed separately.  The canonical Artinian Cech test
map of Proposition~\ref{prop:as-artinian-cech-test-map} compares formalized
support-marked geometry with its support scalar Artinian bar; it does not
identify that support cube with the directional square-zero cube which
computes the multi-derivative.  The raw geometric layer has two useful forms.  The first is the exhaustive singleton-marked sector, which
isolates the traditional marked-word cells.  The second is its canonical
support-marked simplicial closure.  To make the latter genuinely simplicial we
stabilize relative to the fully degenerate copy of $X$ over the common base
$S$, rather than in the fixed slice over the source vertex.  The full PD
substitution system is constructed only after passage to the independently
constructed Artinian cotangent primitive.

\subsection{Relative reduced stabilization and exhaustive singleton-marked cells}

Let $K_\bullet\rightrightarrows X$ be either $\mathfrak G_\bullet$ or
$R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}$.  Write
\[
    u_m:X\longrightarrow K_m
\]
for the fully degenerate simplex.

\begin{definition}[Relative reduced stabilization over the common base]
\label{def:as-relative-reduced-stabilization}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \widetilde{\mathbb S}_{S;X}[K_m]
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(
        \Sigma_{+,S}^{\infty}X
        \xrightarrow{\Sigma_{+,S}^{\infty}u_m}
        \Sigma_{+,S}^{\infty}K_m
    \right)
    \in\mathcal E_S.
}
\]
Every simplicial operator $K_m\to K_n$ carries $u_m$ to $u_n$ and therefore
induces a canonical map
\[
    \widetilde{\mathbb S}_{S;X}[K_m]
    \longrightarrow
    \widetilde{\mathbb S}_{S;X}[K_n].
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Two reduced stabilizations]
The relative object
$\widetilde{\mathbb S}_{S;X}[K_m]\in\mathcal E_S$ is used only for the
face-closed marked Cech geometry.  The fibrewise reduced stabilization
$\widetilde\Sigma_X^\infty$ of pointed objects over $X$ remains the intrinsic
construction used for stable arrows, isotropy, pointed smash products, and the
nonlinear commutator.  No identification of these two constructions is made.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Nondegenerate exhaustive marked words]
\label{def:as-surjection-category}
For $r\geq1$ and $d\geq0$, let $\mathsf{Word}_r(d)$ be the finite set of words
\[
    u=(u_1,\ldots,u_{r+d}),
    \qquad
    u_j\in\{1,\ldots,r\},
\]
such that every label occurs and $u_j\neq u_{j+1}$ for every $j$.  The group
$\Sigma_r$ acts by relabelling.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Marked unit-collapse cube]
\label{con:as-marked-degeneracy-cube}
Fix
\[
    u=(u_1,\ldots,u_m)\in\mathsf{Word}_r(m-r).
\]
For $J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,r\}$ collapse every edge whose label does not lie in
$J$.  This gives a canonical order-preserving quotient
\[
    c_{u,J}:[m]\twoheadrightarrow[m_{u,J}].
\]
For $J\subseteq J'$ the quotient maps factor canonically, so contravariance of
the simplicial relation gives
\[
    K_{m_{u,J}}\longrightarrow K_{m_{u,J'}}.
\]
After relative reduced stabilization these maps form an $r$-cube in
$\mathcal E_S$,
\[
    D_{u,K}(J)
    :=
    \widetilde{\mathbb S}_{S;X}[K_{m_{u,J}}].
\]
Define
\[
\boxed{
    N_u(K):=\operatorname{tcofib}D_{u,K}.}
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{definition}[Separated marked-cell object]
\label{def:as-marked-bar-arity}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb B_K^{\mathrm{sep}}(r)
    :=
    \bigoplus_{d\geq0}
    \bigoplus_{u\in\mathsf{Word}_r(d)}N_u(K)[-d].}
\]
It carries the canonical $\Sigma_r$-action.  It is a graded geometric
collection.  No differential or substitution law is attached to it by
definition.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Functoriality of separated marked cells]
\label{prop:as-marked-bar-substitution}
The construction $K_\bullet\mapsto\mathbb B_K^{\mathrm{sep}}(r)$ is functorial
in simplicial maps of relations preserving the fully degenerate copy of $X$
and equivariant for $\Sigma_r$.  A Cech operator induces a map between
separated cells whenever its transported marking remains a nondegenerate
singleton marking.  These maps satisfy the ambient simplicial coherence
wherever they are defined.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Every simplicial relation map commutes with the fully degenerate unit and with
the quotient-ordinal collapse defining $D_{u,K}$.  It therefore gives a
natural transformation of the relevant cubes and hence a map on total
cofibres.  Relabelling commutes with the same construction.  A general inner
face may merge two different singleton labels and therefore leave the
separated sector; no face-closed differential is inferred on
$\mathbb B_K^{\mathrm{sep}}(r)$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Support-marked simplicial closure}

\begin{definition}[Support markings]
\label{def:as-support-markings}
Let $I$ be a finite nonempty set and $m\geq0$.  Define
\[
    \mathsf{Supp}_I(m)
    :=
    \operatorname{Map}
    \left(\{1,\ldots,m\},\mathcal P(I)\right).
\]
Empty supports and multi-element supports are allowed.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Support-collapse cube]
\label{con:as-support-collapse-cube}
For $\sigma\in\mathsf{Supp}_I(m)$ and $J\subseteq I$, collapse an edge $e$
precisely when
\[
    \sigma(e)\cap J=\varnothing.
\]
Let
\[
    c_{\sigma,J}:[m]\twoheadrightarrow[m_{\sigma,J}]
\]
be the canonical quotient ordinal and define
\[
    D_{\sigma,K}(J)
    :=
    \widetilde{\mathbb S}_{S;X}[K_{m_{\sigma,J}}],
    \qquad
    N_\sigma(K):=\operatorname{tcofib}D_{\sigma,K}.
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Transport of support]
\label{con:as-support-transport}
For $\alpha:[n]\to[m]$ in $\Delta$ and
$\sigma\in\mathsf{Supp}_I(m)$ define
\[
    (\alpha_!\sigma)(j)
    :=
    \bigcup_{\alpha(j-1)<e\leq\alpha(j)}\sigma(e),
    \qquad 1\leq j\leq n,
\]
with the empty union interpreted as $\varnothing$.  Thus an inner face replaces
adjacent supports by their union and a degeneracy inserts the empty support.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Strict support functoriality]
\label{lem:as-support-functoriality}
For composable simplicial operators,
\[
    (\alpha\beta)_!=\beta_!\alpha_!.
\]
Moreover support transport commutes with every unit-collapse quotient
$c_{\sigma,J}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Both assertions are identities of unions over successive intervals in a finite
ordinal.  The retained edges for $J$ are exactly those whose transported
support meets $J$, independently of whether transport or collapse is performed
first.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Support-marked Cech object]
\label{def:as-support-marked-cech}
Define a simplicial object of the common-base stable category $\mathcal E_S$ by
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb B_I^{\mathrm{supp}}(K)_m
    :=
    \bigoplus_{\sigma\in\mathsf{Supp}_I(m)}N_\sigma(K).}
\]
Its structure maps are induced by
Construction~\ref{con:as-support-transport} and
Definition~\ref{def:as-relative-reduced-stabilization}.  Stable normalization
is denoted
\[
    N\mathbb B_I^{\mathrm{supp}}(K).
\]
The singleton exhaustive marked-word cells form distinguished summands in this
face-closed object.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Simplicial closure and symmetric action]
\label{prop:as-support-marked-simplicial}
$\mathbb B_I^{\mathrm{supp}}(K)$ is a canonically defined simplicial stable
object in $\mathcal E_S$, functorial in simplicial relation maps preserving the
fully degenerate copy of $X$, and equivariant for bijections of $I$.
Consequently its stable normalization carries the actual normalized
differential.  No PD substitution law is imposed on it.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A general simplicial operator need not preserve the source projection
$K_m\to X$, which is why the construction is made in $\mathcal E_S$ rather
than $\mathcal E_X$.  It does preserve the fully degenerate map $X\to K_m$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-support-functoriality} therefore gives a natural
transformation of relative reduced stabilization cubes for every face and
degeneracy.  Total cofibres and finite coproducts are functorial.  Relabelling
$I$ commutes with support unions and collapse, so stable normalization is
well-defined and equivariant.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Raw and geometric-formalized marked systems]
\label{def:as-raw-formalized-operation-systems}
For a finite nonempty set $I$ put
\[
    \mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,raw}}(I)
    :=
    N\mathbb B_I^{\mathrm{supp}}(\mathfrak G_\bullet),
\]
\[
    \mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}(I)
    :=
    N\mathbb B_I^{\mathrm{supp}}
    (R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}).
\]
Write $\mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{sep,raw}}(r)$ and
$\mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{sep,fm}}(r)$ for the corresponding separated
singleton-marked collections when $|I|=r$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Raw-to-geometric-formalized marked comparison]
\label{thm:as-raw-formalized-operation-comparison}
The relation formalization map induces, for every finite nonempty $I$,
\[
\boxed{
    \Lambda_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(I):
    \mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,raw}}(I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}(I),}
\]
compatible with the normalized differential and the $\Sigma_I$-action.  It
also induces the evident map on the separated singleton-marked collections.
Define the geometric marked discrepancy
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb C_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{op,Art}}(I)
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(\Lambda_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(I)\right).}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The simplicial map $\lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}$ commutes with every fully
degenerate unit and every support-collapse quotient.  Hence it gives a natural
transformation of each relative reduced stabilization cube.  These
transformations commute with support transport, so they assemble to a map of
support-marked simplicial objects.  Normalization and cofibres are functorial.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Scalar duals, reduced Artinian bars, and the relative Hurewicz map}

\begin{definition}[Support-marked scalar dual]
\label{def:as-support-marked-scalar-dual}
Let $K_\bullet\rightrightarrows X$ be a simplicial relation over $S$ with
fully degenerate copy $X\to K_\bullet$.  For finite nonempty $I$, define the
cosimplicial scalar support object
\[
\boxed{
    \mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp},\bullet}_{\mathcal O,I}(K)
    :=
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal E_S}
    \left(
        \mathbb B_I^{\mathrm{supp}}(K)_\bullet,
        \mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
    \right).
}
\]
The cosimplicial structure is obtained by contravariance of internal Hom.
For the formalized relation write
\[
    \mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp,fm},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}(I)
    :=
    \mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp},\bullet}_{\mathcal O,I}
    (R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}).
\]
Its source-fixed primitive normalization is
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{supp,fm,prim}}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}(I)
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \left(
        \operatorname{Dec}_s
        \mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp,fm},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}(I)
    \right)[-1].
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Scalar restriction along an Artinian Cech test]
\label{prop:as-support-scalar-artinian-restriction}
Let
\[
    \widetilde q:\operatorname{Spec}A\longrightarrow
    Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
be an Artinian point centred at $U=\operatorname{Spec}B$.  The simplicial map
\[
    \beta_{\widetilde q}:
    \check C_\bullet(U/\operatorname{Spec}A)
    \longrightarrow
    R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
induces a canonical morphism of cosimplicial scalar support objects
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{supp}}_{\widetilde q}:
    \mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp,fm},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}(I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp},\bullet}_{A/B,\mathcal O}(I).
}
\]
Here the target is the scalar support object obtained from the represented
Artinian Cech nerve.  In particular, this is a scalar restriction map; the
geometric stable object
$\mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}(I)$ is not its source.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The map $\beta_{\widetilde q}$ gives, in every simplicial degree and for every
support marking, a map of support-collapse cubes after relative reduced
stabilization.  Internal Hom into $\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}$ reverses this
map.  Since the support set is finite, internal Hom converts the finite total
cofibres defining the geometric support cells into the corresponding total
fibres of scalar functions.  Compatibility with support transport gives the
cosimplicial morphism.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Relative reduced scalar dual]
\label{def:as-reduced-scalar-dual}
For a pointed morphism $e:X\to Y$ over $S$, define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathscr O_{\mathrm{red},S}(Y;X)
    :=
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal E_S}
    \left(
        \operatorname{cofib}
        (\Sigma_{+,S}^{\infty}X\to\Sigma_{+,S}^{\infty}Y),
        \mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}
    \right).
}
\]
On a represented affine diagram
$X=\operatorname{Spec}B\to Y=\operatorname{Spec}C$, affine evaluation
identifies this object with the augmentation fibre
\[
    I_C:=\operatorname{fib}(C\to B).
\]
For a finite cube, internal Hom converts the total cofiber into the
corresponding total fibre of these reduced scalar augmentation fibres.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Relative Hurewicz primitive map]
\label{con:as-hurewicz-primitive-map}
Let $C\to B$ be a connective augmentation and put
\[
    I_C:=\operatorname{fib}(C\to B).
\]
Since $\operatorname{cofib}(C\to B)\simeq I_C[1]$, the canonical
relative-cofiber-to-cotangent morphism gives, after base change and shifting,
\[
\boxed{
    h_C:
    B\otimes_C I_C
    \longrightarrow
    L_{B/C}[-1].
}
\]
Composing $I_C\to B\otimes_CI_C$ with $h_C$ gives the natural scalar-to-
cotangent Hurewicz comparison.  No base change is built into the definition of
$I_C$ itself.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Naturality and first unresolved coefficient]
\label{prop:as-hurewicz-primitive-naturality}
Construction~\ref{con:as-hurewicz-primitive-map} is natural in commutative
diagrams of augmented connective spectral algebras.  Whenever the relative
cofiber is $n$-connective with $n\geq1$ and the augmentation is an isomorphism
on $\pi_0$, the map induces the relative spectral Hurewicz isomorphism on the
first nonzero homotopy group.  No equivalence of the whole reduced scalar
augmentation fibre with the cotangent complex is asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Naturality is the naturality of the relative-cofiber-to-cotangent morphism.
The homotopy statement is precisely the relative spectral Hurewicz theorem of
the preceding chapter.
\end{proof}

Let $A\to B$ be a spectral Artinian augmentation and put
\[
    P_A^{[m]}
    :=
    \underbrace{B\otimes_A\cdots\otimes_A B}_{m+1}.
\]
For $\sigma\in\mathsf{Supp}_I(m)$ and $J\subseteq I$, let
\[
    C_{\sigma,J}\longrightarrow B
\]
be the augmented algebra obtained from $P_A^{[m]}$ by the same edge-collapse
rule as in Construction~\ref{con:as-support-collapse-cube}.

\begin{definition}[Multi-reduced scalar Artinian bar]
\label{def:as-reduced-scalar-marked-primitive}
For every support marking define the scalar support cross-effect
\[
    \operatorname{RedCell}_{A/B}(\sigma)
    :=
    \operatorname*{tfib}_{J\subseteq I}
    I_{C_{\sigma,J}},
    \qquad
    I_{C_{\sigma,J}}
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}(C_{\sigma,J}\to B).
\]
Assemble these finite total fibres over all support markings in each Cech
degree and use the scalar restriction maps induced by support transport.  Let
\[
\boxed{
    \mathscr R_{A/B}^{\bullet}(I)
}
\]
denote the resulting cosimplicial scalar support object and put
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{RedBar}_{A/B}(I)
    :=
    N^\bullet\mathscr R_{A/B}^{\bullet}(I).
}
\]
Equivalently, $\mathscr R_{A/B}^{\bullet}(I)$ is the scalar dual, on the
Artinian Cech test, of the support-marked relative reduced stabilization of
the relation.  The separated singleton-marked words give distinguished
normalized cells, but the full support-marked closure supplies the
differential.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Source-fixed primitive reduced scalar bar]
\label{def:as-primitive-reduced-bar}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{RedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A/B}(I)
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \left(
        \operatorname{Dec}_s
        \mathscr R_{A/B}^{\bullet}(I)
    \right)[-1].
}
\]
Thus source-fixed degree zero comes from Cech degree one and is internally
desuspended once.  For a singleton support this degree-zero term is the
shifted reduced diagonal scalar coefficient
\[
    I_{P_{A/B}}[-1].
\]
For general $I$ it is the corresponding fully $I$-multireduced support
coefficient of the single Cech diagonal.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Why the scalar fibre is not base-changed]
The object $I_C=\operatorname{fib}(C\to B)$ is the full reduced scalar
augmentation fibre.  The object $B\otimes_CI_C$ occurs only as the middle term
of the Hurewicz comparison
\[
    I_C\longrightarrow B\otimes_CI_C\longrightarrow L_{B/C}[-1].
\]
Keeping these terms separate prevents the scalar cross-effect from being
silently replaced by its conormal quotient.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Multi-filtered Artinian primitive operations}

Let $I$ be a finite nonempty set and write
\[
    \operatorname{Fil}_I(\operatorname{Mod}_B)
    :=
    \operatorname{Fun}
    ((\mathbb N^I)^{\mathrm{op}},\operatorname{Mod}_B)
\]
with multi-Day convolution.  For $i\in I$ let $e_i$ be the corresponding basis
vector and set
\[
    \mathbf1_I:=\sum_{i\in I}e_i.
\]

\begin{definition}[Rectangular multi-completion]
\label{def:as-multi-completion}
For $\mathbf n=(n_i)_{i\in I}$ let $Q_{\mathbf n}$ denote successive quotient
by filtration pieces beyond the rectangle $0\leq a_i\leq n_i$.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \widehat F
    :=
    \lim_{\mathbf n\in\mathbb N^I}Q_{\mathbf n}F.
}
\]
A multi-filtered object is complete when $F\to\widehat F$ is an equivalence.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Connectivity of tails of almost-perfect filtered modules]
\label{lem:as-aperf-tail-connectivity}
Let $B$ be concentrated in filtration weight zero and let $F^\bullet M$ be a
connective almost-perfect filtered $B$-module.  For every integer $d$ there is
$N_d$ such that
\[
\boxed{
    F^nM\in\operatorname{Mod}_{B,\geq d}
    \qquad
    (n\geq N_d).
}
\]
The same statement holds in a finite multi-filtration: outside a sufficiently
large rectangle every filtration value is $d$-connective.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose a perfect filtered module $P_d$ and a map $P_d\to M$ with
$d$-connective cofiber $C_d$.  Since $B$ is concentrated in weight zero, a
perfect filtered $B$-module is a retract of an object built from finitely many
filtration shifts of $B$ by finite limits and colimits.  Hence $P_d$ is
supported in only finitely many filtration weights; choose $N_d$ with
$F^nP_d\simeq0$ for $n\geq N_d$.  Then $F^nM\simeq F^nC_d$ for those $n$,
and the pointwise filtration $t$-structure makes this $d$-connective.

For finite $I$, a perfect multi-filtered module is built from finitely many
multi-filtration shifts of $B$ and is therefore supported in a finite
rectangle.  The same approximation argument gives the final statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite multi-filtered almost-perfect completeness]
\label{lem:as-multi-filtered-complete}
For finite $I$, every connective almost-perfect multi-filtered $B$-module is
rectangularly complete.  Coordinatewise completion functors commute, and
rectangular completion agrees with iterated one-variable completion.  In
particular the multi-filtered cotangent objects constructed from finite
multi-filtered structured square-zero extensions are complete.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Coordinate quotient and completion functors are formed from finite fibres,
cofibres, and inverse limits in the stable functor category.  Fubini for limits
over the finite product of copies of $\mathbb N$ identifies rectangular
completion with iterated coordinatewise completion and shows that the
coordinate completion functors commute.

Let $M$ be connective and almost perfect.  For every $d$, choose a perfect
multi-filtered approximation $P_d\to M$ with $d$-connective cofiber.  The
perfect object is supported in a finite rectangle and hence is already
complete.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:as-aperf-tail-connectivity}, outside a
sufficiently large rectangle the cofiber has $d$-connective filtration values.
The multi-variable Milnor argument therefore makes the fibre of
$M\to\widehat M$ $(d-1)$-connective.  Since $d$ is arbitrary, that fibre
vanishes.

For a finite multi-filtered structured square-zero extension, the filtered
square-zero finiteness argument of the preceding chapter is internal to the
finite Day-convolution stable category and makes its relative cotangent fibre
connective and almost perfect.  Apply the first part.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite multi-filtered cotangent--square-zero adjunction]
\label{prop:as-multifiltered-cot-sqz}
For finite $I$, the unrestricted multi-filtered Day-convolution categories
carry a canonical relative adjunction
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{cot}^{I}_{B/R}
    \dashv
    \operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R}.
}
\]
On the connective almost-perfect derivation sector generated by finitely many
positive multiweights, the structured square-zero objects and their cotangent
fibres lie in the rectangularly complete sector.  Hence the induced comonad
\[
\boxed{
    G_I
    :=
    \operatorname{cot}^{I}_{B/R}
    \operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R}
}
\]
is defined before completion and its relevant values are already complete.
Evaluation at multiweight zero recovers the ordinary relative
cotangent--square-zero adjunction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The structured square-zero pullback and the relative cotangent universal
property are defined in any presentable stable symmetric monoidal category in
which the tensor product is exact and colimit-preserving separately.  These
properties hold for the finite multi-Day-convolution category, so the same
mapping-space proof as in the unfiltered relative adjunction gives
$\operatorname{cot}^{I}_{B/R}\dashv\operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R}$.

For the finite positive-multiweight objects used below, the filtered
square-zero finiteness proof of the preceding chapter applies coordinatewise:
it uses only stability, Day convolution, finite relation cells, the relative
Hurewicz comparison, and finite bar constructions.  Thus the cotangent fibre
is connective and almost perfect as a multi-filtered module.
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-multi-filtered-complete} makes it rectangularly complete.
No completion is inserted into the adjunction or its comonad.  Evaluation at
weight zero preserves the finite square-zero pullback and relative cotangent
formation, giving the unfiltered adjunction.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Multi-filtered structured square-zero test and Spencer coLie object]
\label{con:as-multi-filtered-colie}
Let
\[
    \alpha_i:L_{B/R}[-1]\longrightarrow M_i,
    \qquad i\in I,
\]
be connective almost-perfect relative derivations.  Place the source in
multiweight $0$ and $M_i$ in multiweight $e_i$.  The direct sum of the
classifying maps gives a multi-filtered relative derivation
\[
    D_I:
    L_{B/R}[-1]
    \longrightarrow
    \bigoplus_{i\in I}M_i(e_i).
\]
Form its structured multi-filtered square-zero extension
\[
    A_I
    :=
    \operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R}(D_I)
    \longrightarrow B.
\]
Then
\[
    G_I(D_I)
    =
    \operatorname{cot}^{I}_{B/R}(A_I)
    =
    L_{B/A_I}[-1]
\]
with its relative anchor from $L_{B/R}[-1]$.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-multi-filtered-complete} this filtered cotangent object is
already complete.  Put
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)
    :=
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}
    G_I(D_I)
    =
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}L_{B/A_I}[-1].
}
\]
Thus $\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)$ is a symmetric
multifunctor of the indexed derivation variables; no tautological $I$-tuple is
attached to a formal leaf.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Symmetry and zero-anchor associated graded]
\label{prop:as-multi-filtered-colie}
The multi-filtered construction is functorial in the indexed derivations and
invariant under bijections of $I$.  Its associated graded is canonically the
zero-anchor multi-filtered coLie object:
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Gr}G_I(D_I)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Gr}G_I(D_I^0),
}
\]
where $D_I^0$ has the same target
$\bigoplus_iM_i(e_i)$ and zero classifying map.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Associated graded is symmetric monoidal and preserves the finite pullback
defining the structured square-zero extension.  The source of every
classifying map is in weight zero and its target is in a positive basis weight,
so each classifying map vanishes on associated graded.  Symmetry is permutation
of the filtration coordinates.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Multi-filtered diagonal cotangent primitive]
\label{def:as-cotangent-marked-primitive}
For the multi-filtered square-zero test $A_I\to B$, form its single Cech
diagonal
\[
    P_I:=B\otimes_{A_I}B\longrightarrow B
\]
in multi-filtered connective commutative algebras.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)
    :=
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}
    L_{B/P_I}[-2].
}
\]
This is a diagonal primitive formed after the independent directions have been
assembled.  It is not the total fibre of the vertexwise cotangent complexes of
the support-collapse cube.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Multi-filtered diagonal primitive theorem]
\label{thm:as-multifiltered-diagonal-primitive}
There is a canonical natural equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr).
}
\]
It is symmetric in $I$ and compatible with coherent spectral-etale base
change.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The bar--cotangent calculation of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-diagonal-cotangent} is internal to any finite
Day-convolution multi-filtered stable coefficient category.  Applied to
$A_I\to B$, cotangent base change gives
\[
    B\otimes_{P_I}L_{P_I/A_I}
    \simeq
    L_{B/A_I}\oplus L_{B/A_I},
\]
and transitivity identifies the first map with the fold.  Hence the
anti-diagonal fibre gives a canonical multi-filtered equivalence
\[
    L_{B/P_I}[-2]
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    L_{B/A_I}[-1]
    =G_I(D_I).
\]
Taking $\mathbf1_I$-graded pieces gives the displayed equivalence.  Naturality
of cotangent base change and transitivity proves symmetry and etale
compatibility.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why the vertexwise cotangent support cube is not used]
For two split directions the vertexwise cotangent support cube has the form
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
L\oplus L \arrow[r] \arrow[d] & L \arrow[d]\\
L \arrow[r] & 0,
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where the two nontrivial arrows are the coordinate projections.  Its total
fibre is zero.  More generally, singleton vertexwise cotangent cubes decompose
as sums of one-coordinate functors and therefore have vanishing higher
cross-effects.  The higher operation is instead the fully multilinear piece
of the single diagonal primitive of
Definition~\ref{def:as-cotangent-marked-primitive}.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Labelled two-level zero-anchor decomposition]
\label{lem:as-two-level-partition-decomposition}
Let $I$ be finite nonempty and let
\[
    D_I^0:
    L_{B/R}[-1]
    \xrightarrow{0}
    \bigoplus_{i\in I}M_i(e_i)
\]
be the zero-anchor multi-graded derivation.  The exact multiweight of the
twice-iterated cotangent--square-zero comonad has a canonical decomposition
indexed by set partitions:
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^2(D_I^0)
    \simeq
    \bigoplus_{[\rho:I\twoheadrightarrow K]\in\operatorname{Part}(I)}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \left(
        K;
        \left(
            \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
            (I_k;(M_i,0)_{i\in I_k}),0
        \right)_{k\in K}
    \right),
\end{aligned}}
\]
where $I_k:=\rho^{-1}(k)$ and the sum is over surjections up to bijection of
the target.  Under
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-multi-filtered-colie}, the same decomposition
canonically identifies
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^2(D_I)$ after replacing each zero-anchor
inner exact multiweight by
$\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
(I_k;(M_i,\alpha_i)_{i\in I_k})$.

For a refinement of a partition, the two possible iterated decompositions are
canonically the two readings of the same three-level labelled rooted tree.
Consequently the decomposition is equivariant for bijections of $I$ and
coherently compatible with refinement.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:as-multi-filtered-colie}, associated graded kills
every positive-weight classifying map.  The exact-multiweight calculation is
therefore the zero-anchor calculation.  In the zero-anchor sector the
cotangent--square-zero comonad is the coalgebraic PD Koszul-dual comonad of
the nonunital spectral $E_\infty$ square-zero algebra construction.  The
bar resolution used in the preceding chapter builds its underlying functor by
colimits and compositions of the homogeneous derived symmetric-power
functors.  After one application, a homogeneous term is represented by one
symmetric-power vertex.  After two applications, expanding the outer
symmetric power of the inner homogeneous terms gives nested symmetric-power
terms; equivalently these are finite two-level rooted relation trees.

Take exact multiweight $\mathbf1_I$.  Expanding each tensor power before
passing to finite homotopy orbits shows that every surviving tensor word
contains one copy of each $M_i$ and no repeated label.  The inner
symmetric-power factors therefore partition the set of labels: two labels
determine the same block precisely when they occur in the same inner
homogeneous factor, equivalently when their leaves enter the same inner
vertex.  Thus every indexing tree determines a
unique set partition
\[
    I=\coprod_{k\in K}I_k.
\]
Conversely, after fixing such a partition, the inner vertices are independent.
The inner vertex indexed by $I_k$ contributes exactly
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_{I_k}}G_{I_k}(D_{I_k}^0)
    =
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    (I_k;(M_i,0)_{i\in I_k}).
\]
This output lies in strictly positive inner weight, so its relative anchor is
zero.  Inserting the block outputs in independent outer basis weights and
taking exact outer weight gives the displayed outer
$\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$ term.

The indexing groupoid of labelled two-level trees is the disjoint union of
the full subgroupoids with a fixed induced partition.  Colimits over this
disjoint union therefore give the displayed canonical direct sum; no splitting
is chosen.  A labelled three-level tree is equivalently a partition together
with a refinement of each block.  Contracting the upper or lower internal
level gives the two iterated partition decompositions.  Associativity of tree
contraction and comonad coassociativity identify them coherently, and
relabelling the leaves gives the symmetric equivariance.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Block extraction and the partition component]
\label{con:as-block-regrading}
Let
\[
    I=\coprod_{j\in J}I_j
\]
be a partition into nonempty blocks and let
\[
    \pi:I\twoheadrightarrow J
\]
be the corresponding block projection.  For an $I_j$-multi-graded object $V$
define its exact inner block extraction by
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Ex}_{I_j}(V)
    :=
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_{I_j}}V.
}
\]
For a tuple of $B$-modules $(N_j)_{j\in J}$ define outer insertion by placing
one copy of $N_j$ in the new basis weight $e_j$,
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Ins}_{J}(N_j)
    :=
    \bigoplus_{j\in J}N_j(e_j).
}
\]
If the $N_j$ are zero-anchor relative derivations, the outer inserted
derivation is
\[
    D^{\mathrm{out}}_J(N_j):
    L_{B/R}[-1]
    \xrightarrow{0}
    \operatorname{Ins}_{J}(N_j).
\]

Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:as-two-level-partition-decomposition} to the associated
graded of the actual iterated comonad.  It gives the canonical decomposition
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^2(D_I)
    \simeq
    \bigoplus_{[\rho:I\twoheadrightarrow K]\in\operatorname{Part}(I)}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \left(
        K;
        \left(
            \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
            (I_k;(M_i,\alpha_i)_{i\in I_k}),0
        \right)_{k\in K}
    \right).
\end{aligned}}
\]
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{pr}_{\pi}^{\mathrm{part}}
}
\]
to be projection to the summand indexed by the chosen partition
$\pi:I\twoheadrightarrow J$.  The refinement and permutation coherences of
this projection are those of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-two-level-partition-decomposition}.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[PD cooperation substitution from multiweights]
\label{thm:as-multiweight-pd-substitution}
For a partition $I=\coprod_{j\in J}I_j$ into nonempty blocks, multi-filtered
comonad comultiplication induces canonical maps
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
  \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)\\
&\qquad\longrightarrow
\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
  \left(
      J;
      \left(
        \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
        (I_j;(M_i,\alpha_i)_{i\in I_j}),0
      \right)_{j\in J}
  \right).
\end{aligned}}
\]
They are equivariant for bijections, unital for singleton blocks, and
coherently associative for iterated partitions.  Under
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-spencer-colie-equals-colie}, they are the complete PD
cooperations of the spectral cotangent--square-zero coLie system.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The comonad $G_I$ is formed first in the unrestricted multi-filtered category.
Its comultiplication preserves the multi-filtration.
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-multi-filtered-complete} shows that the actual cotangent
objects already lie in the complete sector, so no completion is inserted into
the comonad.  Apply exact multiweight $\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}$ to the
multi-filtered comonad comultiplication.  Proposition~\ref{prop:as-multi-filtered-colie} identifies its associated-graded target with the zero-anchor
two-level bar.  Construction~\ref{con:as-block-regrading} gives the canonical
projection $\operatorname{pr}_{\pi}^{\mathrm{part}}$ from that exact
two-level multiweight to the component of the chosen partition.  Composing
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}$ of comultiplication with this projection is
the displayed cooperation.  Every exact positive block output has zero anchor
because the anchor source is concentrated in multiweight zero, so it is a
valid outer input.  The counit supplies the singleton unit.  For an iterated
partition, Construction~\ref{con:as-block-regrading} identifies the two
partition extractions with the two ways of reading the same three-level tree,
and comonad coassociativity identifies the two iterated comultiplications.
This gives all nested-partition coherence.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Integral partition-Lie arity system]
\label{cor:as-integral-pd-arity}
On dually almost-perfect coefficients, continuous pro-coherent duality of the
multiweight cooperation system is the arity/substitution system of
$\operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}$.  Denote the resulting symmetric
multi-input functors by
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Op}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(I;-)
    :=
    \left(
        \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)
    \right)^\vee
}
\]
on the dense continuously dualizable sector and by their canonical right-left
sifted extensions on all pro-coherent inputs.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
On zero anchor this is the refined pro-coherent PD Koszul-dual construction of
the preceding chapter.  Proposition~\ref{prop:as-multi-filtered-colie}
reduces the associated-graded relative calculation to that case, while
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison} identifies the complete relative
monad.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Separate exactness of the multi-input operation system]
\label{prop:as-operation-separate-exactness}
For every finite nonempty set $I$, the exact multiweight functor
\[
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)
\]
is exact separately in each underlying coefficient variable $M_i$ on finite
stable diagrams for which the induced relative-derivation diagrams remain in
the connective almost-perfect sector.  On the dually almost-perfect dual
sector the functor
\[
    \operatorname{Op}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(I;-)
\]
is exact separately in every variable.  Its canonical polarized
pro-coherent right-left extension remains exact separately in every input
variable.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix a label $i_0\in I$.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-multi-filtered-colie}, the exact
$\mathbf1_I$-multiweight of the actual relative construction is canonically
the corresponding exact multiweight of the zero-anchor associated graded:
\[
\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
\bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)
\simeq
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I^0).
\]
Thus only the exact labelled zero-anchor arity term has to be checked.

In the zero-anchor bar, every homogeneous summand in exact multiweight
$\mathbf1_I$ contains each labelled coefficient $M_i$ exactly once.  More
explicitly, after resolving the zero-anchor coLie comonad by its PD
relation-tree bar, a labelled tree contributes a finite composite of tensor
products in which the $i$-th external input occurs once, followed by finite
homotopy orbits and finite fibre/cofiber constructions.  Tensor product in
the stable coefficient category is exact separately in every variable;
finite homotopy orbits are exact; and exact multiweight extraction is a finite
fibre/cofiber construction.  Hence the exact $\mathbf1_I$ term carries a
fibre or cofiber sequence in $M_{i_0}$ to the corresponding fibre or cofiber
sequence.  Since $i_0$ was arbitrary,
$\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)$ is exact separately in all
coefficient variables on the stated dense sector.

Notice that no complete-filtered-detection argument is used here.  The
statement concerns the already extracted exact multiweight; the full
multi-filtered coLie object contains higher powers of a given input and is
not asserted to be separately linear before exact multiweight extraction.

Continuous duality is exact, so the dual labelled arity functor is separately
exact on dually almost-perfect inputs.  The pro-coherent right-left
construction is applied to the labelled polarized arity functors before the
diagonal PD assembly.  In the exact-functor model, right-left extension in a
fixed labelled variable preserves finite exact diagrams in that variable,
because the dense functor is exact there and the defining finite
totalization and almost-eventually-constant tower relations are stable under
finite fibres and cofibres.  Iterating over the finite set of labels proves
separate exactness on all pro-coherent inputs.  The homotopy fixed-point
functors used only after assembling equal unmarked inputs are limits in a
stable category and are exact as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Descended Artinian operation multifunctor]
\label{def:as-descended-artinian-operations}
On a coherent affine-etale pair $(U,V)$ define
\[
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}_{\mathfrak G,U/V}(I;-)
\]
to be the symmetric multifunctor
$\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)$ on the relative derivation
category of that chart.  Spectral-etale base change identifies these
multifunctors and their cooperation maps, so they descend to a global
multi-input operation system
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}_{\mathfrak G}(I;-).
}
\]
Transport through the coSp--coLie equivalence gives
$\operatorname{coOp}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{\mathfrak G}(I;-)$.  Evaluation on a
particular controller is performed only after its actual tangent variables
have been supplied.
\end{definition}

\subsection{Directional Artinian cross-effects and the comonadic scalar-diagonal bar}
\label{sec:as-relative-operadic-bar}

The support-marked geometric Cech object and the multi-derivative of an
Artinian formal leaf are different constructions.  The support-marked object
records nonlinear raw/formalized relation geometry.  The multi-derivative is
obtained by switching the independent square-zero directions themselves on
and off.  We construct that directional cube first and then construct, from
the cotangent--square-zero comonadic resolution, the scalar-diagonal bar which
maps to the primitive cotangent cobar.

\begin{definition}[Source-fixed Cech primitive]
\label{def:as-associative-primitive-bar}
Let $A_I\to B$ be the multi-filtered square-zero test and put
\[
    P_{A_I}^{[m]}
    :=
    \underbrace{B\otimes_{A_I}\cdots\otimes_{A_I}B}_{m+1},
    \qquad m\geq0.
\]
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathsf{CechPrim}^{n}_{A_I/B}
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        P_{A_I}^{[n+1]}\longrightarrow B
    \right)[-1],
    \qquad n\geq0.
}
\]
The cofaces and codegeneracies are those of the source-fixed decalage of the
Cech nerve.  In particular source-fixed degree zero is
\[
    \mathsf{CechPrim}^{0}_{A_I/B}
    =
    \operatorname{fib}(B\otimes_{A_I}B\to B)[-1].
\]
Thus the source-fixed index is $P^{[n+1]}$, not $P^{[n+2]}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Directional square-zero cube]
\label{con:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison}
Retain
\[
    D_I:
    L_{B/R}[-1]
    \longrightarrow
    \bigoplus_{i\in I}M_i(e_i).
\]
For $J\subseteq I$, let
\[
    D_J:
    L_{B/R}[-1]
    \longrightarrow
    \bigoplus_{j\in J}M_j(e_j)
\]
be the composite with the coordinate projection, regarded as an
$I$-multi-filtered derivation with zero coefficient in the missing
coordinates, and define
\[
\boxed{
    A_J:=\operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R}(D_J)\longrightarrow B.
}
\]
For $J\subseteq K$, projection $D_K\to D_J$ induces a morphism
$A_K\to A_J$.  In Cech degree $m$ put
\[
    P_J^{[m]}
    :=
    \underbrace{B\otimes_{A_J}\cdots\otimes_{A_J}B}_{m+1},
    \qquad
    I_J^{[m]}
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}(P_J^{[m]}\to B).
\]
The maps $A_K\to A_J$ induce compatible maps
$P_K^{[m]}\to P_J^{[m]}$ and $I_K^{[m]}\to I_J^{[m]}$.

For $n\geq0$ define the source-fixed directional primitive by
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \mathsf{DirPrim}_{A_I/B}^{n}(I)
    &:={}
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        I_I^{[n+1]}[-1]
        \longrightarrow
        \lim_{J\subsetneq I}I_J^{[n+1]}[-1]
    \right),\\
    \mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)
    &:={}
    N^\bullet\mathsf{DirPrim}_{A_I/B}^{\bullet}(I).
\end{aligned}}
\]
Equivalently, $\mathsf{DirPrim}$ is the total fibre of the cube obtained by
switching the square-zero directions themselves on and off.  The Cech maps
are natural in $A_J$, so these directional total fibres form a cosimplicial
object before normalization.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Directional cross-effect--positive-multiweight identification]
\label{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}
For the universal finite multi-filtered square-zero test $A_I\to B$, there is
a canonical equivalence of coherent cochain objects
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    N^\bullet
    F^{\mathbf1_I}
    \mathsf{CechPrim}^{\bullet}_{A_I/B}.
}
\]
Here $F^{\mathbf1_I}$ is the all-positive filtration stage: every occurring
multiweight $\mathbf a=(a_i)_{i\in I}$ satisfies $a_i\geq1$ for every $i$.
The equivalence is symmetric in $I$, compatible with the source-fixed Cech
differential, and natural under coherent spectral-etale base change.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $J\subsetneq I$, the $i$-th positive filtration of $A_J$ is zero whenever
$i\notin J$.  Hence the all-positive filtration of every
$I_J^{[n+1]}[-1]$ vanishes for proper $J$, and the inclusion
\[
    F^{\mathbf1_I}I_I^{[n+1]}[-1]
    \longrightarrow
    I_I^{[n+1]}[-1]
\]
canonically factors through the directional total fibre.

Pass to associated graded.  The multi-filtered classifying maps start in
weight zero and land in positive basis weights, so they vanish on associated
graded.  The square-zero algebra becomes the split multi-graded square-zero
algebra on the independent generators $M_i(e_i)$.  A homogeneous Cech word
has a multiweight $\mathbf a$.  Under the map from the full test to the
$J$-test, that word survives exactly when
\[
    \operatorname{supp}(\mathbf a)
    :=\{i\mid a_i>0\}
    \subseteq J.
\]
The Boolean total fibre over the directional cube therefore kills the word
unless $\operatorname{supp}(\mathbf a)=I$, and is the word itself when every
coordinate occurs.  Thus
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}
    \mathsf{DirPrim}_{A_I/B}^{n}(I)
    \simeq
    \bigoplus_{a_i\geq1}
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf a}
    \mathsf{CechPrim}_{A_I/B}^{n}
    =
    \operatorname{Gr}
    F^{\mathbf1_I}
    \mathsf{CechPrim}_{A_I/B}^{n}.
\]

The finite multi-filtered square-zero finiteness theorem makes all terms
connective and almost perfect, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-multi-filtered-complete} makes them rectangularly complete.
The positive tails become arbitrarily connective by
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-aperf-tail-connectivity}.  Complete filtered detection
therefore upgrades the associated-graded equivalence to the displayed
filtered equivalence.  Naturality of the Cech maps gives the cosimplicial
compatibility, and permutation of filtration coordinates gives the symmetric
action.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Support markings and directional derivatives are distinct]
The support-collapse cube of
Construction~\ref{con:as-support-collapse-cube} collapses relation edges.
It does not switch the square-zero coefficient directions on and off.  In
particular it is not used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}.  The support-marked
system remains the geometric raw/formalized comparison object, while the
directional cube is the Artinian multi-derivative.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{The comonadic scalar-diagonal bar}

Retain the finite multi-filtered adjunction
\[
    L_I:=\operatorname{cot}^{I}_{B/R}
    \dashv
    R_I:=\operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R},
    \qquad
    G_I:=L_IR_I.
\]
For $A_I=R_I(D_I)$, the cotangent--square-zero adjunction gives its canonical augmented cosimplicial
adjunction cobar
\[
    A_I\longrightarrow A_I^\bullet,
    \qquad
    A_I^n:=R_I G_I^{n+1}(D_I),
    \quad n\geq0.
\]
Every positive term is a structured square-zero extension of $B$.  Because
$A_I$ lies in the image of the right adjoint $R_I$, the counit
$L_IR_I\to\operatorname{id}$ supplies canonical extra codegeneracies for this
augmented adjunction cobar.  In particular its cotangent image is split
cosimplicial.  No square-zero presentation is chosen or retained.

\begin{lemma}[The adjunction cobar becomes the cofree cotangent cobar]
\label{lem:as-adjunction-cobar-cofree}
Let
\[
    \eta_I:\operatorname{id}\longrightarrow R_IL_I,
    \qquad
    \epsilon_I:L_IR_I\longrightarrow\operatorname{id}
\]
be the unit and counit of $L_I\dashv R_I$.  For
$A_I=R_I(D_I)$, applying $L_I$ to the canonical augmented adjunction cobar
$A_I\to A_I^\bullet$ gives a canonical equivalence of augmented
cosimplicial relative derivations
\[
\boxed{
    L_I(A_I\to A_I^\bullet)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \left(
        G_I(D_I)
        \longrightarrow
        G_I^2(D_I)
        \rightrightarrows
        G_I^3(D_I)
        \longrightarrow\cdots
    \right),
}
\]
where the right-hand side is the Cartan cobar of the cofree
$G_I$-coalgebra $(G_I(D_I),\delta_{D_I})$.  In particular,
\[
\boxed{
    L_I(A_I^n)
    \simeq
    G_I^{n+2}(D_I),
    \qquad n\geq0.
}
\]
Under these equivalences every coface induced by insertion of the adjunction
unit becomes insertion of the comonad comultiplication
\[
    \delta=L_I\eta_I R_I:G_I\to G_I^2,
\]
every codegeneracy becomes insertion of the comonad counit $\epsilon_I$, and
the augmented coaction is $\delta_{D_I}$.  Applying the exact multiweight
functor $\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}$ therefore gives precisely the
primitive cotangent cobar of
Definition~\ref{def:as-cotangent-primitive-bar}, including all cofaces,
codegeneracies, and higher coherence.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By definition,
\[
    A_I^n=R_IG_I^{n+1}(D_I),
\]
so
\[
    L_I(A_I^n)
    =L_IR_IG_I^{n+1}(D_I)
    =G_I^{n+2}(D_I),
\]
while the augmented term is
$L_I(A_I)=L_IR_I(D_I)=G_I(D_I)$.

The standard adjunction cobar cofaces are obtained by inserting the unit
$\eta_I:\operatorname{id}\to R_IL_I$.  After applying $L_I$, such an
insertion is exactly
$L_I\eta_I R_I$, which is the comonad comultiplication.  The cobar
codegeneracies are induced by the multiplication
$R_I\epsilon_I L_I$ of the monad $R_IL_I$; after applying $L_I$ these are the
corresponding insertions of the comonad counit.  Since the augmented object
itself is $R_I(D_I)$, the first augmented coface becomes the canonical
coaction
$\delta_{D_I}:G_I(D_I)\to G_I^2(D_I)$ of the cofree coalgebra.

The cosimplicial identities on the adjunction cobar are the triangle and
associativity identities of the same adjunction.  Their images under $L_I$
are therefore exactly the comonad counit and coassociativity identities, with
the same higher coherences.  Exact multiweight extraction preserves the
resulting augmented cosimplicial object.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Exact scalar diagonal primitive and scalar comonadic cobar]
\label{def:as-scalar-comonadic-cobar}
For an $I$-multi-filtered augmentation $C\to B$ put
\[
    P_C:=B\otimes_C B,
    \qquad
    I_{P_C}:=\operatorname{fib}(P_C\to B),
\]
and define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{ScPrim}_I(C)
    :=
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}I_{P_C}[-1].
}
\]
Define the augmented scalar comonadic cobar by
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{-1}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    &:={}
    \operatorname{ScPrim}_I(A_I),\\
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{n}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    &:={}
    \operatorname{ScPrim}_I(A_I^n),
    \qquad n\geq0.
\end{aligned}}
\]
Its cofaces and codegeneracies are induced functorially from the canonical
cotangent--square-zero adjunction cobar.  Put
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{ScCotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Primitive cotangent cobar]
\label{def:as-cotangent-primitive-bar}
The cofree $G_I$-coalgebra on $D_I$ is $(G_I(D_I),\delta_{D_I})$.  Its Cartan
cobar has augmented term
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I)
\]
and nonaugmented cosimplicial degree $n$
\[
\boxed{
    \mathsf{CotCobar}^{n}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    :=
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^{n+2}(D_I),
    \qquad n\geq0.
}
\]
Thus the augmented cobar is
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^2(D_I)
    \rightrightarrows
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^3(D_I)
    \rightrightarrows\cdots.
\]
The comonad counit gives the extra codegeneracy, so the augmented object is
split.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    :=
    N^\bullet\mathsf{CotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Comonadic scalar-diagonal Hurewicz comparison]
\label{thm:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison}
There is a canonical morphism of augmented cosimplicial objects
\[
\boxed{
    h_I^{\mathrm{comon},\bullet}:
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathsf{CotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
}
\]
In augmented degree $-1$ it is
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}I_{P_{A_I}}[-1]
    &\longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}L_{B/P_{A_I}}[-2]\\
    &\xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}L_{B/A_I}[-1]
    =
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I),
\end{aligned}}
\]
where the first arrow is the shifted relative Hurewicz morphism and the second
