is the Cech-diagonal cotangent equivalence.  In cosimplicial degree $n$ the
same construction is applied to $A_I^n$, and its target is
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^{n+2}(D_I)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For any multi-filtered augmentation $C\to B$, the relative
cofiber-to-cotangent morphism applied to
$P_C=B\otimes_C B\to B$ gives
\[
    I_{P_C}[-1]
    \longrightarrow
    L_{B/P_C}[-2].
\]
The natural Cech-diagonal calculation gives
\[
    L_{B/P_C}[-2]
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    L_{B/C}[-1].
\]
Taking exact multiweight $\mathbf1_I$ gives a natural transformation
\[
    \operatorname{ScPrim}_I(C)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}L_{B/C}[-1].
\]
Apply it to the augmented cosimplicial diagram
$A_I\to A_I^\bullet$.  Naturality of the relative Hurewicz map and of the
diagonal cotangent equivalence makes the resulting maps commute with every
coface and codegeneracy.  Since
\[
    L_{B/A_I^n}[-1]
    =
    L_I R_I G_I^{n+1}(D_I)
    =
    G_I^{n+2}(D_I),
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-adjunction-cobar-cofree} identifies these degreewise targets,
with the cofaces and codegeneracies induced from $A_I\to A_I^\bullet$, with
the correctly indexed cofree cotangent cobar.  Naturality of the Hurewicz and
diagonal cotangent maps therefore proves the asserted morphism of augmented
cosimplicial objects, not merely a degreewise comparison.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Bar-level Hurewicz transformation]
\label{con:as-bar-level-hurewicz}
Stable normalization of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison} gives
\[
\boxed{
    h_I^{\mathrm{comon}}:
    \mathbf{ScCotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
}
\]
This is the integral bar-level scalar-to-cotangent comparison.  It is
constructed from the canonical cotangent--square-zero adjunction cobar, not from a
split-augmentation operadic construction and not from the
support-collapse cube.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Compatibility of the comonadic Hurewicz transformation]
\label{prop:as-bar-level-hurewicz}
The morphism $h_I^{\mathrm{comon}}$ is compatible with the normalized
differential, symmetric relabelling, multi-filtered comonad comultiplication,
PD cooperation substitution, and coherent spectral-etale base change.  No
assertion that it is an equivalence is made.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The comparison is natural before normalization in every augmented arrow of
the canonical comonadic resolution.  Hence it commutes with the full
cosimplicial structure and therefore with the normalized differential.
Permutation of the multi-filtration coordinates commutes with square-zero
formation, cotangent, the scalar diagonal, and exact multiweight extraction.
The set-partition cooperations are extracted from the same multi-filtered
comonad comultiplication, so naturality of the Hurewicz and diagonal maps gives
compatibility with PD substitution.  Cotangent base change, square-zero base
change, and the Cech diagonal equivalence are coherent under spectral-etale
transport, proving the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Primitive cotangent totalization]
\label{prop:as-cotbar-totalization}
There are canonical equivalences
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}
    \mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    &\xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)\\
    &\xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr).
\end{aligned}}
\]
The first equivalence identifies both sides with
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I)$; the second is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-multifiltered-diagonal-primitive}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The primitive cotangent cobar is the Cartan cobar of the cofree coalgebra
$(G_I(D_I),\delta_{D_I})$.  Its augmented extra codegeneracy therefore gives
\[
    \operatorname*{Tot}
    \mathsf{CotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I).
\]
By Definition~\ref{def:as-dk-totalization},
\[
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}
    \mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \simeq
    \operatorname*{Tot}
    \mathsf{CotCobar}^{\bullet}_{B/R}(I;D_I),
\]
so the displayed equivalence is correctly typed on the normalized cochain
object.
Since $A_I=R_I(D_I)$,
\[
    G_I(D_I)=L_{B/A_I}[-1].
\]
The multi-filtered single-diagonal theorem gives
\[
    L_{B/(B\otimes_{A_I}B)}[-2]
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    L_{B/A_I}[-1].
\]
Taking exact multiweight $\mathbf1_I$ gives the two displayed equivalences.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Directional diagonal meeting map]
\label{prop:as-directional-diagonal-meeting}
The degree-zero directional primitive has a canonical map to the augmented
scalar comonadic diagonal:
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \left(\mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)\right)^0
    &\simeq
    F^{\mathbf1_I}I_{P_{A_I}}[-1]\\
    &\longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}I_{P_{A_I}}[-1]
    =
    \mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{-1}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
\end{aligned}}
\]
The second arrow is the canonical quotient from the all-positive filtration
stage to exact multiweight.  No morphism of cosimplicial objects from the
directional Cech bar to the comonadic scalar bar is asserted: their
cosimplicial directions are different.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first equivalence is the degree-zero case of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}.  Exact multiweight is
the quotient of the all-positive filtration by the union of the higher
coordinate filtration pieces, giving the second map.  The target is precisely
the definition of the augmented scalar diagonal primitive.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Geometric support restriction and directional Artinian operation extraction]
\label{thm:as-formalized-operation-linearization}
Let
\[
    \widetilde q:
    T=\operatorname{Spec}A\longrightarrow Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
be a coherent spectral Artinian point of a formal leaf, centred at
$U=\operatorname{Spec}B$, and let $I$ be finite nonempty.

The canonical simplicial map
\[
    \beta_{\widetilde q}:
    \check C_\bullet(U/T)
    \longrightarrow
    R_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
induces the already constructed scalar restriction
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{supp,prim}}_{\widetilde q}:
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{supp,fm,prim}}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}(I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbf{RedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A/B}(I).
}
\]
This is the scalar shadow of support-marked geometric formalization.

Independently, for the universal multi-filtered square-zero test $A=A_I$,
Construction~\ref{con:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison},
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-directional-diagonal-meeting} construct the
directional multi-derivative and its exact scalar diagonal.  The canonical
comonadic scalar-diagonal Hurewicz comparison then gives
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{ScCotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \xrightarrow{\ h_I^{\mathrm{comon}}\ }
    \mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I),
}
\]
and after totalization
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}\mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    &\xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr)\\
    &\xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \bigl(I;(M_i,\alpha_i)\bigr).
\end{aligned}}
\]
All constructions are compatible with symmetric relabelling and coherent
spectral-etale base change; the second branch is compatible with PD
cooperation substitution.

No equivalence, or canonical morphism, between the support-marked scalar bar
$\mathbf{RedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)$ and the directional Artinian bar
$\mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)$ is asserted.  The former
remembers edge-support geometry; the latter is the actual multi-directional
square-zero cross-effect.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first map is
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-support-scalar-artinian-restriction} followed by
source-fixed decalage, internal desuspension, and stable normalization.

The directional branch is
Construction~\ref{con:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}.  Its degree-zero
meeting with the scalar comonadic bar is
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-directional-diagonal-meeting}.  The map of normalized
comonadic bars is
Construction~\ref{con:as-bar-level-hurewicz}, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-cotbar-totalization} identifies its cotangent target
after totalization with the single-diagonal primitive and the multiweight
coLie operation.  The stated compatibilities are those of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-bar-level-hurewicz} and the naturality of the
directional cube.  Since the support-collapse cube and the directional
square-zero cube have different vertices and different universal properties,
no comparison between their total fibres is inserted.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Represented quotient collapse for marked operations]
\label{cor:as-represented-raw-formalized-operations}
If $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ is represented, then every
$\Lambda_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(I)$ is an equivalence and every
$\mathbb C_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{op,Art}}(I)$ vanishes.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The quotient formalization is an equivalence, so the two relation nerves and
all support-marked relative stabilization cubes are degreewise equivalent.
This statement concerns the geometric support-marked layer; the directional
Artinian operation system is independently unchanged because its construction
uses only the Artinian square-zero directions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The corrected operation-comparison architecture]
The nonlinear and algebraic constructions form two branches:
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,raw}}(I)
&\longrightarrow
\mathbb B_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}(I),\\
\mathscr B^{\mathrm{supp,fm},\bullet}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}(I)
&\xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{supp}}_{\widetilde q}\ }
\mathscr R_{A/B}^{\bullet}(I)
\longrightarrow
\mathbf{RedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A/B}(I),
\end{aligned}}
\]
while the directional Artinian branch is
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\left(\mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)\right)^0
&\longrightarrow
\mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{-1}_{B/R}(I;D_I),\\
\mathbf{ScCotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
&\xrightarrow{h_I^{\mathrm{comon}}}
\mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I),\\
\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}\mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
&\xrightarrow{\sim}
\operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)\\
&\xrightarrow{\sim}
\operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\operatorname{coOp}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(I;-).
\end{aligned}}
\]
The first branch records geometric formalization.  The second extracts the
actual directional multi-derivative and then compares its exact scalar
diagonal, through the canonical comonadic scalar bar, with the cotangent
operation system.  The Cech and comonadic cosimplicial directions are not
identified.  No raw Artinian primitive object is asserted without a raw
Artinian lift.
\end{remark}

\section{The integral partition-Lie/formalized-Spencer comparison}
\label{sec:as-partition-spencer-comparison}

For a complete almost-finite augmented arrow $A\to B$ define
\[
\boxed{
    D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(A)
    :=
    \left(
        \operatorname{cot}_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(A)
    \right)^\vee.
}
\]
The coLie structure from
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-spencer-colie-equals-colie} gives this object its
$\operatorname{Lie}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}_{B/R}$-algebra structure.

\begin{proposition}[Spencer Koszul-dual cells]
\label{prop:as-spencer-D-cells}
Under
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison}, there is a natural equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{sp}}(A)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(A).
}
\]
For a structured square-zero extension $B_\alpha\to B$, both sides are the
free spectral partition-Lie algebroid on
\[
    M^\vee
    \xrightarrow{\alpha^\vee}
    \mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1].
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Dualize the bar--cotangent equivalence.  The square-zero statement is
Corollary~\ref{cor:as-square-zero-bar-colie} together with the
square-zero/free theorem of the preceding chapter.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Affine Spencer formal-moduli recognition]
\label{thm:as-affine-spencer-recognition}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Diff}_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}:
    \operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{Lie}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}_{B/R}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Anch}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{B/R}
    \right).
}
\]
Its inverse is
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Int}_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(\mathfrak a)(A)
    :=
    \operatorname{Map}
    \left(
        D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(A),
        \mathfrak a
    \right).
}
\]
Under the monad equivalence this is the previously constructed affine
spectral differentiation/integration equivalence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The affine recognition theorem of the preceding chapter is obtained from the
finite cellular subcategory generated by square-zero Koszul-dual cells,
Artinian excision, and cellular restricted Yoneda.
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-spencer-D-cells} identifies the cells and their
attachment maps, while
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison} identifies their algebraic
monads.  The same restricted-Yoneda argument therefore gives the displayed
equivalence.  The mapping formula is its inverse by construction.
\end{proof}

For a coherent affine-etale pair $(U,V)$ write
\[
    F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}
    :=
    \mathcal L_{\mathfrak G,U/V}^{\mathrm{fm,sp}}.
\]

\begin{definition}[Artinian-formalized Spencer controller]
\label{def:as-formalized-spencer-controller}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}|_{U/V}
    :=
    \operatorname{Diff}_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Sp}}
    \left(
        F_{\mathfrak G,U/V}
    \right).
}
\]
Etale transport makes these affine objects a cocartesian section; denote its
global section by
$\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Integral partition-Lie/formalized-Spencer equivalence]
\label{thm:as-global-integral-operation-comparison}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \Xi_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Art}}:
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}.
}
\]
It preserves the anchor, the complete PD operation monad, symmetric actions,
substitution, unit, and all higher coherence.  It is natural for fixed-object
formal-groupoid morphisms and for the spectral-etale varying-object variance
constructed in the preceding chapter.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On every affine-etale pair this is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-affine-spencer-recognition} transported through
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison}.  The construction uses Cech
bar formation on Artinian tests, cotangent base change, finite-Tor
pro-coherent Beck--Chevalley, and restricted Yoneda, all of which commute
with the two-variable etale transport.  Hence the affine equivalences glue.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Raw marked bars remain independent]
The equivalence
$\Xi_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Art}}$ identifies the Artinian cotangent-primitive
operation system of the formalized Artinian deformation theory with the
spectral partition-Lie controller.
It does not identify the raw marked-bar objects with their formalized images
unless the maps
$\Lambda_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}(I)$
have separately been proved invertible for the relevant finite sets $I$.  It also does not identify either
controller with the independent Hopf-derived primitive object
$\mathfrak l_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{sp}}$.
\end{remark}

\section{The canonical filtered tangent enveloping monad}
\label{sec:as-enveloping-weight}

The preceding chapter constructs an anchor-weight filtration of the actual
relative coLie object by placing the anchor source in weight zero and the
square-zero coefficient in weight one.  The filtered cotangent object is
proved there to be connective, almost perfect, and already complete.  We now
use that filtration twice: first to obtain an exhaustive arity filtration on
the partition-Lie endofunctor and then, after slicing at an algebra, to obtain
an additive one-marked filtration on the tangent enveloping monad.  The
finite stages are pieces of one filtered monad; they are not monads
individually.

\subsection{Filtered cotangent--square-zero comonad}

\begin{proposition}[Unrestricted filtered cotangent--square-zero adjunction]
\label{prop:as-filtered-cot-sqz}
In the Day-convolution category of filtered spectral modules and filtered
commutative algebras there is a canonical adjunction
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Fil}}_{B/R}
    \dashv
    \operatorname{sqz}^{\mathrm{Fil}}_{B/R}.
}
\]
It is defined before imposing completeness.  The induced comonad has filtered
counit and comultiplication.  For the anchor-weight objects obtained by
placing $L_{B/R}[-1]$ in weight zero and the square-zero coefficient in weight
one, the filtered cotangent object is connective, almost perfect, and already
complete; evaluation at $F^0$ recovers
$\operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}$ with the anchor-weight
filtration of the preceding chapter.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Structured square-zero formation and the relative cotangent universal
property make sense in the unrestricted filtered Day-convolution category and
give the adjunction exactly as in the unfiltered case.  The filtered
square-zero finiteness theorem of the preceding chapter makes the resulting
cotangent module connective and almost perfect.  Its filtered
almost-perfect completeness theorem then shows that it is already complete.
Thus completion is a proved property of the constructed cotangent object and
is not inserted into the comonad.  Evaluation at filtration weight zero
commutes with the finite square-zero pullback and relative cotangent
formation, giving the ordinary relative coLie object.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Positive anchor-weight normalization]
\label{lem:as-positive-anchor-weight}
For a connective almost-perfect relative derivation
$\alpha:L_{B/R}[-1]\to M$,
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}^0
    \operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(M,\alpha)
    \simeq0,
    \qquad
    \operatorname{Gr}^1
    \operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(M,\alpha)
    \simeq M.
\]
Thus the first nonzero coLie weight is one.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Associated graded kills the positive-weight classifying map and reduces to
the split positively graded square-zero algebra $B\oplus M(1)\to B$.  Its
reduced cotangent object has no weight-zero term, its indecomposable
weight-one term is $M$, and every decomposable term has weight at least two.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Anchor-weight tails are arbitrarily connective]
\label{lem:as-anchor-weight-tail-connectivity}
For every connective almost-perfect relative derivation
$(M,\alpha)$ and every integer $d$, there is $N_d$ such that
\[
\boxed{
    F^n
    \operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(M,\alpha)
    \in
    \operatorname{Mod}_{B,\geq d}
    \qquad
    (n\geq N_d).
}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:as-filtered-cot-sqz}, the anchor-weight coLie object
is a connective almost-perfect filtered $B$-module and $B$ is concentrated in
filtration weight zero.  Apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-aperf-tail-connectivity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Finite weight pieces of the partition-Lie endofunctor]
\label{con:as-finite-anchor-weight-monad}
On dually almost-perfect anchored objects define
\[
\boxed{
    W_{\le r}\operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    :=
    \left(
        F^0\operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}/
        F^{r+2}\operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    \right)^\vee.
}
\]
The shift by two places the unary identity in $W_{\le0}$.  Extend the finite
pieces to all anchored pro-coherent objects by the same relative right-left
sifted-completion construction used for the full partition-Lie monad.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Exhaustivity of the anchor-weight dual filtration]
\label{thm:as-anchor-weight-exhaustive}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname*{colim}_{r}
    W_{\le r}
    \operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}.
}
\]
No separate exhaustivity or pronilpotence hypothesis is imposed.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On a dually almost-perfect anchored generator, the underlying coLie filtered
module is complete and its tails are arbitrarily connective by
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-anchor-weight-tail-connectivity}.  The continuous-dual
lemma for complete almost-perfect filtrations proved in the preceding chapter
therefore gives
\[
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    \left(
        F^0\operatorname{coLie}/
        F^{r+2}\operatorname{coLie}
    \right)^\vee
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \left(
        F^0\operatorname{coLie}
    \right)^\vee.
\]
The right side is the relative partition-Lie endofunctor on the dense
dually almost-perfect sector.  Both sides extend by the same
sifted-colimit/right-left completion relations, so the equivalence extends
uniquely to all anchored pro-coherent objects.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Arity-resolved filtered partition-Lie substitution]
\label{prop:as-filtered-partition-monad}
The $W_{\le r}$ form an exhaustive increasing filtration of the underlying
partition-Lie endofunctor and the monad unit factors through $W_{\le0}$.
If an outer homogeneous operation has arity $m$ and excess $r=m-1$, and the
$m$ inserted inner operations have excesses $s_1,\ldots,s_m$, substitution
has excess
\[
\boxed{
    r+\sum_{i=1}^m s_i.
}
\]
Equivalently, substitution is filtered arity-by-arity.  In particular the
coarse truncations satisfy
\[
\boxed{
    W_{\le r}\operatorname{Lie}
    \circ
    W_{\le s}\operatorname{Lie}
    \longrightarrow
    W_{\le r+(r+1)s}\operatorname{Lie}.
}
\]
No map to $W_{\le r+s}$ is asserted for the full unmarked monad.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Filtered coLie comultiplication is a morphism in the complete filtered
category.  On associated graded the anchor vanishes and the filtration is the
ordinary positive-input/arity filtration of the zero-anchor PD bar
construction.  Substitution of an $m$-ary operation with inner arities
$n_i=s_i+1$ has arity $\sum_i n_i$, hence excess
\[
    \sum_i(s_i+1)-1=(m-1)+\sum_i s_i.
\]
Continuous duality transports this cooperation law to the monad.  The coarse
bound follows from $m\le r+1$ and $s_i\le s$.  Exhaustivity is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-anchor-weight-exhaustive}.
\end{proof}

Fix
\[
    \mathfrak a
    \in
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}.
\]

\begin{definition}[Zero-anchor tangent coefficient]
\label{def:as-zero-anchor-tangent-coefficient}
For $M\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$ write
\[
\boxed{
    z(M)
    :=
    \left(
        M\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1]
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{Anch}^{\mathrm{pc}}_{B/R}.
}
\]
This is the zero-anchor object constructed by the zero-anchor theorem of the
preceding chapter.  It is the tangent direction used in one-marked
operations.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Linearization of a multilinear operation]
\label{lem:as-one-marked-linearization}
Let $\mathcal C$ and $\mathcal D$ be stable presentable $\infty$-categories
and let
\[
    L:\mathcal C^q\longrightarrow\mathcal D
\]
be exact separately in every variable.  Fix $A_1,\ldots,A_q$ and replace any
subset of the inputs by a variable tangent coefficient $M$.  Under
one-variable stabilization at the fixed base point, the summands containing
exactly one occurrence of $M$ survive, while every summand containing at least
two occurrences of $M$ has zero linearization.

Consequently, for a polynomial operation assembled from separately exact
multi-input functors, its tangent at a fixed algebra is the one-marked
operation obtained by placing the tangent coefficient in exactly one input and
the fixed algebra in all remaining inputs.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Exactness in each variable expands the value on a retractive split object
$A\oplus M$ into the finite direct sum of terms indexed by the subsets of
slots occupied by $M$.  A term with $k$ tangent slots is the diagonal of a
reduced $k$-multilinear functor in $M$, with all remaining inputs fixed.
Such a diagonal is $k$-homogeneous in the Goodwillie sense.  One-variable
stabilization is the universal reduced $1$-excisive approximation.  The
$1$-excisive approximation of a reduced $k$-homogeneous functor is zero for
$k\geq2$, whereas a $1$-homogeneous functor is already exact and is unchanged.
Thus precisely the summands with one tangent input survive.  The construction
is natural in the fixed inputs and in maps of multilinear functors.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[One-marked relative operation module]
\label{con:as-tangent-weight}
Let
\[
    T:=\operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
\]
and let $\mathfrak a$ be a $T$-algebra with underlying anchored coefficient
$A=U\mathfrak a$.  Let $\operatorname{Fin}_*^{\simeq}$ denote the groupoid of
finite nonempty pointed sets and pointed bijections.  The multi-input
operation system of Corollary~\ref{cor:as-integral-pd-arity} has a canonical
one-marked derivative.  For $(I,*)\in\operatorname{Fin}_*^{\simeq}$ and
$M\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$, put
\[
\boxed{
    \partial_*T(I;\mathfrak a,M)
    :=
    \operatorname{Op}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    \left(
        I;
        z(M)\text{ in the }*\text{-slot},
        A\text{ in every unmarked slot}
    \right).
}
\]
Pointed bijections act by the symmetric relabelling already constructed for
the operation system.  The PD/pro-coherent assembly takes homotopy fixed
points under the pointed automorphism group in each arity and then the
coproduct over arities.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \partial T_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    :=
    \bigoplus_{[(I,*)]\in\pi_0(\operatorname{Fin}_*^{\simeq})}
    \partial_*T(I;\mathfrak a,M)^{h\operatorname{Aut}(I,*)}.
}
\]
Equivalently, on the standard pointed sets,
\[
\boxed{
    \partial T_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \simeq
    \bigoplus_{n\geq0}
    \operatorname{Op}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    \left(
        \{*,1,\ldots,n\};
        z(M),A,\ldots,A
    \right)^{h\Sigma_n}.
}
\]
More generally, before evaluating the unmarked inputs on $A$, write
$\partial T(-;M)$ for the same one-marked symmetric sequence in the unmarked
anchored variable.  PD substitution in the unmarked slots makes
$\partial T(-;M)$ a right $T$-module, while substitution in the unique marked
slot composes one-marked operations.  Separate exactness in the marked
coefficient variable follows from
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-operation-separate-exactness}, since homotopy fixed
points are exact in the stable target.

Use the $T$-algebra structure $T(A)\to A$ of $\mathfrak a$ to form the
relative monadic bar
\[
    \operatorname{Bar}_\bullet
    (\partial T,T,\mathfrak a;M),
\]
with
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Bar}_n
    (\partial T,T,\mathfrak a;M)
    :=
    \partial T(T^nA;M),
    \qquad n\geq0.
}
\]
The nonterminal faces use multiplication of $T$, the terminal face evaluates
the final unmarked $T$ by the algebra structure of $\mathfrak a$, and the
degeneracies use the monad unit.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk}}(M)
    :=
    \left|
        \operatorname{Bar}_\bullet
        (\partial T,T,\mathfrak a;M)
    \right|.
}
\]
Grafting at the unique marked slot commutes with the right-$T$ bar relations
and therefore induces a monad structure on
$\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk}}$.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[One-marked construction of the tangent enveloping monad]
\label{thm:as-one-marked-tangent-identification}
There is a canonical equivalence of sifted-colimit-preserving monads
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk}}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}.
}
\]
Under this equivalence, a tangent representation is exactly a coherent action
of every partition-Lie operation containing one coefficient input and
arbitrarily many inputs evaluated on $\mathfrak a$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Work first on the dense dually almost-perfect anchored sector on which the
multi-input operation system is obtained by continuous duality from the
multiweight coLie cooperations, and first truncate to a finite arity stage of
the exhaustive anchor-weight filtration.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-operation-separate-exactness}, every labelled arity
functor is exact separately in the tangent coefficient.  A spectrum object in
the slice
\[
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}/\mathfrak a
\]
has, under the fibre-at-$A$ equivalence in the stabilized underlying anchored
slice, tangent coefficient $M$.  Expanding an arity-$n$ operation on the
corresponding retractive object separates the terms by the subset of slots
occupied by $M$.  The symmetric group permutes these subset summands.  The
orbit of the summands with exactly one tangent slot is induced from the
stabilizer of a chosen marked slot, so the Shapiro equivalence for homotopy
fixed points identifies its $h\Sigma_n$-term with
\[
    \operatorname{Op}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}
    \left(
        \{*,1,\ldots,n-1\};z(M),A,\ldots,A
    \right)^{h\Sigma_{n-1}}.
\]
These are exactly the arity summands of
$\partial T_{\mathfrak a}(M)$.

Lemma~\ref{lem:as-one-marked-linearization} shows that stabilization kills
all orbits of terms containing two or more tangent inputs.  The surviving
single-tangent input carries the zero anchor $z(M)$ because it is the fibre in
the anchored slice.  Thus every finite arity stage linearizes to the
corresponding finite stage of the coherent one-marked operation system.  The
anchor-weight filtration is exhaustive, and stabilization and the tangent
free--forgetful construction preserve the resulting filtered colimit, so the
same identification holds for the full operation system of
Construction~\ref{con:as-tangent-weight}.

Substitution among the unmarked inputs is evaluated by the fixed $T$-algebra
structure of $\mathfrak a$.  The relative right-$T$ bar with terms
$\partial T(T^nA;M)$ imposes precisely those relations, while grafting in the
marked slot gives composition of coefficient operations.  Therefore there is
an equivalence over
$\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$
\[
    \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk}}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \left(
        \operatorname{LieAlgd}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}/\mathfrak a
    \right).
\]
Both sides preserve the right-left completion relations used to extend the
partition-Lie monad from the dense sector, so the equivalence extends to all
pro-coherent tangent coefficients.

Theorem~\ref{thm:as-tangent-monadicity} identifies the right-hand side with
the Eilenberg--Moore category of $\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}$ through the
same forgetful functor.  Uniqueness of the Eilenberg--Moore monad gives the
displayed monad equivalence, including unit and multiplication.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Additive one-marked tangent filtration]
\label{con:as-one-marked-tangent-filtration}
Use the rooted-tree expansion of the relative bar in
Construction~\ref{con:as-tangent-weight}.  Every tree has one distinguished
external coefficient leaf and finitely many unmarked external leaves evaluated
on $\mathfrak a$.  Give a tree weight equal to the number of those unmarked
external leaves.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    F_{\leq r}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M)
}
\]
through the equivalence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-one-marked-tangent-identification} as the realization of
the sub-bar spanned by trees of weight at most $r$.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Filtered tangent enveloping monad]
\label{thm:as-filtered-enveloping-monad}
The functors $F_{\leq r}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}$ form a canonical
exhaustive increasing filtration
\[
    F_{\leq0}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
    \longrightarrow
    F_{\leq1}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
    \longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi},
\]
with
\[
\boxed{
    F_{\leq0}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M)\simeq M.
}
\]
Grafting through the unique marked coefficient slot adds the unmarked
$\mathfrak a$-leaf weights, giving canonical coherent multiplication maps
\[
\boxed{
    F_{\leq r}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
    \circ
    F_{\leq s}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
    \longrightarrow
    F_{\leq r+s}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}.
}
\]
The first positive graded piece is the universal anchored first-order action of
the underlying coefficient of $\mathfrak a$ on $M$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A weight-zero one-marked tree has no unmarked $\mathfrak a$-leaf.  After the
unit relations in the relative bar are imposed, the only such operation is the
unary identity on the coefficient, giving
$F_{\leq0}\simeq\operatorname{id}$.  Grafting one marked tree into the marked
leaf of another does not identify or duplicate any unmarked external leaf, so
weights add exactly.  Associativity and unitality of the filtered maps are the
associativity and unit coherences of PD substitution transported through the
relative bar.

Every tree occurring in the relative bar is finite and hence has finite
unmarked weight.  Therefore the full relative bar is the filtered colimit of
its finite-weight sub-bars.  Realization commutes with this filtered colimit,
so
\[
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    F_{\leq r}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}.
\]
A weight-one tree has exactly one unmarked algebra input, so its quotient over
weight zero is the universal anchored first-order coefficient action.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Spectral connection on a coefficient]
\label{def:as-spectral-connection}
For $M\in\operatorname{QC}_B^\vee$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M)
    :=
    \operatorname{Map}_{M/}
    \left(
        F_{\le1}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M),
        M
    \right),
}
\]
where the restriction along
$M=F_{\le0}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M)$ is the identity.  A connection is
therefore a point of a constructed mapping space; it is not part of the
algebroid data.
\end{definition}

On a represented coherent local quotient chart
\[
    q:\operatorname{Spec}B\longrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}A
\]
over $\operatorname{Spec}R$, let $F_q\in\operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{B/R}$
be the represented quotient formal leaf and put
\[
    \mathfrak a_q
    :=
    \operatorname{Diff}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}(F_q).
\]
Write $P^1_{A/B}(N)$ for the first-principal-parts object of the corresponding
first structured neighbourhood.

\begin{proposition}[Comparison with represented first-principal-parts connections]
\label{prop:as-connection-principal-parts}
On the preceding represented coherent affine quotient chart, let
$N\in\operatorname{APerf}(B)_{\ge0}$ and put $M=N^\vee$.  There is a canonical
equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak a_q}^{\pi}(N^\vee)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(B)_{/N}}
    \left(N,P^1_{A/B}(N)\right).
}
\]
Equivalently, spectral first-weight connections for the represented quotient
controller are exactly splittings of
\[
    L_{B/A}\otimes_BN
    \longrightarrow
    P^1_{A/B}(N)
    \longrightarrow
    N.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $B_q^{(1)}\to B$ be the affine algebra of the first structured
self-relation of $q$.  The represented first-neighbourhood theorem identifies
it as the structured square-zero extension whose fibre is $L_{B/A}$ and whose
source--target difference is the universal $A$-relative derivation.  Hence
\[
    P^1_{A/B}(N)
    \simeq
    B_q^{(1)}\otimes_{B,t}N
\]
and there is the canonical fibre sequence
\[
    L_{B/A}\otimes_BN
    \longrightarrow
    P^1_{A/B}(N)
    \longrightarrow
    N.
\]
A section of the last map is therefore exactly a lift of the coefficient
$N$ through the represented first structured self-relation.

We compare this lifting problem before applying any ordinary duality to the
principal-parts extension.  The affine formal-moduli/partition-Lie
equivalence of the preceding chapter is characterized on structured
square-zero tests: a square-zero coefficient is carried to the corresponding
free anchored partition-Lie cell, with the attaching morphism recording its
anchor.  Slice that equivalence over the represented quotient formal leaf
$F_q$ and stabilize.  The resulting tangent equivalence sends the universal
one-stage coefficient-lifting problem for $q$ to the free one-marked tangent
cell with coefficient $N^\vee$.

On the nonlinear side, the represented first-neighbourhood theorem evaluates
that universal one-stage lifting problem as
\[
    N\longrightarrow
    B_q^{(1)}\otimes_{B,t}N=P^1_{A/B}(N)
\]
over $N$.  Hence its space of fillers is
\[
    \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QCoh}(B)_{/N}}
    \left(N,P^1_{A/B}(N)\right).
\]
On the differentiated side, the represented first-order comparison
identifies the anchor of $\mathfrak a_q$ with the continuous pro-coherent
dual of cotangent transitivity
\[
    L_{B/R}[-1]\longrightarrow L_{B/A}[-1].
\]
The one-marked linearization theorem identifies the corresponding free
one-stage tangent cell with
$F_{\le1}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a_q}^{\pi}(N^\vee)$, and a filler is precisely
a retraction of its unit over $N^\vee$, namely a point of
\[
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak a_q}^{\pi}(N^\vee).
\]

The sliced tangent differentiation equivalence is fully faithful and carries
the square-zero/free comparison, its anchor, and its unit to one another.
It therefore identifies these two filler spaces canonically.  This proves the
displayed equivalence.  No perfectness of $L_{B/A}$ and no dualization of the
whole principal-parts extension is used.
\end{proof}

\section{Curvature and Bianchi from the constructed weight obstruction tower}
\label{sec:as-curvature}

The obstruction theory is formed from the one-marked relative operation
system before a full coefficient action is chosen.  We use reduced rooted
trees, with unary unit vertices contracted from the outset.  The only
positive-weight vertices which require new coefficient-action data are the
vertices on the distinguished marked path.  In a proper weight-$r$
decomposition every such marked-path vertex has positive weight strictly
smaller than $r$.  Unmarked algebroid vertices may carry all $r$ unmarked
leaves; they are evaluated by the already fixed $T$-algebra structure of
$\mathfrak a$.

\begin{definition}[Reduced one-marked rooted-tree category]
\label{def:as-marked-rooted-tree-category}
For $r\geq1$, let $\mathsf{Tree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_r$ be the small category of
finite rooted trees with one distinguished external leaf labelled by the
coefficient variable $M$ and exactly $r$ unmarked external leaves evaluated on
$\mathfrak a$.  Unary vertices representing the monad unit are contracted.
Vertices on the marked path are decorated by one-marked operation functors;
vertices disjoint from the marked path are decorated by unmarked operations.
Morphisms are generated by contractions of internal edges, represented by PD
substitution.

Let $c_r$ be the one-vertex marked corolla of weight $r$.  Let
\[
    \partial\mathsf{Tree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_r
    \subset
    (\mathsf{Tree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_r)_{/c_r}
\]
be the punctured latching category obtained by deleting the identity
$c_r\to c_r$.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Corolla and rooted-tree latching object]
\label{con:as-preaction-rooted-bar}
For a reduced marked tree $\tau$, evaluate its marked external leaf on
$z(M)$, its unmarked leaves on the underlying anchored coefficient
$U\mathfrak a$, and its vertices on the corresponding one-marked or unmarked
multi-input operations.  This gives
\[
    E_\tau(\mathfrak a;M)\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B
\]
functorial under internal-edge contraction.

Define
\[
\boxed{
    C_r(\mathfrak a;M):=E_{c_r}(\mathfrak a;M)
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
    L_r(\mathfrak a;M)
    :=
    \operatorname*{colim}_{\tau\in
      \partial\mathsf{Tree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_r}
    E_\tau(\mathfrak a;M).
}
\]
Contracting every internal edge of a proper tree to the corolla gives
\[
\boxed{
    \lambda_r:
    L_r(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow
    C_r(\mathfrak a;M).
}
\]
Every positive-weight \emph{coefficient-action vertex on the marked path} of a
proper reduced weight-$r$ tree has weight strictly smaller than $r$.
Unmarked vertices are evaluated by the fixed algebra $\mathfrak a$ and are not
subject to this inequality.
\end{construction}

\begin{definition}[Weight relation object]
\label{def:as-weight-relations}
For $r\geq2$ define
\[
\boxed{
    Q_r^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(
        L_r(\mathfrak a;M)
        \xrightarrow{\lambda_r}
        C_r(\mathfrak a;M)
    \right)
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal R_r^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    :=
    Q_r^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)[-1].
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Partial representation spaces]
\label{def:as-partial-representation-space}
Let $\operatorname{Act}_{\le r}(\mathfrak a;M)$ be the space of one-marked
operation maps of unmarked weight at most $r$ which restrict to the identity
in weight zero and satisfy every unit and PD-substitution relation of total
unmarked weight at most $r$.  Equivalently, through
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-one-marked-tangent-identification}, it is the space of
finite-weight algebra actions for the filtered tangent monad.  Restriction
gives
\[
    \cdots
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Act}_{\le2}
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Act}_{\le1},
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Act}_{\le1}(\mathfrak a;M)
    =
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Corolla--latching extension theorem]
\label{thm:as-corolla-latching-extension}
Assume a partial action has been constructed through weight $r-1$, with
$r\ge2$.  Every proper reduced weight-$r$ tree is canonically evaluable by the
already constructed lower-weight marked-path actions together with the fixed
$T$-algebra structure of $\mathfrak a$.  These evaluations assemble to
\[
\boxed{
    g_r:
    L_r(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow M.
}
\]
The space of extensions through weight $r$ is canonically
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{fib}_{g_r}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Map}(C_r(\mathfrak a;M),M)
        \longrightarrow
        \operatorname{Map}(L_r(\mathfrak a;M),M)
    \right).
}
\]
Equivalently, the cofiber sequence
\[
    L_r\longrightarrow C_r\longrightarrow Q_r^\pi
\]
defines the canonical obstruction
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{ob}_r:
    \mathcal R_r^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow M,
}
\]
and extension through weight $r$ is equivalent to a specified nullhomotopy of
$\operatorname{ob}_r$ together with the corresponding corolla filler.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
A weight-$r$ representation relation has exactly $r$ unmarked external inputs.
The only new indecomposable coefficient-action datum at that weight is the
weight-$r$ marked corolla.  Every proper decomposition is represented by a
proper reduced tree.  Along the marked path, every positive-weight
coefficient-action vertex has weight smaller than $r$ and is therefore
already evaluable; every vertex away from the marked path is evaluated by the
fixed algebra structure of $\mathfrak a$, regardless of its weight.
Compatibility of the partial action with the lower-weight substitution
relations makes the evaluations compatible under further contractions, so
the colimit universal property gives $g_r$.

An extension through weight $r$ is exactly a map $C_r\to M$ restricting to
$g_r$ on every proper decomposition.  This is the displayed homotopy fibre.
Applying $\operatorname{Map}(-,M)$ to
$L_r\to C_r\to Q_r^\pi$ identifies the same fibre with the space of
nullhomotopies of the connecting morphism $Q_r^\pi[-1]\to M$ together with
the corresponding lift.  That connecting morphism is
$\operatorname{ob}_r$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Connection evaluation on the binary relation]
\label{prop:as-connection-binary-relation}
A connection
$\nabla\in\operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M)$ evaluates the
weight-two punctured latching category and therefore gives
\[
    g_2:L_2(\mathfrak a;M)\longrightarrow M.
\]
The cofiber sequence
$L_2\to C_2\to Q_2^\pi$ determines
\[
\boxed{
    \partial_\nabla^{(2)}:
    \mathcal R_2^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow M.
}
\]
Its latching faces are the two composites of first-weight coefficient actions,
the first-weight action of the binary algebroid operation, and the relative
anchor face.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Every marked-path coefficient-action vertex in a proper reduced weight-two
tree has weight one, so it is evaluated by $\nabla$; every unmarked vertex is
evaluated by the fixed algebra structure of $\mathfrak a$.  The lower-weight
identities identify the values on common contractions.  Apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-corolla-latching-extension} with $r=2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Spectral curvature]
\label{def:as-complete-curvature}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal F_\nabla
    :=
    \partial_\nabla^{(2)}:
    \mathcal R_2^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow M.
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Flatness by weight extension]
\label{thm:as-flatness-criterion}
For a connection $\nabla$:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item a lift to
    $\operatorname{Act}_{\le2}(\mathfrak a;M)$ is equivalent to a coherent
    nullhomotopy of $\mathcal F_\nabla$ together with the corresponding
    weight-two corolla filler;
    \item after a partial action has been constructed through weight $r-1$,
    extension through weight $r$ is equivalent to a coherent nullhomotopy of
    \[
        \operatorname{ob}_r:
        \mathcal R_r^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)\longrightarrow M
    \]
    together with the corresponding filler;
    \item there is a canonical equivalence
    \[
    \boxed{
        \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}}(M)
        \simeq
        \lim_r\operatorname{Act}_{\le r}(\mathfrak a;M).
    }
    \]
\end{enumerate}
Thus flatness is the existence of a compatible system of constructed fillers;
it is not part of the definition of a connection.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first two assertions are
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-corolla-latching-extension}.  For the final assertion,
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-filtered-enveloping-monad} gives
\[
    \mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M)
    \simeq
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    F_{\le r}\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}(M).
\]
Every unit or multiplication relation has finite unmarked weight.  A full
algebra structure is therefore exactly a compatible system of finite-weight
action maps satisfying those finite relations.  Taking the limit of the
corresponding action spaces gives the displayed equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Universal monad-defect syzygy and the Bianchi operator]
\label{con:as-bianchi-boundary}
Write
\[
    T:=\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}
\]
for the tangent monad.  For an arbitrary unital candidate action
$a:TM\to M$, not assumed associative, define its monad-associativity defect
\[
\boxed{
    F_a
    :=
    a\,T(a)-a\,\mu_M:
    T^2M\longrightarrow M.
}
\]
There is a canonical coherent nullhomotopy on $T^3M$
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \mathfrak B_a
    :={}&
    a\,T(F_a)
    -F_a\,\mu_{TM}
    +F_a\,T(\mu_M)
    -F_a\,T^2(a)
    \simeq0.
\end{aligned}}
\]
Indeed, after expanding $F_a$, the first and fourth nested-action terms cancel,
as do the two terms containing $aT(a)T(\mu_M)$.  The remaining terms cancel
by monad associativity
$\mu_M\mu_{TM}\simeq\mu_MT(\mu_M)$ and naturality
$T(a)\mu_{TM}\simeq\mu_MT^2(a)$.  The monad coherence supplies the specified
homotopy and its higher compatibility.

We now extract this identity at a partial action without choosing a
weight-$r$ filler.  Let a partial action be given through weight $r-1$.  Let
\[
    \mathsf{BTree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_{r+1;r}
\]
be the category of reduced three-stage one-marked rooted trees of total
unmarked weight $r+1$ together with a distinguished connected two-stage
marked subtree of total weight $r$.  Unit vertices are already contracted.
At the distinguished subtree do not choose a corolla action.  Instead apply
the exact one-marked operation functors to the cofiber sequence
\[
    L_r(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow
    C_r(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow
    Q_r^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
\]
and use its shifted cofiber
$\mathcal R_r^\pi=Q_r^\pi[-1]$ as the distinguished relation input.  Every
remaining marked-path vertex has positive weight $<r$ and is evaluated by the
partial action; every unmarked algebroid vertex is evaluated by the fixed
$T$-algebra structure of $\mathfrak a$.  Exactness in the distinguished input
makes this construction independent of a representative of the cofiber
class and makes it functorial under all contractions that preserve the
distinguished relation.  Write
\[
    E^{\mathrm{def}}_\tau(\mathfrak a;M)
\]
for the resulting stable diagram and define the one-defect Bianchi object
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal B_{r+1}^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    :=
    \operatorname*{colim}_{
      \tau\in\mathsf{BTree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_{r+1;r}}
    E^{\mathrm{def}}_\tau(\mathfrak a;M).
}
\]
The four ways of contracting the three-stage tree through the distinguished
relation are exactly the four placements of the defect in
$\mathfrak B_a$.  Evaluating that distinguished relation by
$\operatorname{ob}_r:\mathcal R_r^\pi\to M$ and the other vertices by the
partial action therefore gives four canonical maps out of
$\mathcal B_{r+1}^\pi$.  Their alternating sum defines
\[
\boxed{
    d_{a_{<r}}\operatorname{ob}_r:
    \mathcal B_{r+1}^\pi(\mathfrak a;M)
    \longrightarrow M.
}
\]
No weight-$r$ corolla filler enters this definition.  For $r=2$ and
$a_{<2}=\nabla$ write
\[
    d_\nabla\mathcal F_\nabla
    :=
    d_{\nabla}\operatorname{ob}_2.
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Bianchi coherence]
\label{thm:as-bianchi}
For every connection $\nabla$, without a flatness assumption, there is a
canonical nullhomotopy
\[
\boxed{
    d_\nabla\mathcal F_\nabla\simeq0.
}
\]
More generally, after a partial action through weight $r-1$,
\[
\boxed{
    d_{a_{<r}}\operatorname{ob}_r\simeq0.
}
\]
The higher monad coherences on $T^4M,T^5M,\ldots$ give the coherent higher
Bianchi hierarchy.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The four maps defining
$d_{a_{<r}}\operatorname{ob}_r$ are the total-weight $r+1$ component of the
four terms in the universal monad-defect expression $\mathfrak B_a$, with the
unique unresolved weight-$r$ relation replaced by its cofiber class
$\mathcal R_r^\pi$.  Exactness of the remaining one-marked contexts carries
the canonical nullhomotopy $\mathfrak B_a\simeq0$ through that cofiber.  All
other positive marked-path vertices have weight $<r$, so their relations
vanish by the defining coherence of the partial action.  Consequently the
four-term nullhomotopy descends to
$d_{a_{<r}}\operatorname{ob}_r\simeq0$ without any weight-$r$ filler.  For
$r=2$ the distinguished relation is the curvature relation, so
$\operatorname{ob}_2=\mathcal F_\nabla$.  Higher coherence is the corresponding
restriction of the higher associativity coherences of the monad to iterated
one-defect tree diagrams.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The parallel obstruction tower for morphisms}

Let $\mathfrak b$ and $\mathfrak a$ be spectral partition-Lie algebroids over
the same coherent affine base and let
\[
    h:U\mathfrak b\longrightarrow U\mathfrak a
\]
be a morphism of their underlying anchored pro-coherent objects.

\begin{construction}[Morphism corolla and latching relation]
\label{con:as-morphism-latching}
Replace the distinguished coefficient leaf of
$\mathsf{Tree}^{\mathrm{mk,red}}_r$ by a distinguished internal morphism edge
labelled by $h$.  Source-side vertices are evaluated by the operation system of
$\mathfrak b$, target-side vertices by that of $\mathfrak a$, and contraction
across the distinguished edge uses $h$.  Denote the weight-$r$ corolla and
punctured latching objects by
\[
    C_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h),
    \qquad
    L_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h),
\]
with contraction map
\[
    \lambda_r^{\mathrm{mor}}:
    L_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
    \longrightarrow
    C_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h).
\]
Put
\[
\boxed{
    Q_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}(\lambda_r^{\mathrm{mor}}),
    \qquad
    \mathcal R_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
    :=
    Q_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)[-1].
}
\]
In a proper morphism tree, every vertex at which new morphism compatibility is
required has smaller positive weight; arbitrary source- and target-algebra
vertices are evaluated by the already fixed algebra structures.
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Universal morphism-defect syzygy]
\label{con:as-morphism-defect-syzygy}
Let $T=\operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}$, and write
\[
    a:T(U\mathfrak b)\to U\mathfrak b,
    \qquad
    b:T(U\mathfrak a)\to U\mathfrak a
\]
for the two full algebra structures.  Define the morphism defect of the
underlying anchored map $h$ by
\[
\boxed{
    K_h
    :=
    h\,a-b\,T(h):
    T(U\mathfrak b)\longrightarrow U\mathfrak a.
}
\]
There is a canonical coherent nullhomotopy on $T^2(U\mathfrak b)$
\[
\boxed{
    K_h\,\mu
    -K_h\,T(a)
    -b\,T(K_h)
    \simeq0.
}
\]
After expansion, the source algebra law cancels
$h(a\mu-aT(a))$, while naturality of $\mu$ and the target algebra law cancel
the remaining two terms.  This identity is the morphism analogue of the
monad-defect Bianchi syzygy.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Weight obstruction tower for algebroid morphisms]
\label{prop:as-morphism-obstruction-tower}
There is a canonical tower
\[
    \cdots\to
    \operatorname{MorAct}_{\le2}(h)
    \to
    \operatorname{MorAct}_{\le1}(h),
\]
where $\operatorname{MorAct}_{\le r}(h)$ is the space of coherent morphism
relations through weight $r$ extending the underlying anchored map $h$.  A
partial morphism through weight $r-1$ determines
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{ob}^{\mathrm{mor}}_r(h):
    \mathcal R_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
    \longrightarrow
    U\mathfrak a.
}
\]
Extension through weight $r$ is equivalent to a coherent nullhomotopy of this
obstruction together with the corresponding corolla filler.  The total-weight
$r+1$ part of the universal morphism-defect syzygy gives its canonical Bianchi
cocycle.

In weight two, after rational dg rectification, the obstruction is
\[
    [h(x),h(y)]-h([x,y]),
\]
with the anchor compatibility already fixed because $h$ is an anchored map.
If the composite of $h$ with a morphism of target algebroids is already a full
algebroid morphism, every obstruction maps canonically to zero and therefore
factors through the corresponding homotopy fibre.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The latching argument is the same as for coefficient actions: vertices where
new morphism compatibility occurs have smaller positive weight, while all
source- and target-algebra vertices are evaluated by their fixed algebra
structures.  Hence a partial morphism through weight $r-1$ evaluates the
punctured latching diagram.  Mapping the cofiber sequence
\[
    L_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
    \longrightarrow
    C_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
    \longrightarrow
    Q_r^{\mathrm{mor}}(h)
\]
into $U\mathfrak a$ gives the obstruction and filler description.

The weight-$r$ component of $K_h$ is exactly this latching obstruction: it is
the difference between applying $h$ after the source operation and applying
the target operation after $h$ in every input.  Taking total weight $r+1$ in
the identity of
Construction~\ref{con:as-morphism-defect-syzygy}, and quotienting the
lower-weight defects which vanish for a partial morphism through $r-1$, gives
the canonical Bianchi nullhomotopy.  Rational rectification identifies the
weight-two defect with the displayed bracket difference.  Finally,
postcomposition is functorial in $K_h$; if the postcomposite is a full
algebroid morphism, its defect is zero, so the universal property of the
homotopy fibre gives the asserted factorization.
\end{proof}

\section{Arity two and the nonlinear isotropy commutator}
\label{sec:as-binary-commutator}

The formal-groupoid chapter constructed
\[
    \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}:
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}
    \times_X
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}
    \longrightarrow
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}},
    \qquad
    \operatorname{Comm}(g,h)=ghg^{-1}h^{-1},
\]
and the preceding chapter independently differentiated the one-object formal
isotropy groupoid
\[
    \mathfrak I_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}
    :=
    B_\bullet I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}
\]
to
$\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$.

\begin{lemma}[Strong monoidality of reduced fibrewise stabilization]
\label{lem:as-reduced-stabilization-monoidal}
For pointed objects $Y,Z$ over $X$ there is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \widetilde\Sigma_X^\infty(Y\wedge_XZ)
    \simeq
    \widetilde\Sigma_X^\infty Y
    \otimes_X
    \widetilde\Sigma_X^\infty Z.
}
\]
It is natural, symmetric, associative, unital, and compatible with arbitrary
base change.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The tensor product in $\mathcal E_X$ is exact separately in each variable.
The tensor product of the defining cofiber sequences is the total cofiber of
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
\mathbb S_X \arrow[r] \arrow[d] &
\Sigma^\infty_{+,X}Y \arrow[d]\\
\Sigma^\infty_{+,X}Z \arrow[r] &
\Sigma^\infty_{+,X}(Y\times_XZ).
\end{tikzcd}
\]
The corresponding pointed quotient is
$(Y\times_XZ)/(Y\vee_XZ)=Y\wedge_XZ$.  Symmetry, associativity, and base
change follow from those of fibrewise smash, tensor product, and exact
pullback.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Bi-reduced nonlinear commutator]
\label{con:as-binary-commutator-linearization}
The identities
$\operatorname{Comm}(g,1)\simeq1$ and
$\operatorname{Comm}(1,h)\simeq1$ give a pointed factorization
\[
    \overline{\operatorname{Comm}}_{\mathfrak G}:
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}
    \wedge_X
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}
    \longrightarrow
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}.
\]
Reduced stabilization gives the raw stable shadow
\[
\boxed{
    [-,-]_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,raw}}:
    \mathbb I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}}
    \otimes
    \mathbb I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}}.
}
\]
No bilinearity or Lie identity is asserted for this whole stable shadow.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[First differential of inversion]
\label{lem:as-inversion-first-differential}
On every represented first structured identity coefficient of a formal group
object,
\[
\boxed{
    d(\operatorname{inv})_e=-\operatorname{id}.
}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The composite
\[
    I^{\mathrm{for}}
    \xrightarrow{(\operatorname{id},\operatorname{inv})}
    I^{\mathrm{for}}\times_XI^{\mathrm{for}}
    \xrightarrow{m}
    I^{\mathrm{for}}
\]
is the unit.  The first structured differential of multiplication at the
identity is addition of the two first-order coefficients.  Differentiating
the identity gives
$\operatorname{id}+d(\operatorname{inv})_e=0$.
\end{proof}

The manuscript uses the bracket obtained from right-invariant vector fields.
For a smooth group this is the opposite of the usual left-invariant matrix
bracket.  The nonlinear commutator above has the left-invariant first mixed
sign.  The comparison must therefore include the already constructed
inversion morphism rather than hide this sign in terminology.

\begin{definition}[Right-normalized nonlinear commutator]
\label{def:as-right-normalized-commutator}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{ri}}
    :=
    \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}
    \circ
    (\operatorname{inv}\times\operatorname{id}).
}
\]
It is again canonically trivial when either input is the identity and
therefore has a bi-reduced pointed factorization.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Directional mixed square-zero commutator coefficient]
\label{con:as-mixed-square-zero-commutator}
Work on a coherent affine identity chart of the formal-isotropy groupoid.  Let
$M$ and $N$ be two connective square-zero directions and form the directional
bi-cube
\[
    A_{\varnothing}=B,
    \qquad
    A_M,
    \qquad
    A_N,
    \qquad
    A_{M,N},
\]
by
Construction~\ref{con:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison}.  The formalized
right-normalized commutator is functorial on Artinian points, so it gives a
morphism of these four-point diagrams.  Take the total fibre in the two
square-zero directions, pass to the exact bidegree $(1,1)$ scalar diagonal,
and apply the comonadic scalar-diagonal Hurewicz comparison.  The resulting
cotangent coefficient is
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}_{(1,1)}
    L_{B/(B\otimes_{A_{M,N}}B)}[-2]
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    (\{1,2\};M,N).
\]

For the continuously dualizable coefficient underlying differentiated actual
isotropy, this procedure gives a binary cotangent cooperation
\[
\boxed{
    d_{\mathrm{cot}}^{(1,1)}
    \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{ri,fm}}.
}
\]
On the dense dually almost-perfect sector its continuous dual is denoted
\[
\boxed{
    d_{\pi}^{(1,1)}
    \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{ri,fm}}
    :=
    \left(
        d_{\mathrm{cot}}^{(1,1)}
        \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{ri,fm}}
    \right)^\vee.
}
\]
The right-left pro-coherent extension defines the same operation on the full
coefficient sector.  No support-collapse cotangent cube is used.
\end{construction}

\begin{lemma}[Right-normalized mixed commutator sign]
\label{lem:as-commutator-marked-bar}
For a represented first identity coefficient, the exact directional bidegree
$(1,1)$ of the ordinary commutator is the alternating ordered product
$gh-hg$ in the left-invariant convention.  Precomposition in the first input
by inversion changes its sign.  Consequently
\[
\boxed{
    d_{\mathrm{cot}}^{(1,1)}
    \operatorname{Comm}^{\mathrm{ri}}
    =
    -
    d_{\mathrm{cot}}^{(1,1)}
    \operatorname{Comm}
}
\]
under the first-order additive identification, and the right-normalized sign
is the right-invariant convention fixed in the preceding chapter.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The directional bi-cross-effect kills every term depending on only one of the
two square-zero directions.  In exact bidegree $(1,1)$ the four-factor
expression $ghg^{-1}h^{-1}$ therefore retains the difference of the two
ordered mixed products.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-inversion-first-differential}, precomposition in the first
variable by inversion acts as $-\operatorname{id}$ on the first identity
coefficient.  The mixed cotangent cooperation changes sign.  For a matrix
group the ordinary commutator derivative is $XY-YX$, whereas the commutator
of the right-invariant fields $X^R(g)=Xg$ and $Y^R(g)=Yg$ is
$(YX-XY)g$, exactly the resulting convention.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Integral arity-two commutator comparison]
\label{thm:as-arity-two-comparison}
Apply quotient formalization to the one-object formal-isotropy groupoid
$\mathfrak I_{\mathfrak G,\bullet}$.  On every coherent spectral Artinian leaf
of its formalized quotient, directional bi-square-zero differentiation of the
right-normalized commutator gives the binary cotangent cooperation whose
continuous dual is the binary operation of differentiated actual isotropy:
\[
\boxed{
    d_{\pi}^{(1,1)}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{ri,fm}}
    \right)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    [-,-]_{\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}}^{\pi}.
}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\boxed{
    \left(
        d_{\mathrm{cot}}^{(1,1)}
        \operatorname{Comm}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{ri,fm}}
    \right)^\vee
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    [-,-]_{\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}}^{\pi}.
}
\]
The comparison is integral.  The raw stable map
$[-,-]_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,raw}}$ remains a separate stable shadow; no
identification of its coefficient with
$\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$ is asserted.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Formalization is functorial for multiplication and inversion and hence for
$\operatorname{Comm}^{\mathrm{ri}}$.  Evaluate it on the four directional
Artinian tests
$B,A_M,A_N,A_{M,N}$.  The total fibre is the genuine two-variable
square-zero cross-effect.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}, its all-positive
piece is the directional bidegree of the source-fixed Cech primitive, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-directional-diagonal-meeting} passes canonically to
the exact scalar diagonal.  The comonadic Hurewicz comparison and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-cotbar-totalization} identify the resulting exact
cotangent coefficient with
\[
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(\{1,2\};M,N).
\]
The coSp--coLie equivalence identifies this cooperation with the binary
cooperation of $\operatorname{coLie}^{\pi,E_\infty}$ for the differentiated
actual-isotropy formal-moduli problem.  Continuous pro-coherent duality turns
that cooperation into the binary spectral partition-Lie operation.  The sign
is the right-invariant sign of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-commutator-marked-bar}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Direct geometric comparison when the discrepancy vanishes]
\label{cor:as-arity-two-discrepancy-zero}
If
\[
    \mathbb C_{\mathfrak I_{\mathfrak G}}^{\mathrm{op,Art}}
    (\{1,2\})
    \simeq0,
\]
then the raw and geometric-formalized two-marked commutator cells are
equivalent.  In particular this holds when the effective quotient of the
formal-isotropy groupoid is represented.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Actual isotropy remains distinct from the anchor fibre]
The theorem does not alter
\[
    \gamma_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}:
    \mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb F_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}.
\]
The binary operation belongs to actual differentiated isotropy and transports
to the anchor fibre only when this independently constructed comparison is an
equivalence.
\end{remark}

\section{Anchor--Leibniz compatibility and higher coherence}
\label{sec:as-leibniz-jacobi}

\begin{theorem}[Integral anchored Spencer compatibility]
\label{thm:as-integral-leibniz}
The Artinian Spencer monad is a relative anchored monad over
\[
    \mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1],
\]
and
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison} identifies its anchor with the
anchor of $\operatorname{Lie}^{\pi,E_\infty}_{B/R}$.  The directional
multi-filtered cotangent primitive construction preserves the source--target
contribution which produces that anchor.  The support-marked geometric branch
is not needed for this anchored algebraic statement.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On an Artinian cell $A\to B$, the first-order difference between target and
source scalar multiplication is classified by cotangent transitivity
\[
    L_{B/R}[-1]
    \longrightarrow
    L_{B/A}[-1].
\]
This is the anchor of $\operatorname{cot}_{B/R}(A)$ and, by the
bar--cotangent theorem, also the anchor of the normalized Cech cotangent
primitive.  The multi-filtered construction keeps the source in weight zero
and all operation inputs in positive weights, so every higher multiweight
cooperation is formed in the relative derivation category over the same
source $L_{B/R}[-1]$.  Consequently the PD cooperation substitution of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-multiweight-pd-substitution} is a relative anchored
cooperation system.  Continuous duality gives the anchored Spencer monad,
and Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-monad-comparison} identifies it with the
spectral partition-Lie anchor.

The formalized relation map preserves source, target, unit, and composition.
Definitions~\ref{def:as-cotangent-marked-primitive} and~\ref{con:as-multi-filtered-colie} therefore carry the source--target face to
the same transitivity anchor.  No assertion about the decomposable part of
the reduced scalar cross-effect is required.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Graded Leibniz formula after dg rectification]
\label{cor:as-spencer-leibniz}
On the represented rational dg locus, for homogeneous local sections
$\alpha,\beta$ and a homogeneous scalar $f$,
\[
\boxed{
    [\alpha,f\beta]_{\mathrm{Sp}}
    =
    \rho(\alpha)(f)\beta
    +
    (-1)^{|\alpha||f|}
    f[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Sp}}.
}
\]
In degree zero this is the classical formula
\[
    [\alpha,f\beta]_{\mathrm{Sp}}
    =
    \rho(\alpha)(f)\beta
    +
    f[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{Sp}}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Rational rectification sends the relative anchored partition-Lie monad to the
ordinary homologically graded dg Lie-algebroid monad.  Its binary anchored
compatibility is the graded derivation rule with the displayed Koszul sign.
The degree-zero specialization removes the sign.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Higher Artinian Spencer coherence]
\label{thm:as-higher-coherence}
The multiweight cooperation maps of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-multiweight-pd-substitution} satisfy all symmetric,
unital, and substitution coherences of the integral PD cooperation system.
The directional Artinian cross-effects and the comonadic scalar-diagonal
Hurewicz maps are natural for these cooperation maps.  Continuous
pro-coherent duality gives the complete spectral partition-Lie substitution
system.  After rational rectification, the arity-three relation becomes the
dg homotopy-Jacobi relation and, in a strict dg Lie model, the ordinary Jacobi
identity.  The support-marked geometric Cech system remains a separate
raw/formalized object and is not used to define these PD coherences.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The symmetric, unit, and substitution identities are the corresponding
identities of the multi-filtered cotangent--square-zero comonad after
extracting exact multiweights.  Coassociativity of the comonad is precisely
associativity for iterated partitions of the input set.
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification} identifies the genuine
directional square-zero cross-effect with the all-positive Cech multiweight,
while Theorem~\ref{thm:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison} gives the natural
scalar-diagonal comparison on the canonical comonadic resolution.  Their
naturality makes them compatible with the same comonad cooperations.
Continuous duality reverses the cooperation diagrams to the partition-Lie
substitution diagrams.  Rational rectification carries the PD spectral
partition-Lie monad to the shifted dg Lie-algebroid monad, whose first
nontrivial three-input relation is homotopy Jacobi.
\end{proof}

\section{Constructed adic principal parts and operator order}
\label{sec:as-adic-operators}

Fix a spectral Artinian arrow $A\to B$ and write
\[
    P_{A/B}=B\otimes_A B\longrightarrow B.
\]
The two structural maps
\[
    s,t:B\longrightarrow P_{A/B},
    \qquad
    s(b)=b\otimes1,
    \quad
    t(b)=1\otimes b,
\]
will be retained explicitly.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-diagonal-complete-aft}, the diagonal augmentation lies in
the complete almost-finite sector.

\begin{definition}[Completed principal-parts filtration]
\label{def:as-completed-principal-parts}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    :=
    \widehat{\operatorname{adic}}
    (P_{A/B}\to B)
}
\]
and
\[
    P_{A/B}^{\le r}
    :=
    F^0P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    /
    F^{r+1}P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\]
Each quotient retains its natural source and target $B$-actions through
$s$ and $t$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Associated graded of completed principal parts]
\label{thm:as-principal-parts-associated-graded}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Gr}
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/A}(1)
    \right).
}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The adic associated-graded theorem of the preceding chapter gives
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/P_{A/B}}[-1](1)
    \right).
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-diagonal-cotangent} identifies
$L_{B/P_{A/B}}[-1]\simeq L_{B/A}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite principal parts are almost perfect]
\label{prop:as-finite-principal-parts-aperf}
For every finite $r$, the source-$B$-module and target-$B$-module underlying
$P_{A/B}^{\le r}$ are almost perfect.  The assertion is natural under
coherent spectral-etale base change.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The finite quotient is built by finitely many extensions whose associated
graded pieces are
\[
    \operatorname{Sym}_B^i(L_{B/A}),
    \qquad 0\le i\le r.
\]
The cotangent coefficient of a spectral Artinian arrow is almost perfect,
finite derived symmetric powers of an almost-perfect module over a coherent
connective ring are almost perfect, and almost-perfect modules are closed
under finite extensions.  Interchanging $s$ and $t$ is induced by the
transposition of the two Cech vertices, so the same conclusion holds for
both module structures.  Cotangent and adic base change identify the
filtrations after coherent spectral-etale transport.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite-order Spencer operators]
\label{def:as-finite-order-spencer-operators}
For $B$-modules $M,N$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Diff}_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}(M,N)
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{B,s}}
    \left(
        P_{A/B}^{\le r}
        \otimes_{B,t}M,
        N
    \right).
}
\]
Put
\[
    \operatorname{Diff}_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}(M,N)
    :=
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    \operatorname{Diff}_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}(M,N).
\]
For $M=N=B$ write
\[
    \mathcal D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r},
    \qquad
    \mathcal D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}.
\]
\end{definition}

\subsection{The finite-diagonal pro-coherent kernel category}

The two $B$-actions must not be collapsed to a single central action.  The
kernel category must also be closed under convolution on the dense coefficient
sector on which continuous duality is performed.  Source almost-perfectness
alone does not provide such closure.  We therefore use the intrinsic finite
formal-diagonal order detected by the already constructed adic filtration.

\begin{definition}[Finite diagonal neighbourhoods]
\label{def:as-finite-diagonal-neighbourhoods}
Put
\[
    \Delta_{B/R}:=B\otimes_RB\xrightarrow{\mu}B
\]
and form its functorial completed adic filtration
\[
    F^\bullet\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    :=
    \widehat{\operatorname{adic}}(\Delta_{B/R}\to B).
\]
For $d\geq0$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \Delta_{B/R}^{\leq d}
    :=
    F^0\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    /F^{d+1}\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
}
\]
A relative $(B,B)$-kernel $K$ has \emph{finite diagonal order} if the action
map
\[
    B\otimes_RB\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}(K)
\]
factors through $\Delta_{B/R}^{\leq d}$ for some finite $d$.  This is a
property of the kernel; no value of $d$ is retained as structure.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Finite-order almost-perfect two-sided kernels]
\label{def:as-aperf-kernels}
Let
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{APerfKern}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}
}
\]
be the full $\infty$-category of relative $(B,B)$-bimodule spectra $K$ such
that:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the source restriction of $K$ is almost perfect over $B$;
    \item the target restriction of $K$ is almost perfect over $B$;
    \item $K$ has finite diagonal order.
\end{enumerate}
For $K,L$ in this category define
\[
\boxed{
    K\odot_BL
    :=
    K\otimes_{B,t/s}L,
}
\]
using the target action of $K$ and the source action of $L$ in the middle.
No connectivity condition is imposed in the definition: it would not be
stable under desuspension.  The principal-parts kernels used below happen to
be connective objects of this stable category.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Additivity of diagonal order under convolution]
\label{lem:as-kernel-order-additive}
If $K$ has diagonal order at most $r$ and $L$ has diagonal order at most $s$,
then $K\odot_BL$ has diagonal order at most $r+s$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Cech correspondence comultiplication of the base diagonal
\[
    \Delta_{B/R}
    \longrightarrow
    \Delta_{B/R}\otimes_{B,t/s}\Delta_{B/R}
\]
is a morphism of augmented algebras.  The adic adjunction and Day convolution
therefore give the completed filtered lift
\[
    F^\bullet\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \longrightarrow
    F^\bullet\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \widehat\otimes_{\mathrm{Day},B,t/s}
    F^\bullet\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\]
Modulo weight $r+s+1$ it factors through
$\Delta_{B/R}^{\leq r}\otimes_B\Delta_{B/R}^{\leq s}$, because a Day weight
$i+j\geq r+s+1$ forces $i\geq r+1$ or $j\geq s+1$.  The two given kernel
actions therefore induce an action of $\Delta_{B/R}^{\leq r+s}$ on
$K\odot_BL$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Almost-perfect closure of finite-order convolution]
\label{lem:as-kernel-aperf-convolution}
If $K,L\in\operatorname{APerfKern}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}$, then
$K\odot_BL$ is almost perfect for both its source and target $B$-module
structures.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Almost-perfect modules over a connective ring are bounded below.  Choose
integers $a,b$ such that the target restriction $K_t[a]$ and the source
restriction $L_s[b]$ are connective.  Then
\[
    (K\odot_BL)[a+b]
    \simeq
    K[a]\otimes_{B,t/s}L[b].
\]
We prove that this shifted convolution is almost perfect as a source module.
Fix a Postnikov degree $d$.  Since $L_s[b]$ is connective almost perfect,
choose a perfect source-$B$ module $P_d\to L[b]$ with $d$-connective cofiber.
Tensor over the middle $B$-action with $K[a]$.  Because $P_d$ is finite cell
as a source module,
$K[a]\otimes_{B,t/s}P_d$ is obtained from the source-$B$ module underlying
$K[a]$ by finite limits, colimits, shifts, and retracts and is therefore
almost perfect.  The tensor of the $d$-connective cofiber with the connective
target-$B$ module $K_t[a]$ remains $d$-connective.  Hence the shifted
convolution admits perfect source-$B$ approximations through every Postnikov
degree and is almost perfect.  Desuspending proves the source statement for
$K\odot_BL$.

For the target statement, choose shifts making the target restriction of $K$
and the source restriction of $L$ connective, approximate the target
restriction of $K$ by perfect modules, and use the same argument with the two
sides interchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Dense monoidal finite-order kernel category]
\label{prop:as-finite-kernel-monoidal}
The product $\odot_B$ makes
$\operatorname{APerfKern}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}$ an idempotent-complete stable
monoidal $\infty$-category with unit the regular diagonal kernel $B$.  Every
finite principal-parts kernel $P_{A/B}^{\leq r}$ belongs to this category and
has diagonal order at most $r$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Closure under convolution is
Lemmas~\ref{lem:as-kernel-order-additive} and~\ref{lem:as-kernel-aperf-convolution}.
Associativity and the unit are those of relative tensor product.  The
subcategory of bimodules whose two restrictions are almost perfect is stable
and idempotent complete, and finite diagonal order is closed under finite
limits, finite colimits, shifts, and retracts.  Hence the displayed finite
order subcategory is stable and idempotent complete.

For an Artinian arrow $A\to B$, the coefficient map $R\to A$ gives a morphism
of augmented diagonal algebras
\[
    \Delta_{B/R}=B\otimes_RB
    \longrightarrow
    P_{A/B}=B\otimes_AB.
\]
Functoriality of the adic filtration sends
$F^{r+1}\Delta_{B/R}^{\mathrm{ad}}$ into
$F^{r+1}P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}$.  Therefore the action on
$P_{A/B}^{\leq r}$ factors through $\Delta_{B/R}^{\leq r}$.  The two module
restrictions are almost perfect by
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-finite-principal-parts-aperf}; in fact they are
connective.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Pro-coherent kernel category and convolution]
\label{con:as-procoherent-kernel-category}
Continuous source duality sends
$K\in\operatorname{APerfKern}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}$ to a pro-coherent
source-$B$ coefficient
\[
    K^{\vee_s}\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B,
\]
retaining the target $B$-action by precomposition.  Let
\[
    \operatorname{dAPerfKern}^{\vee,\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}
\]
