be the full subcategory of these continuous duals.  On dense generators put
\[
\boxed{
    K^{\vee_s}\star_BL^{\vee_s}
    :=
    (K\odot_BL)^{\vee_s}.
}
\]
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}
}
\]
to be the relative pro-coherent right-left completion of
$\operatorname{dAPerfKern}^{\vee,\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}$ with the same finite
cosimplicial-totalization and almost-eventually-constant tower relations used
for $\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$.  Extend $\star_B$ by the corresponding
sifted-colimit/right-left universal property.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Pro-coherent kernel convolution]
\label{prop:as-procoherent-kernel-convolution}
The preceding construction makes
\[
\boxed{
    \left(
        \operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R},
        \star_B
    \right)
}
\]
a presentable monoidal stable $\infty$-category.  On dense generators,
\[
    K^{\vee_s}\star_BL^{\vee_s}
    \simeq
    (K\otimes_{B,t/s}L)^{\vee_s}.
\]
The forgetful functor remembers the pro-coherent source coefficient and target
$B$-action.  Coherent spectral-etale transport preserves the finite-diagonal
subcategory and is monoidal for $\star_B$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the dense subcategory, associativity and the unit are inherited from
relative tensor product.  Lemma~\ref{lem:as-kernel-aperf-convolution} gives
the continuity estimates needed for the right-left extension.  Convolution is
exact separately and preserves sifted colimits.  After shifting an
almost-perfect kernel into the connective range, tensoring preserves finite
totalizations in the almost-perfect sector.  Likewise, if a tower has fibres
whose connectivity tends to $+\infty$, tensoring with a fixed almost-perfect
kernel changes those connectivity bounds by at most a fixed integer depending
on a lower-connectivity bound for that kernel.  Hence an
almost-eventually-constant tower is carried to another such tower.  The same
right-left extension theorem used for the pro-coherent partition-Lie monad
therefore extends the product, unit, associativity, and higher coherence
uniquely to the completion.

For coherent spectral-etale change of centre, the finite adic diagonal tower
base-changes by cotangent etale base change and the universal property of the
adic filtration.  Hence finite diagonal order is preserved.  Extension of
scalars preserves almost-perfect modules and relative tensor products, while
finite-Tor pro-coherent Beck--Chevalley identifies continuous duals.  The
transported dense product is therefore monoidal, and uniqueness of the
right-left extension gives the completed monoidal Beck--Chevalley
equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Source-linear enrichment and continuous coduality]
\label{prop:as-source-linear-enrichment}
There is a canonical colimit-preserving action
\[
\boxed{
    \odot_s:
    \operatorname{Mod}_B
    \times
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B
}
\]
characterized on
$P\in\operatorname{Perf}(B)$ and
$N\in\operatorname{APerf}(B)$ by
\[
\boxed{
    P\odot_sN^\vee
    \simeq
    \left(P^\vee\otimes_BN\right)^\vee.
}
\]
For every $V\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$, the functor
$(-)\odot_sV$ has a $\operatorname{Mod}_B$-valued right adjoint.  Write
\[
\boxed{
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}(V,W)
    \in
    \operatorname{Mod}_B
}
\]
for the resulting source-linear internal Hom, characterized by
\[
    \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_B}
    \left(
        P,
        \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}(V,W)
    \right)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
    \left(P\odot_sV,W\right).
\]
On the dense almost-perfect sector there is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
    \left(N^\vee,K^\vee\right)
    \simeq
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_B(K,N).
}
\]
In particular, with $B\simeq B^\vee$,
\[
\boxed{
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
    \left(N^\vee,B\right)
    \simeq N.
}
\]
The internal Hom is contravariant in its first variable and therefore carries
colimits there to limits.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The rigid symmetric monoidal category $\operatorname{Perf}(B)$ acts on the
continuous-dual dense sector by the displayed formula.  If $P,Q$ are perfect,
rigidity gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
(P\otimes_BQ)\odot_sN^\vee
&\simeq
\left((P\otimes_BQ)^\vee\otimes_BN\right)^\vee\\
&\simeq
P\odot_s\left(Q\odot_sN^\vee\right),
\end{aligned}
\]
and the regular module is the unit.  Continuous duals of coherent modules
compactly generate $\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$, so this action extends
uniquely by colimits in the second variable.  Since
$\operatorname{Mod}_B\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Perf}(B))$, it
then extends uniquely by colimits in the first variable to the displayed
$\operatorname{Mod}_B$-action.

For fixed $V$, the functor $(-)\odot_sV$ preserves colimits between
presentable categories and therefore admits a right adjoint.  To identify it
on the dense sector, let $P$ be perfect and let $N,K$ be almost perfect.  Full
faithfulness of continuous duality and rigidity give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
\left(P\odot_sN^\vee,K^\vee\right)
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
\left((P^\vee\otimes_BN)^\vee,K^\vee\right)\\
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_B}
\left(K,P^\vee\otimes_BN\right)\\
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_B}
\left(P,\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_B(K,N)\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Yoneda gives the asserted internal-Hom formula.  Taking $K=B$ gives the
source-codual formula.  Finally, for any $P\in\operatorname{Mod}_B$,
colimit preservation of $P\odot_s(-)$ gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_B}
\left(
    P,
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
    \left(\operatorname*{colim}_\lambda V_\lambda,W\right)
\right)
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
\left(
    \operatorname*{colim}_\lambda(P\odot_sV_\lambda),W
\right)\\
&\simeq
\lim_\lambda
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
\left(P\odot_sV_\lambda,W\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Yoneda therefore identifies the source-linear internal Hom of the colimit with
the inverse limit of the source-linear internal Homs, proving the final
assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Kernel action on pro-coherent coefficients and continuous source codual]
\label{con:as-kernel-module-action}
The two-sided kernel category acts on one-sided pro-coherent coefficients.
On dense generators define
\[
\boxed{
    K^{\vee_s}\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}N^\vee
    :=
    \left(
        K\otimes_{B,t}N
    \right)^\vee,
}
\]
for
$K\in\operatorname{APerfKern}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}$ and
$N\in\operatorname{APerf}(B)$, where the tensor product uses the target
$B$-action of $K$ and the ordinary $B$-action of $N$, while the resulting
source $B$-action is retained before continuous duality.  The same
right-left extension used in
Construction~\ref{con:as-procoherent-kernel-category} extends this formula
to a colimit-preserving action
\[
\boxed{
    \star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}:
    \operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}
    \times
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B.
}
\]
Associativity and the unit are inherited from relative tensor product:
\[
    (K\star_BL)\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}M
    \simeq
    K\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}
    \left(L\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}M\right),
    \qquad
    B\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}M\simeq M.
\]

Use the source-linear enrichment of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-source-linear-enrichment}.  For
$V\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$ define its continuous source codual by
\[
\boxed{
    V^{\dagger_s}
    :=
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B}
    (V,B)
    \in
    \operatorname{Mod}_B,
}
\]
where $B\simeq B^\vee$ is the continuous dual of the perfect regular
$B$-module.  On the dense almost-perfect sector,
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-source-linear-enrichment} gives
\[
\boxed{
    (N^\vee)^{\dagger_s}\simeq N.
}
\]
Since the source-linear internal Hom is contravariant in its first variable,
\[
\boxed{
    \left(\operatorname*{colim}_\lambda V_\lambda\right)^{\dagger_s}
    \simeq
    \lim_\lambda V_\lambda^{\dagger_s}.
}
\]
Thus continuous source coduality converts the filtered colimit of finite-order
distribution kernels into the inverse-limit completion of principal parts.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Module action and coduality are continuous]
\label{prop:as-kernel-module-action}
The action $\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}$ is exact separately and preserves sifted
colimits in each variable.  It is coherently compatible with
$\star_B$ and with coherent spectral-etale transport.  On dense generators
continuous source coduality reverses the action:
\[
\boxed{
    \left(
        K^{\vee_s}\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}N^\vee
    \right)^{\dagger_s}
    \simeq
    K\otimes_{B,t}N.
}
\]
The same statement holds for finite iterated kernel actions.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the dense sector the assertions are the corresponding properties of
relative tensor product and exact continuous duality.  The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-procoherent-kernel-convolution} shows that tensoring
with a fixed almost-perfect kernel preserves the finite totalization
relations and changes the connectivity bounds of an
almost-eventually-constant tower by only a fixed amount.  The same estimates
apply when the final factor is a one-sided almost-perfect coefficient.
Therefore the right-left universal property extends the dense action,
associativity, unit, and Beck--Chevalley maps uniquely.  The codual formula on
dense generators is full faithfulness of continuous duality, and finite
iteration follows by associativity.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Relative $R$-linear enrichment of kernel module actions]
\label{prop:as-relative-r-linear-enrichment}
Put $\mathcal C_B:=\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$.  Restriction of the
source-$B$ action along $R\to B$ defines a canonical
$\operatorname{Mod}_R$-action
\[
\boxed{
    P\odot_RV
    :=
    (B\otimes_RP)\odot_sV,
    \qquad
    P\in\operatorname{Mod}_R,
    \quad
    V\in\mathcal C_B.
}
\]
Its $\operatorname{Mod}_R$-valued internal Hom is
\[
\boxed{
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_B,R}(V,W)
    :=
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_B}(V,W),
}
\]
characterized by
\[
    \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_R}
    \left(
        P,
        \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_B,R}(V,W)
    \right)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C_B}
    (P\odot_RV,W).
\]

For every relative two-sided kernel
$K^{\vee_s}\in\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$, the one-sided action
\[
    K^{\vee_s}\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}(-):\mathcal C_B\to\mathcal C_B
\]
is canonically $R$-linear.  Consequently, if $\mathbb D$ is an $E_1$-algebra
in $\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$, the monad
\[
    \mathbb T_{\mathbb D}(V)
    :=
    \mathbb D\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}V
\]
is $R$-linear and its Eilenberg--Moore category
$\operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D}$ is canonically tensored and
enriched over $\operatorname{Mod}_R$.  Write
\[
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D}}(-,-)
\]
for this enrichment.  If
$F_{\mathbb D}(V)=\mathbb T_{\mathbb D}(V)$ is the free module, then
\[
\boxed{
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D}}
    (F_{\mathbb D}(V),N)
    \simeq
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_B,R}(V,UN).
}
\]
No source-$B$ linearity of $\mathbb T_{\mathbb D}$ is asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For the internal-Hom formula, adjunction along $R\to B$ and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-source-linear-enrichment} give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Map}_R
\left(
    P,
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_B}(V,W)
\right)
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}_B
\left(
    B\otimes_RP,
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_B}(V,W)
\right)\\
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal C_B}
\left((B\otimes_RP)\odot_sV,W\right),
\end{aligned}
\]
which is the required universal property.

It remains to prove $R$-linearity of the kernel action.  On dense generators,
let $P\in\operatorname{Perf}(R)$,
$K\in\operatorname{APerfKern}^{\mathrm{fin}}_{B/R}$, and
$N\in\operatorname{APerf}(B)$.  Since $P$ is perfect,
\[
    (B\otimes_RP)^\vee_B
    \simeq
    B\otimes_RP^\vee.
\]
Using the dense formulas for $\odot_s$ and $\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}$ gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
K^{\vee_s}\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}
(P\odot_RN^\vee)
&\simeq
\left(
K\otimes_{B,t}
\bigl((B\otimes_RP^\vee)\otimes_BN\bigr)
\right)^\vee\\
&\simeq
\left(
P^\vee\otimes_R
(K\otimes_{B,t}N)
\right)^\vee\\
&\simeq
P\odot_R
\left(K^{\vee_s}\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}N^\vee\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
The middle equivalence uses precisely that a relative $(B,B)$-kernel is a
module over $B\otimes_RB$: its source and target restrictions have the same
underlying $R$-action.  The right-left extension is generated from these
dense objects by relations preserved by both sides, so the equivalence
extends uniquely to all $P$, $K$, and $V$.

An $R$-linear monad on an $R$-tensored presentable category has an
$R$-tensored Eilenberg--Moore category with enriched free--forgetful
adjunction.  Concretely, testing the proposed free-module formula against
$P\in\operatorname{Mod}_R$ and using $R$-linearity of the monad reduces it to
the ordinary free--forgetful adjunction, which proves the displayed enriched
equivalence by Yoneda.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Internal pro-coherent distribution kernel]
\label{def:as-internal-distributions}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}
    :=
    \left(P_{A/B}^{\le r}\right)^{\vee_s}
    \in
    \operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}.
}
\]
Put
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    :=
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}.
}
\]
The source continuous dual, target coefficient action, and finite diagonal
order are all retained.  Stable scalar sections recover the scalar operator
spectrum in the usual range where the section/continuous-dual comparison
applies.
\end{definition}


\begin{proposition}[Canonical one-sided distribution module]
\label{prop:as-canonical-distribution-module}
The filtered $E_1$-algebra
$\mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}$ acts canonically on
$B\in\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$ through the module action
$\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}$:
\[
\boxed{
    \mathrm{ev}_A:
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    \star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}B
    \longrightarrow B.
}
\]
This is a one-sided coefficient action.  It is not a morphism of
two-sided kernel algebras
$\mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}\to B$ and therefore does not supply an
operator-algebra augmentation.

At finite order $r$, the action is the continuous source dual of the source
structural map
\[
    s_r:B\longrightarrow P_{A/B}^{\le r}.
\]
The finite-order actions are compatible with the order filtration and assemble
to $\mathrm{ev}_A$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the dense finite-order sector,
Construction~\ref{con:as-kernel-module-action} gives
\[
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}
    \star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}B
    \simeq
    \left(P_{A/B}^{\le r}\right)^{\vee_s}.
\]
The map $s_r:B\to P_{A/B}^{\le r}$ is linear for the retained source
$B$-action.  Continuous source duality therefore gives
\[
    \left(P_{A/B}^{\le r}\right)^{\vee_s}
    \longrightarrow
    B^\vee\simeq B.
\]
The module associativity square is the continuous dual of the simplicial
identity saying that the fully source-degenerate vertex is preserved by Cech
composition.  Equivalently, the two maps from $B$ to the corresponding
two-arrow principal-parts kernel agree after the completed Cech
comultiplication.  The unit identity is dual to the unit degeneracy.  These
identities are compatible with passage from order $r$ to order $r+1$, so the
filtered colimit gives the asserted module action.

The action map is required to be linear only for the source coefficient
structure.  For a nonzero anchor it is not linear for the second kernel
$B$-action; hence it is not a morphism to the monoidal unit in
$\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$ and does not define an algebra
augmentation.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Completed Cech convolution}

\begin{construction}[Completed Day-convolution Cech comultiplication]
\label{con:as-adic-day-convolution}
The ordinary Cech composition map
\[
    \Delta:
    P_{A/B}
    \longrightarrow
    P_{A/B}\otimes_{B,t/s}P_{A/B}
\]
is a morphism of augmented $B$-algebras with the indicated middle
target/source action.

Let
\[
    F^\bullet P
    :=
    \operatorname{adic}(P_{A/B}\to B).
\]
The units $P\to F^0P$ in the two factors and $\Delta$ define a map of
augmented algebras
\[
    P_{A/B}
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{aug}
    \left(
        F^\bullet P
        \otimes_{\mathrm{Day},B,t/s}
        F^\bullet P
    \right).
\]
By the adjunction $\operatorname{adic}\dashv\operatorname{aug}$ there is a
unique filtered lift.  Applying the complete-filtration reflector gives
\[
\boxed{
    \Delta^{\mathrm{ad}}:
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \longrightarrow
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \widehat\otimes_{\mathrm{Day},B,t/s}
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
}
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Coassociativity of completed principal parts]
\label{prop:as-adic-cech-coassociative}
The morphism $\Delta^{\mathrm{ad}}$ is coherently coassociative and counital.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The two composites to the completed triple Day tensor are, under
$\operatorname{adic}\dashv\operatorname{aug}$, both adjoint to the same
three-arrow Cech map
\[
    P_{A/B}
    \longrightarrow
    P_{A/B}
    \otimes_{B,t/s}
    P_{A/B}
    \otimes_{B,t/s}
    P_{A/B}.
\]
Uniqueness of the adjoint lift identifies them canonically.  The same
argument with the unit degeneracy $P_{A/B}\to B$ gives the counit, and the
higher Cech simplicial identities give all higher coherence.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Higher Cech degrees and iterated completed corings}

For a spectral Artinian arrow $A\to B$ put
\[
    P_A^{[n]}
    :=
    \underbrace{
        B\otimes_A\cdots\otimes_A B
    }_{n+1},
    \qquad n\geq0,
\]
with multiplication augmentation $P_A^{[n]}\to B$.

\begin{lemma}[Cotangent coefficient in every Cech degree]
\label{lem:as-higher-cech-cotangent}
There is a canonical natural equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    L_{B/P_A^{[n]}}[-1]
    \simeq
    L_{B/A}^{\oplus n}.
}
\]
For $n=0$ both sides are zero.  The equivalences are compatible with all Cech
faces and degeneracies.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Cotangent base change gives
\[
    B\otimes_{P_A^{[n]}}
    L_{P_A^{[n]}/A}
    \simeq
    L_{B/A}^{\oplus(n+1)}.
\]
In the transitivity triangle for
\[
    A\longrightarrow P_A^{[n]}\longrightarrow B,
\]
the first map after base change is the fold
\[
    L_{B/A}^{\oplus(n+1)}
    \longrightarrow
    L_{B/A}.
\]
Its homotopy fibre is canonically
$L_{B/A}^{\oplus n}$.  Transitivity identifies that fibre with
$L_{B/P_A^{[n]}}[-1]$.  Naturality of cotangent base change and transitivity
under multiplication, omission, and insertion of tensor factors gives the
Cech compatibility.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Higher Cech augmentations are complete almost-finite]
\label{lem:as-higher-cech-complete-aft}
If $A\to B$ is spectral Artinian, then every
\[
    P_A^{[n]}\longrightarrow B
\]
lies in the complete almost-finite sector and its completed adic filtration
does not change the underlying augmentation.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Surjectivity of $\pi_0(A)\to\pi_0(B)$ gives
\[
    \pi_0(P_A^{[n]})
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \pi_0(B),
\]
so the degree-zero augmentation ideal is zero.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-higher-cech-cotangent},
$L_{B/P_A^{[n]}}[-1]$ is a finite direct sum of the connective
almost-perfect module $L_{B/A}$.  The complete almost-finite criterion of the
preceding chapter applies.  Objects in that sector are fixed by the
constructed adic completion.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Associated graded Cech bar]
\label{prop:as-higher-cech-associated-graded}
Equip every $P_A^{[n]}$ with its completed adic filtration.  Then
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Gr}
    F^\bullet P_A^{[n]}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/A}^{\oplus n}(1)
    \right).
}
\]
Under these equivalences, the associated-graded Cech structure maps are the
ordinary additive bar maps on the direct-sum primitive coordinates.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply the adic associated-graded theorem of the preceding chapter to
$P_A^{[n]}\to B$ and use
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-higher-cech-cotangent}.  Naturality of the adic adjunction
and of the cotangent equivalence identifies each Cech map on associated
graded.  Multiplying adjacent Cech factors adds the corresponding primitive
coordinates; inserting a unit inserts zero.  These are the ordinary additive
bar maps.
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[Iterated completed Cech coring]
\label{prop:as-iterated-completed-cech-coring}
Let
\[
    F^\bullet P^{\mathrm{ad}}
    :=
    F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
\]
and interpret the zero-fold completed Day tensor as $B$ in filtration weight
zero.  For every $n\geq0$ there is a canonical equivalence of complete
filtered source--target kernels
\[
\boxed{
    F^\bullet P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \underbrace{
        F^\bullet P^{\mathrm{ad}}
        \widehat\otimes_{\mathrm{Day},B,t/s}
        \cdots
        \widehat\otimes_{\mathrm{Day},B,t/s}
        F^\bullet P^{\mathrm{ad}}
    }_{n}.
}
\]
The equivalences are compatible with all Cech faces and degeneracies.  In
particular the completed $n$-simplex is the completed $n$-fold convolution
tensor power of the completed degree-one principal-parts coring.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $n=0$ both sides are $B$.  For $n\geq1$, the ordinary Cech correspondence
identifies
\[
    P_A^{[n]}
    \simeq
    \underbrace{
        P_{A/B}\otimes_{B,t/s}\cdots\otimes_{B,t/s}P_{A/B}
    }_{n}.
\]
Together with the units from each uncompleted factor to filtration zero of
$F^\bullet P^{\mathrm{ad}}$, this gives a morphism of augmented arrows from
$P_A^{[n]}\to B$ to the augmentation of the completed $n$-fold Day tensor.
The adjunction
$\operatorname{adic}\dashv\operatorname{aug}$ gives a canonical filtered
lift
\[
    F^\bullet P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}
    \longrightarrow
    \left(F^\bullet P^{\mathrm{ad}}\right)^{
        \widehat\otimes_{\mathrm{Day},B,t/s}n}.
\]

We check that it is an equivalence on associated graded.
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-higher-cech-associated-graded} gives
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}
    F^\bullet P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/A}^{\oplus n}(1)
    \right).
\]
On the target, associated graded is symmetric monoidal for Day convolution,
and Theorem~\ref{thm:as-principal-parts-associated-graded} gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \operatorname{Gr}
    \left(
        \left(F^\bullet P^{\mathrm{ad}}\right)^{
        \widehat\otimes_{\mathrm{Day}}n}
    \right)
    &\simeq
    \bigotimes_{j=1}^n
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/A}(1)
    \right)\\
    &\simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/A}^{\oplus n}(1)
    \right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Under the cotangent identification of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-higher-cech-cotangent}, the weight-one map is the direct sum
of the $n$ adjacent Cech primitive coordinates and is the identity on
$L_{B/A}^{\oplus n}$.  Since both associated gradeds are free graded
commutative algebras on that weight-one coefficient, the associated-graded
map is an equivalence.  Both sides are complete, so complete filtered
detection gives the displayed equivalence.

Every map used to construct the comparison is induced from the corresponding
Cech map before applying the adic adjunction.  Uniqueness of the filtered
adjoint lift therefore identifies the comparisons after every face,
degeneracy, identity, composition, and higher simplicial pasting.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Completed Artinian Cech--Spencer cochains]
\label{def:as-completed-artinian-cech-spencer}
For a spectral Artinian arrow $A\to B$, let
\[
\boxed{
    \widehat{\mathscr C}^{\,\bullet}_{A/B}
    :=
    \left(
        [n]\longmapsto
        F^\bullet P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}
    \right)
}
\]
be the complete filtered cosimplicial commutative algebra furnished by
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-higher-cech-complete-aft}.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    :=
    N^\bullet\widehat{\mathscr C}^{\,\bullet}_{A/B}.
}
\]
Its underlying unfiltered cochain object is
$\mathbf{Sp}_{A}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}$ because every
$P_A^{[n]}\to B$ is fixed by the constructed adic completion.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Composition and operator order]
\label{thm:as-spencer-operator-convolution}
For $r,s\ge0$ there are canonical composition maps
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Diff}_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}(M,N)
    \otimes
    \operatorname{Diff}_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le s}(N,K)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Diff}_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r+s}(M,K).
}
\]
They are coherently associative and unital.  On internal kernels there are
canonical multiplication maps
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}
    \star_B
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le s}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r+s}.
}
\]
Hence
$\mathcal D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}$ is an $E_1$-ring spectrum with
multiplicative order filtration, while
$\mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}$ is a filtered $E_1$-algebra object in
$\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Modulo filtration $r+s+1$, the Day-convolution target of
$\Delta^{\mathrm{ad}}$ factors through
\[
    P_{A/B}^{\le r}
    \otimes_{B,t/s}
    P_{A/B}^{\le s},
\]
because a Day weight $i+j\ge r+s+1$ forces $i\ge r+1$ or $j\ge s+1$.
Thus there is a canonical kernel-composition map
\[
    P_{A/B}^{\le r+s}
    \longrightarrow
    P_{A/B}^{\le r}
    \otimes_{B,t/s}
    P_{A/B}^{\le s}.
\]
Tensor with the coefficient using the target/source convention and apply the
tensor--Hom adjunction to obtain scalar operator composition.

For the internal statement, every finite principal-parts quotient is
almost perfect by
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-finite-principal-parts-aperf}.  Continuous source
duality and the defining formula for $\star_B$ therefore turn the displayed
coalgebra map into
\[
    (P^{\le r})^{\vee_s}
    \star_B
    (P^{\le s})^{\vee_s}
    \longrightarrow
    (P^{\le r+s})^{\vee_s}.
\]
Coassociativity and the unit follow from
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-adic-cech-coassociative}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Artinian-cell operator reconstruction}

Let
\[
    j_{B/R}:
    (\operatorname{Art}_{R/B}^{\mathrm{sp}})^{\mathrm{op}}
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{LieAlgd}_{B/R}^{\pi,E_\infty}
\]
be the Koszul-dual cell embedding
$A\mapsto D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{sp}}(A)$.

\begin{theorem}[Artinian Koszul-dual cell density]
\label{thm:as-artinian-cell-density}
For every spectral partition-Lie algebroid $\mathfrak a$, the canonical
morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname*{colim}_{(jA\to\mathfrak a)}jA
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathfrak a
}
\]
is an equivalence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The preceding chapter identifies
$(\operatorname{Art}_{R/B}^{\mathrm{sp}})^{\mathrm{op}}$ with the finite
cellular algebroid subcategory: structured square-zero pullbacks correspond
to negative cellular attachments, the Koszul-dual functor is fully faithful,
and Artinian excision identifies attachment maps.  Its cellular restricted
Yoneda theorem then identifies the whole algebroid category with the
sifted/cellular completion generated by these finite cells.  The displayed
comma-category colimit is the canonical density presentation supplied by
that theorem; no presentation is selected or retained on $\mathfrak a$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Cell operator functor]
\label{def:as-cell-operator-functor}
On the dense cell category define
\[
\boxed{
    K_{B/R}
    \left(
        D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{sp}}(A)
    \right)
    :=
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
}
\]
as a filtered $E_1$-algebra object in
$\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$.  An Artinian morphism induces the
corresponding principal-parts morphism, and continuous source duality defines
the functorial map on distributions.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Finite-order Spencer operator algebra of a controller]
\label{def:as-cellular-spencer-operator-algebra}
For
$\mathfrak a\in\operatorname{LieAlgd}_{B/R}^{\pi,E_\infty}$ define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    :=
    (\operatorname{Lan}_{j_{B/R}}K_{B/R})(\mathfrak a)
}
\]
in the presentable category of filtered $E_1$-algebras in
$\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$.
Equivalently,
\[
    \mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname*{colim}_{(D(A)\to\mathfrak a)}
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
\]
where the colimit is taken in that algebra category.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Intrinsicness and etale Beck--Chevalley]
\label{prop:as-cellular-operator-independence}
The preceding construction is functorial in $\mathfrak a$, retains no chosen
cellular presentation, and satisfies coherent spectral-etale base change.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Functoriality and presentation-independence are the universal properties of
left Kan extension.  Under coherent spectral-etale base change, the
two-variable Artinian transport carries cells to cells and the Koszul-dual
embedding to the transported embedding.  Proposition~\ref{prop:as-finite-principal-parts-aperf} and finite-Tor pro-coherent
Beck--Chevalley give
\[
    f^*
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb D_{A'/B'}^{\mathrm{Sp},\le r}
\]
in every finite order, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-procoherent-kernel-convolution} makes these
equivalences monoidal.  Passing to the filtered colimit gives the cellwise
equivalence.  The Beck--Chevalley transformation for the left Kan extension
is therefore an equivalence on the dense cells and hence on every
controller.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Formalized and raw co-Kleisli operators}

Define
\[
    \mathcal D_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{coKl,raw}}(E)
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{\mathcal E_X}(J_{\mathfrak G}E,E)
\]
and
\[
    \mathcal D_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{coKl,fm}}(E)
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{\mathcal E_X}(J_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}E,E).
\]

\begin{proposition}[Extension from raw to formalized co-Kleisli operators]
\label{prop:as-formalized-raw-operator-restriction}
Precomposition with
$\kappa^J_{\mathfrak G}:J_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}\to J_{\mathfrak G}$
gives an $E_1$ morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{ext}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{op}}:
    \mathcal D_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{coKl,raw}}(E)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal D_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{coKl,fm}}(E).
}
\]
It is an equivalence whenever quotient formalization is an equivalence.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A morphism of comonads preserves comultiplication and counit.
Precomposition therefore carries the co-Kleisli product for the raw comonad
to the co-Kleisli product for the formalized comonad.  If quotient
formalization is an equivalence, so is the comonad comparison.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cellwise distribution module bar and relative scalar coduality]
\label{thm:as-cellwise-operator-action}
Let $A\to B$ be a spectral Artinian arrow and abbreviate
\[
    \mathbb D_A
    :=
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}.
\]
The kernel action of
Construction~\ref{con:as-kernel-module-action} and the $E_1$ multiplication
of $\mathbb D_A$ define a sifted-colimit-preserving monad
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb T_A(M)
    :=
    \mathbb D_A\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}M
}
\]
on $\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-relative-r-linear-enrichment}, this monad is
canonically $R$-linear.  Let
\[
    \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D_A}
    :=
    \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbb T_A}
    (\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)
\]
be its one-sided module category.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-canonical-distribution-module}, $B$ is canonically
an object of this category.

Define the simplicial free module bar by
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B)_n
    :=
    \mathbb T_A^{\,n+1}(B),
    \qquad n\geq0.
}
\]
The nonterminal faces use multiplication of $\mathbb D_A$, the terminal face
uses the one-sided action on $B$, and the degeneracies use the unit.  Every
term is the free $\mathbb D_A$-module on $\mathbb T_A^n(B)$.

There is a canonical equivalence of complete filtered cosimplicial
$R$-modules
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \widehat{\mathscr C}^{\,\bullet}_{A/B}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D_A}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B)_\bullet,
        B
    \right).
}
\]
Consequently stable normalization gives
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    N^\bullet
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D_A}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B)_\bullet,
        B
    \right).
}
\]
The individual Cech terms retain their source and target $B$-module
structures, but the cosimplicial structure is asserted only to be
$R$-linear.  For a nonzero anchor it is not source-$B$-linear in general.
No two-sided algebra augmentation $\mathbb D_A\to B$ and no untyped action of
a pro-coherent kernel directly on the ordinary scalar Cech object is used.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Because the $n$-th bar term is free on $\mathbb T_A^n(B)$, the enriched
free--forgetful adjunction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-relative-r-linear-enrichment} gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D_A}}
    \left(
        \mathbb T_A^{n+1}(B),
        B
    \right)
    &\simeq
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B,R}
    \left(
        \mathbb T_A^n(B),
        B
    \right)\\
    &=
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \left(\mathbb T_A^n(B)\right)^{\dagger_s}.
\end{aligned}
\]
For $n=0$ this is $\operatorname{Res}_R^B B$, the restricted Cech
degree-zero coefficient.

For $n\geq1$, expand the filtered colimit defining $\mathbb D_A$ and use the
dense formula for the kernel action.  There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\mathbb T_A^n(B)
\simeq
\operatorname*{colim}_{r_1,\ldots,r_n}
\left(
    P_{A/B}^{\le r_1}
    \otimes_{B,t/s}\cdots\otimes_{B,t/s}
    P_{A/B}^{\le r_n}
\right)^{\vee_s}.
\]
Continuous source coduality converts this colimit to the inverse limit
\[
\left(\mathbb T_A^n(B)\right)^{\dagger_s}
\simeq
\lim_{r_1,\ldots,r_n}
P_{A/B}^{\le r_1}
\otimes_{B,t/s}\cdots\otimes_{B,t/s}
P_{A/B}^{\le r_n}.
\]
The right side is the filtration-zero object of the completed $n$-fold Day
tensor of $F^\bullet P_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}$.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-iterated-completed-cech-coring}, it is canonically
the completed Cech term $F^0P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}$.  Keeping the finite
rectangular order quotients rather than only their inverse limit gives the
complete filtered identification, and restricting scalars gives the stated
$R$-module equivalence.

It remains to check the cosimplicial structure.  Under the enriched free
module adjunction, an internal bar face is induced by convolution
multiplication; continuous coduality identifies it with the corresponding
completed Cech composition map.  The terminal face is induced by the
canonical one-sided action on $B$, whose dual is the fully source-degenerate
Cech map.  A degeneracy is induced by the distribution unit and dualizes to
insertion of a Cech unit.  All of these maps are $R$-linear because the two
kernel $B$-actions restrict to the same $R$-action.  They need not be linear
for the source $B$-action: already in Cech degree zero to one the target
scalar map records the anchor.  The simplicial identities and all higher
coherence are the same Cech identities used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-adic-cech-coassociative}.  Stable normalization gives
the second equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Kernel algebra versus scalar coefficient action]
\label{rem:as-kernel-versus-scalar-action}
The $E_1$-algebra $\mathbb D_A$ lives in the two-sided kernel category, while
its canonical coefficient module $B$ lives in the one-sided category
$\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B$.  The action
$\mathbb D_A\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}B\to B$ is therefore not an augmentation in
$\operatorname{Kern}^{\vee}_{B/R}$.  The completed scalar Cech object, after restriction along $R\to B$, is
recovered by \emph{relative $R$-linear continuous coduality} of the free
module bar.  This distinction is retained below in the rational PBW and
CE--Spencer comparisons.
\end{remark}


\section{Enveloping-to-Spencer actions}
\label{sec:as-enveloping}

The universal formalized scalar Spencer cochain object is a representation of
the global spectral controller.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-global-tangent-monad}, this is equivalently an algebra
over the descended tangent enveloping monad.  Thus there is a canonical
degreewise action
\[
\boxed{
    \mu_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}:
    \mathbb U^\pi_{\mathbb A^\pi_{\mathfrak G}}
    \left(
        U\mathbf{Sp}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \right)
    \longrightarrow
    U\mathbf{Sp}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}.
}
\]
The normalized differential is equivariant.  This is the integral
enveloping-to-Spencer action; no separately presented integral enveloping
algebra is introduced.

\begin{proposition}[Weight-one symbol comparison]
\label{prop:as-first-graded-comparison}
On the represented locally coherent first-order locus, the first positive
weight of
$\mu_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$
is the continuous dual of cotangent transitivity
\[
\boxed{
    L_{X/S}
    \longrightarrow
    L_{X/Q_{\mathfrak G}}
    \simeq
    \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^{\vee}.
}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first positive tangent weight is the universal anchored first-order
action by
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-filtered-enveloping-monad}.  The represented first-order
comparison of the preceding chapter identifies the underlying anchor of
$\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$ with the continuous dual of the
displayed cotangent transitivity map.  The bar--cotangent theorem identifies
the same first-order class on every Artinian Spencer cell.  The degreewise
action glues by
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-global-tangent-monad}.
\end{proof}

\section{Rational PBW--Spencer comparison}
\label{sec:as-rational-pbw}

Work in the rational coherent coefficient and boundedness range in which the
dg formal-moduli and dg representation comparison of the preceding chapter
applies.  Ordinary tensor-dual formulas will be stated only when the
relevant cotangent coefficient is perfect.

\begin{theorem}[Rational enveloping-algebra representation]
\label{thm:as-rational-enveloping-algebra}
On the coefficient/model range of the dg Lie-algebroid representation
theorem, rational rectification identifies
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}
    \left(
        \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Lie}}
    \right)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Mod}_{U(\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Lie}})}.
}
\]
Here $U(\mathfrak a)$ is an associative algebra in dg $B$-bimodules; the
anchor prevents the two $B$-actions from being silently identified.
Under this equivalence, rectification of
$\mu_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$ is the ordinary dg enveloping action on the
rectified Spencer cochain object.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Rational rectification identifies the relative spectral partition-Lie monad
with the shifted pro-coherent dg Lie-algebroid monad.  Slicing at the
controller and stabilizing identifies the tangent monads.  A spectrum object
in the slice of dg Lie algebroids over $\mathfrak a$ is a square-zero
semidirect extension
$\mathfrak a\ltimes M\to\mathfrak a$; its dg Lie-algebroid structure is
equivalent to a dg representation on $M$.  In the stated dg representation
range those representations are modules over the bimodule enveloping algebra
$U(\mathfrak a)$.  Thus the rectified tangent monad is the enveloping-module
monad, and its action on the Spencer cochain object is the usual enveloping
action.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Intrinsic pro-coherent Spencer symbol]
\label{prop:as-intrinsic-spencer-symbol}
For every spectral Artinian arrow $A\to B$,
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{gr}_r
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    \simeq
    \left(
        \operatorname{Sym}_B^rL_{B/A}
    \right)^{\vee_s}
}
\]
as a pro-coherent kernel.  On associated graded the source and target
$B$-actions agree.  No ordinary dualizability hypothesis is required.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The associated-graded principal-parts theorem gives
\[
    \operatorname{gr}_rP_{A/B}^{\mathrm{ad}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}_B^rL_{B/A}.
\]
Every finite principal-parts quotient is almost perfect, so continuous source
duality of the finite filtration quotient identifies its $r$-th graded
piece with the displayed continuous dual.  The source-target difference has
positive adic order, hence disappears on associated graded.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Ordinary symmetric symbol under perfectness]
\label{thm:as-rational-spencer-symbol}
Let $A\to B$ be a rational Artinian cell for which
$L_{B/A}$ is perfect.  Then
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{gr}
    \mathbb D_{A/B,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}_{B_{\mathrm{dg}}}
    \left(
        (L_{B/A})^\vee
    \right)
}
\]
with grading by operator order.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-intrinsic-spencer-symbol},
\[
    \operatorname{gr}_r
    \mathbb D_{A/B,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}
    \simeq
    \left(
        \operatorname{Sym}_B^rL_{B/A}
    \right)^\vee.
\]
Write $L:=L_{B/A}$.  Since $L$ is perfect,
\[
    \operatorname{Sym}_B^rL
    \simeq
    \left(L^{\otimes_B r}\right)_{h\Sigma_r}
\]
and mapping out of homotopy orbits gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
    \left(\operatorname{Sym}_B^rL\right)^\vee
    &\simeq
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_B
    \left(
        \left(L^{\otimes_B r}\right)_{h\Sigma_r},
        B
    \right)\\
    &\simeq
    \left(
        \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_B
        \left(L^{\otimes_B r},B\right)
    \right)^{h\Sigma_r}\\
    &\simeq
    \left(
        (L^\vee)^{\otimes_B r}
    \right)^{h\Sigma_r}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The rational Tate-vanishing theorem of the preceding chapter identifies the
norm map from homotopy orbits to homotopy fixed points for the finite group
$\Sigma_r$ as an equivalence.  Therefore
\[
    \left(
        (L^\vee)^{\otimes_B r}
    \right)^{h\Sigma_r}
    \simeq
    \left(
        (L^\vee)^{\otimes_B r}
    \right)_{h\Sigma_r}
    =
    \operatorname{Sym}_B^r(L^\vee).
\]
Rational symmetric-monoidal rectification gives the displayed dg formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Cellwise first-order Spencer symbol]
\label{con:as-cellwise-spencer-symbol}
Let
\[
    \mathfrak a_A
    :=
    D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(A)
\]
be a rectified dg Artinian cell in the dg representation range.  The
weight-one Spencer action and the one-sided distribution action on $B$ give a
canonical $B$-linear first-order symbol map
\[
\boxed{
    \sigma_A:
    \mathfrak a_A^{\mathrm{und}}
    \longrightarrow
    F_{\le1}
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}},
}
\]
whose induced action on the scalar coefficient $B$ is the dg anchor of
$\mathfrak a_A$.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Binary Hurewicz--convolution and Lie--Rinehart symbol relations]
\label{prop:as-binary-hurewicz-convolution}
For homogeneous local sections $x,y$ of $\mathfrak a_A$ and a homogeneous
scalar $b\in B$, the first-order symbol satisfies
\[
\boxed{
    [\sigma_A(x),\sigma_A(y)]
    =
    \sigma_A([x,y]),
}
\]
and
\[
\boxed{
    \sigma_A(x)b
    -
    (-1)^{|x||b|}b\sigma_A(x)
    =
    \rho_A(x)(b).
}
\]
The symbol is compatible with the dg differentials.  Hence $\sigma_A$ is a
Lie--Rinehart realization of $\mathfrak a_A$ in the filtered associative
$B$-bimodule algebra
$\mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}$; no dg Lie-algebroid structure on the
whole distribution algebra is asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The scalar relation is the weight-one source--target relation.  The
first-order difference between target and source scalar multiplication is the
continuous dual of cotangent transitivity, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-leibniz}, after rational rectification, gives
exactly the displayed graded commutator with the anchor action.

For the bracket relation, first note that the convolution commutator of two
order-one distributions has order at most one.  Indeed,
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-intrinsic-spencer-symbol} identifies the associated
graded principal-parts kernel with the continuous dual of the graded
commutative symmetric coalgebra on $L_{B/A}$.  The associated-graded Cech
comultiplication is the additive, cocommutative bar comultiplication, so the
order-two symbols of the two ordered convolution products cancel in their
graded commutator.

It remains to identify the order-one symbol.  Polarize the two first-order
inputs as independent square-zero directions.  The exact directional
bidegree $(1,1)$ of the scalar diagonal records the alternating two ordered
convolution products.  The comonadic scalar-diagonal Hurewicz comparison of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison} is compatible with
multi-filtered comonad comultiplication and PD substitution by
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-bar-level-hurewicz}.  After rational rectification,
Corollary~\ref{cor:as-integral-pd-arity} identifies the corresponding exact
cotangent bidegree with the binary dg Lie-algebroid operation.  Therefore
\[
    \operatorname{gr}_1
    [\sigma_A(x),\sigma_A(y)]
    =
    \operatorname{gr}_1
    \sigma_A([x,y]).
\]
Consequently
\[
    c(x,y)
    :=
    [\sigma_A(x),\sigma_A(y)]
    -
    \sigma_A([x,y])
\]
has order zero and hence lies in
$F_{\le0}\mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}\simeq B$.
Under the canonical one-sided action on $B$, this element acts as
\[
    [\rho_A(x),\rho_A(y)]-\rho_A([x,y]),
\]
which is zero because the anchor of a dg Lie algebroid is a morphism of dg Lie
algebras.  An order-zero element acts on $B$ by multiplication, so a zero
action forces $c(x,y)=0$.  This proves the bracket relation.  Compatibility
with the differential is inherited from the rectified degreewise Spencer
action and the dg convolution differential.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Cellwise enveloping-to-distribution morphism]
\label{con:as-cellwise-pbw-map}
Retain the symbol map of
Construction~\ref{con:as-cellwise-spencer-symbol}.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-binary-hurewicz-convolution}, it satisfies the dg
Lie--Rinehart bracket, scalar, and differential relations in the associative
$B$-bimodule algebra
$\mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}$.  The universal property of the
bimodule enveloping algebra therefore gives a unique filtered morphism of
$E_1$-algebras
\[
\boxed{
    \Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a_A)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}.
}
\]
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Naturality of the cellwise PBW map]
\label{prop:as-cellwise-pbw-natural}
The morphisms $\Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}$ are natural in Artinian cell
morphisms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first-order symbol, the two-marked cotangent primitive, the anchor map,
and completed Cech convolution are natural in augmented Artinian arrows.
Thus an Artinian morphism gives a commutative square of first-order symbol
maps, and Proposition~\ref{prop:as-binary-hurewicz-convolution} identifies
both squares with the corresponding dg Lie--Rinehart relations.  The
universal property of the bimodule enveloping algebra carries this square to
the corresponding square of filtered $E_1$-algebras.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cellwise rational PBW--Spencer equivalence]
\label{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer-cell}
Assume $L_{B/A}$ is perfect and that the rectified dg cell lies in the dg PBW
model range.  Then
\[
\boxed{
    \Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a_A)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}
}
\]
is a filtered equivalence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
PBW gives
\[
    \operatorname{gr}U(\mathfrak a_A)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}_B
    \left(
        \mathfrak a_A^{\mathrm{und}}
    \right).
\]
For the Koszul-dual Artinian cell,
\[
    \mathfrak a_A^{\mathrm{und}}
    \simeq
    (L_{B/A})^\vee,
\]
while
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-spencer-symbol} gives the same associated
graded for the distribution algebra.  The degree-one map is the identity
under this identification.  Multiplicativity therefore makes every
homogeneous symmetric degree an equivalence.  Induction on the finite order
quotients proves that $\Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}$ is a filtered
equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Controller-level PBW--Spencer morphism]
\label{con:as-controller-pbw-map}
For a rational dg controller $\mathfrak a$ in the dg representation range,
naturality of the cellwise maps gives a canonical morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \Theta_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}.
}
\]
Indeed, the Koszul-dual cell density theorem gives the canonical density
diagram
\[
    \operatorname*{colim}_{(\mathfrak a_A\to\mathfrak a)}
    \mathfrak a_A
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathfrak a.
\]
The enveloping functor is a left adjoint in the dg representation range and
therefore preserves this colimit, while
$\mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}$ is the corresponding left
Kan extension of the cell distribution kernels.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Controller-level PBW under explicit perfect-cell cofinality]
\label{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer}
Let $\mathfrak a$ be a rational dg controller in the dg representation range.
Assume that the full subcategory of Artinian cells
\[
    \mathfrak a_A\longrightarrow\mathfrak a
\]
for which $L_{B/A}$ is perfect and the cell lies in the dg PBW model range is
cofinal in the Artinian-cell density diagram.  Then
\[
\boxed{
    \Theta_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}
}
\]
is a filtered equivalence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By the cofinality hypothesis both sides are computed by the same colimit over
the indicated perfect cells.  On each such cell,
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer-cell} is a filtered equivalence.
Colimits preserve equivalences, giving the result.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the ordinary PBW formula]
The controller-level perfect-cell cofinality condition is a theorem
hypothesis, not additional structure retained on $\mathfrak a$.  The
intrinsic pro-coherent symbol
\[
    \left(
        \operatorname{Sym}^r L_{B/A}
    \right)^\vee
\]
exists without it.  What perfectness permits is the further ordinary formula
\[
    \left(
        \operatorname{Sym}^r L_{B/A}
    \right)^\vee
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^r
    \left(
        L_{B/A}^\vee
    \right)
\]
and hence the classical point-set PBW comparison.  No ``finite-duality
package'' or presentation is attached to an algebroid.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Compatibility of the PBW module bar with the universal Spencer action]
\label{prop:as-pbw-action-compatibility}
Let $A\to B$ satisfy the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer-cell}.  Under the filtered
equivalence
\[
    \Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a_A)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}},
\]
the canonical one-sided distribution module $B$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-canonical-distribution-module} identifies with the
usual $U(\mathfrak a_A)$-module $B$ defined by the dg anchor.  Consequently
the free module bars and their relative $R$-linear coduals identify.  Under
the Cartan representation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation}, the resulting underlying
cochain equivalence is equivariant, and the induced
$U(\mathfrak a_A)$-representation on the completed Spencer object is the
restriction of the rectified tangent-enveloping action
$\mu^{\mathrm{Sp}}$.

If the controller-level PBW equivalence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer} applies, the same compatibility
holds after the canonical Artinian-cell left Kan reconstruction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The weight-one component of $\Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}$ is the symbol map of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-first-graded-comparison}.  On $B$, the source-unit
evaluation defining the one-sided distribution action sends a first-order
symbol to its derivation on the target scalar coefficient.  Under the
represented first-order anchor comparison this is precisely the anchor action
of $\mathfrak a_A$ on $B$.  The exact directional bidegree theorem identifies
the commutator of two such first-order symbols with the binary dg
Lie-algebroid bracket, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-leibniz} identifies the anchor--scalar relation.
Therefore the restricted $U(\mathfrak a_A)$-module structure on $B$ is the
ordinary enveloping module determined by the dg representation.

Functoriality of the free module bar carries the PBW equivalence to an
equivalence of the two bar resolutions.  Relative $R$-linear module coduality
then identifies their underlying Spencer/CE cochain objects.  The Cartan
Lie-derivative action of Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation}
and the anchor/bracket relations of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-binary-hurewicz-convolution} make this identification
equivariant.  The rectified tangent monad is
the enveloping-module monad on the stated dg representation range, so this is
exactly the rectified tangent-enveloping action.  Naturality of the cellwise
PBW maps and the universal property of left Kan extension give the
controller-level statement whenever the controller-level PBW equivalence is
available.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Integral statement]
Integrally the constructed enveloping object is the tangent monad
$\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}$ with its weight filtration and its action on
formalized Spencer representations.  The finite-order principal-parts
construction independently gives the filtered internal kernel algebra
$\mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}$.  Their identification with an
ordinary bimodule enveloping algebra is asserted only in the explicit
rational dg PBW range above.
\end{remark}

\section{Coherent operator-valued Cech transport}
\label{sec:as-operator-mc}

The operator-valued Maurer--Cartan system begins with complete Cartan
transport.  The raw and formalized scalar transports are related by
restriction along the geometric relation map, in the same direction as the
formalized-to-raw Cartan cobar comparison.

\begin{construction}[Raw scalar Cartan transport]
\label{con:as-raw-operator-transport}
The canonical scalar Cartan structure gives, for every $n\geq1$, an
equivalence over $\mathfrak G_n$
\[
\boxed{
    \theta_n^{\mathrm{raw}}:
    (p_0^{(n)})^*\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    (p_n^{(n)})^*\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}.
}
\]
Applying the Cartan cobar and stable normalization gives the same coherent
transport on
$\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Cart},\bullet}
(\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})$.
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Formalized scalar Cartan transport]
\label{con:as-formalized-operator-transport}
The coefficient
\[
    \mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
    \simeq
    (q^{\mathrm{fm}})^*
    \mathcal O_{Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}}^{\mathrm{add}}
\]
has its canonical $J_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$-coalgebra structure.
Hence, for every $n\geq1$,
\[
\boxed{
    \theta_n^{\mathrm{fm}}:
    (p_0^{(n)})^*\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    (p_n^{(n)})^*\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}
}
\]
over $R_{\mathfrak G,n}^{\mathrm{fm}}$, and the same transport acts on the
formalized scalar Cartan Spencer cobar.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Formalized-to-raw operator-valued Cech cocycle comparison]
\label{thm:as-integral-mc}
The families
$\{\theta_n^{\mathrm{raw}}\}$ and $\{\theta_n^{\mathrm{fm}}\}$ satisfy the
complete Cech cocycle identities.  Pullback of
$\theta_n^{\mathrm{fm}}$ along
\[
    \lambda^R_{\mathfrak G,n}:
    \mathfrak G_n\longrightarrow R_{\mathfrak G,n}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
is canonically $\theta_n^{\mathrm{raw}}$.  Under the source-fixed decalage
theorem, this equality is the transport-level form of
\[
    \kappa_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Cobar}}:
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}})
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Cobar}_{\mathfrak G}^{\bullet}
    (\mathcal O_X^{\mathrm{add}}).
\]
Thus the formalized complete operator-valued Cech cocycle restricts
canonically to the raw cocycle.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Both transport families are descent data obtained by pulling back the scalar
coefficient from the corresponding quotient.  Their cocycle identities are
the Cartan coalgebra identities.  Since
$q^{\mathrm{fm}}=\ell q$, pulling formalized quotient transport back along the
raw relation is the same endpoint identification as raw quotient transport.
Taking right-adjoint mates gives the comonad morphism
$J^{\mathrm{fm}}\to J$, and hence the formalized-to-raw Cartan cobar map.
The source-fixed decalage comparison identifies these two descriptions in
every degree.
\end{proof}

\section{Normalized monadic Spencer twisting systems}
\label{sec:as-twisting}

The integral twisting construction is made from the tangent monad and its
representation bar.  No augmentation of the finite-order distribution
algebra is assumed: for a nonzero anchor, evaluation of a differential
operator at the unit is not multiplicative and therefore does not define an
algebra augmentation to $B$.

\begin{definition}[One-marked tangent bar of a representation]
\label{def:as-tangent-marked-bar}
Let $T=\mathbb U_{\mathfrak a}^{\pi}$ and let $M$ be a $T$-algebra.  Define
the simplicial one-marked monadic bar
\[
\boxed{
    B_n^{\mathrm{tan}}(\mathfrak a;M):=T^{n+1}M.
}
\]
The nonterminal faces are induced by monad multiplication, the terminal face
by the $T$-algebra action $TM\to M$, and degeneracies by the monad unit.
Give every degree the filtration by the total number of unmarked positive
$\mathfrak a$-inputs.  This construction is made only for a full
representation; the pre-action latching system of
Section~\ref{sec:as-curvature} is the action-independent construction used
for curvature and flatness obstructions.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Stable simplicial chain normalization convention]
\label{def:as-stable-chain-normalization}
Let $\mathcal C$ be a stable $\infty$-category.  Using the pointed indexing
category $\Xi_{\geq0}$ fixed in Chapter~10, define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\mathcal C)
    :=
    \operatorname{Fun}_*(\Xi_{\geq0},\mathcal C).
}
\]
Stable Dold--Kan gives a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    N_\bullet:
    \operatorname{Fun}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal C)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\mathcal C).
}
\]
For a simplicial object, the image of the chain differential in the homotopy
category is the alternating face sum
\[
    \sum_i(-1)^i d_i.
\]
For $M\in\mathcal C$, write $M[0]$ for the chain object concentrated in chain
degree zero.  The chain degree introduced here is distinct from any internal
stable or dg grading in $\mathcal C$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Normalized one-marked Spencer bar]
\label{def:as-reduced-principal-bar}
Define
\[
\boxed{
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    :=
    N_\bullet B_\bullet^{\mathrm{tan}}(\mathfrak a;M)
    \in
    \operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}
    \left(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B\right).
}
\]
Let
\[
    W_{\le r}
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
\]
be the chain subobject induced by total unmarked tangent weight at most $r$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Exhaustivity of the one-marked bar weight filtration]
\label{prop:as-one-marked-bar-exhaustive}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    W_{\le r}
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M).
}
\]
No assertion that the bar is the inverse limit of its finite-weight
quotients is made.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The filtered tangent monad is the colimit of its finite marked-weight stages
by
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-filtered-enveloping-monad}.  Iterating the monad in a
fixed simplicial degree preserves the induced exhaustive filtration because
each operation has finite arity and finite total unmarked weight.  Stable
normalization is formed from finite limits and colimits degreewise and
therefore commutes with the filtered colimit.  This gives the displayed
equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Integral Spencer twisting system]
\label{def:as-operator-associative-bar}
The $T$-algebra action on $M$ evaluates every terminal face of the one-marked
bar.  Together with monad multiplication it defines the canonical simplicial
bar augmentation
\[
    T^{n+1}M\longrightarrow M
\]
in every degree.  Stable chain normalization gives a morphism in
$\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)$,
\[
\boxed{
    \tau_{\mathfrak a,M}^{\mathrm{Sp}}:
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \longrightarrow M[0].
}
\]
Its restriction to
$W_{\le r}\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$ is the finite-weight
normalized action relation through weight $r$.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Bar identity for the integral twisting system]
\label{thm:as-twisting-identity}
The morphism
$\tau_{\mathfrak a,M}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$ satisfies the normalized monadic bar
identity.  In weight two the identity contains the curvature boundary
formula; in weight three it contains the Bianchi coherence; in arbitrary
finite weight it is the PD representation relation determined by the
filtered tangent monad.  The construction is natural in morphisms of
algebroids and representations and compatible with coherent spectral-etale
base change.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The unnormalized maps are the augmentation of the simplicial bar of a monad
algebra.  The simplicial identities are exactly the monad unit,
multiplication, and algebra-action identities.  Stable normalization carries
them to the normalized bar identity.  The weight filtration is preserved by
monad multiplication, so every identity has a finite-weight decomposition.
The weight-two latching boundary is the curvature obstruction of
Section~\ref{sec:as-curvature}; the next contraction identity is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-bianchi}.  In arbitrary weight the multiweight operation
theorem identifies the normalized components with the PD representation
relations.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Universal formalized Spencer twisting systems]
\label{def:as-twisting}
For the universal formalized scalar Spencer representation define
\[
\boxed{
    \tau_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}
    :=
    \tau_{
        \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty},
        \mathbf{Sp}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    }^{\mathrm{Sp}},
}
\]
applied degreewise in the Spencer cochain object.  The formalized adjoint
representation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-differentiated-adjoint-representation} similarly gives
\[
\boxed{
    \tau_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm}}
    :=
    \tau_{
        \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty},
        \mathbf{Ad}_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,\mathrm{fm}}
    }^{\mathrm{Sp}}.
}
\]
No twisting system for the independently constructed actual-isotropy
controller as an ambient
$\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$-representation is asserted without
a separate comparison theorem.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Presentation independence and geometric separation]
\label{prop:as-raw-twisting-comparison}
The universal twisting systems retain no chosen Artinian chain,
prorepresenting algebra, operator-algebra augmentation, or completion
presentation.  On every directional multi-square-zero Artinian test, the
exact multiweight cotangent operation evaluates to the corresponding finite
weight component of
$\tau_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,fm}}$ under the universal Spencer
representation.  Separately, pullback along
$\lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}$ carries the formalized Cartan transport underlying
the support-marked scalar system to the raw Cartan transport of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-mc}.  No map from a support-marked geometric cell
to a directional PD operation is asserted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The twisting system is constructed directly from the tangent monad and its
algebra action, so it is intrinsic in the controller and representation.
Corollary~\ref{cor:as-integral-pd-arity} identifies the exact directional
multiweights with the partition-Lie operations, and the universal Spencer
representation evaluates those operations by the corresponding normalized
components of the monadic bar.  The support-marked geometric branch is
independent; its raw/formalized transport statement is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-integral-mc}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why no integral associative bar is used]
The finite-order distribution object
$\mathbb D_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}}$ is an $E_1$-algebra in the
two-sided kernel category, but for a nonzero anchor it has no canonical
augmentation to $B$; evaluation at $1$ is not multiplicative as a map of
kernel algebras.  The one-sided coefficient action and free distribution
module bar of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-cellwise-operator-action} nevertheless exist and recover
completed Artinian Cech cochains by relative $R$-linear coduality.  They are not an
augmented-algebra bar and do not replace the tangent monad as the intrinsic
integral representation controller.  The integral twisting system is
therefore the tangent-monadic bar construction above.  After rational PBW
rectification, the tangent monad becomes the ordinary enveloping-module monad
and the same construction becomes the familiar dg bar evaluation system; its
Maurer--Cartan twisting cochain is obtained below by suspended
positive-corolla corestriction.
\end{remark}

\section{Rational CE--Spencer and Maurer--Cartan comparison}
\label{sec:as-rational-mc}

Work in the rational locally coherent coefficient and boundedness range in
which the dg Lie-algebroid representation theorem applies.  When a literal
ordinary dual of a symmetric coalgebra is used, the relevant cotangent
coefficient will be assumed perfect.  The controller-level CE--Spencer
comparison constructed below uses stabilized module Koszul duality and does
not require that ordinary perfectness condition.

\begin{definition}[dg Chevalley--Eilenberg Spencer resolution]
\label{def:as-ce-spencer-resolution}
Let $\mathfrak a$ be a dg Lie algebroid over $B/R$ in the dg representation
range and let
\[
    U(\mathfrak a)
\]
be its bimodule enveloping algebra.  The anchor gives $B$ its canonical
one-sided $U(\mathfrak a)$-module structure.  Write
\[
    T_{U(\mathfrak a)}(M)
    :=
    U(\mathfrak a)\otimes_{B,t}M
\]
for the corresponding module monad and define the simplicial free module bar
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{U(\mathfrak a)}(B)_n
    :=
    T_{U(\mathfrak a)}^{\,n+1}(B).
}
\]
Every term is the free $U(\mathfrak a)$-module on
$T_{U(\mathfrak a)}^n(B)$; the nonterminal faces use multiplication, the
terminal face uses the anchor module action on $B$, and the degeneracies use
the unit.

The module monad is canonically $R$-linear, not source-$B$-linear in general.
Define the underlying relative Chevalley--Eilenberg Spencer cochain object by
$R$-linear module coduality,
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE},R}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak a)
    :=
    N^\bullet
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        U(\mathfrak a)\text{-}\mathrm{mod}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{U(\mathfrak a)}(B)_\bullet,
        B
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{Mod}_R).
}
\]
On the dg representation range, the standard enveloping-module/Lie-bar
comparison gives a canonical equivalent model of underlying $R$-linear
cochain objects
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE},R}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak a)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Hom}_B
    \left(
        \operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a),
        B
    \right).
}
\]
No algebra augmentation $U(\mathfrak a)\to B$ is used; the terminal bar face
uses the one-sided module action determined by the anchor.

Let
$W_{\le r}\operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)$
be the finite primitive-word-length stage of the Lie bar.  Its $B$-linear
codual
\[
    \operatorname{Hom}_B
    \left(
        W_{\le r}\operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a),
        B
    \right)
\]
is regarded for the moment as an $R$-linear coherent cochain object.  The
Cartan representation structure constructed next preserves these finite
stages.  The index $r$ is primitive-word length; it is not a truncation in
Cech or cosimplicial degree.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Cartan representation on CE Spencer cochains]
\label{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation}
Put
\[
    C_\bullet^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
    :=
    \operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a).
\]
For every homogeneous local section $x$ of $\mathfrak a$ there is a canonical
coderivation
\[
    \operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_x:
    C_\bullet^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
    \longrightarrow
    C_\bullet^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
\]
of degree $|x|$, characterized on scalar coefficients by the anchor derivation
$\rho(x)$ and on suspended primitive coefficients by the suspension of
$y\mapsto[x,y]$, with the homological Koszul signs determined by the fixed
bar-suspension convention.  It preserves primitive word length.

For a homogeneous $B$-linear cochain $f$ define the Lie derivative
\[
\boxed{
    (\mathcal L_xf)(c)
    :=
    \rho(x)(f(c))
    -
    (-1)^{|x||f|}
    f\left(\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_x(c)\right).
}
\]
These operators satisfy
\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
    \mathcal L_x(bf)
    &=
    \rho(x)(b)f
    +(-1)^{|x||b|}b\mathcal L_xf,\\
    [\mathcal L_x,\mathcal L_y]
    &=
    \mathcal L_{[x,y]},\\
    [d,\mathcal L_x]
    &=
    \mathcal L_{dx},
\end{aligned}}
\]
with graded commutators.  The normalized CE differential is equivariant for
this action.  Hence the underlying cochain object
$\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE},R}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak a)$ carries a
canonical dg Lie-algebroid representation structure; define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak a)
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \left(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)\right)
}
\]
to be this Cartan-enhanced object.  Likewise define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{CE}^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak a,\le r}
    :=
    \operatorname{Hom}_B
    \left(
        W_{\le r}\operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a),
        B
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \left(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)\right)
}
\]
with the restricted Cartan action.  Forgetting the representation recovers
$\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE},R}^{\bullet}$ and its finite
primitive-weight stages.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The adjoint action of a dg Lie algebroid on itself is compatible with the
anchor: the Lie--Rinehart Leibniz identity says that $\operatorname{ad}_x$ is
a derivation over $\rho(x)$.  Therefore the action on scalars and primitives
extends uniquely through the cofree Lie-bar coalgebra to the displayed
coderivation.  Jacobi gives
$[\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_x,
\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_y]
=\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_{[x,y]}$, while the dg compatibility of the
bracket gives
$[d,\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_x]
=\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}_{dx}$.  The bar differential is assembled
from the internal differential, bracket, and anchor, so the same identities
show that this coderivation action is compatible with the complete bar
differential and preserves word length.

The displayed formula for $\mathcal L_x$ is the standard induced action on a
mapping object from a module carrying the adjoint action to the scalar module
carrying the anchor action.  Expanding it on $bc$ and using the
Lie--Rinehart Leibniz identity gives the first displayed formula.  The second
and third follow by expanding graded commutators and using the corresponding
identities for $\operatorname{ad}^{\mathrm{bar}}$ and for the anchor.  Thus
$x\mapsto\mathcal L_x$ is a dg Lie--Rinehart action, equivalently a one-sided
$U(\mathfrak a)$-module structure in every CE cochain degree.  Compatibility
with the bar differential makes the normalized CE differential a morphism of
representations.  Since word length is preserved, every finite stage is a
subrepresentation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Primitive-weight completeness of CE cochains]
\label{prop:as-ce-weight-complete}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak a)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \lim_r
    \mathbf{CE}^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak a,\le r}.
}
\]
No pronilpotence or perfectness hypothesis is required beyond the stated dg
representation range.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The dg Lie bar is the exhaustive filtered colimit of its finite
primitive-word-length stages:
\[
    \operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
    \simeq
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    W_{\le r}\operatorname{Bar}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a).
\]
The Cartan coderivations preserve primitive word length by
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation}.  Applying the $B$-linear
mapping object into $B$ converts the exhaustive bar colimit to an inverse
limit on underlying $R$-linear cochains.  Limits of dg Lie-algebroid
representations are created in the underlying module category, so the same
comparison is an inverse-limit equivalence in
$\operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a))$.
The standard module-bar/Lie-bar comparison is compatible with the
primitive-word-length filtration, giving the displayed CE Spencer cochain
object.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Degreewise completed Cech principal-parts calculation}

Retain the integral higher-Cech notation and results of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-higher-cech-cotangent},
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-higher-cech-complete-aft},
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-higher-cech-associated-graded}, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-iterated-completed-cech-coring}.  Rational
rectification carries these complete filtered Cech terms, their faces and
degeneracies, and their associated-graded additive primitive coordinates to
the corresponding dg constructions.

\begin{construction}[Completed filtered formal-leaf Spencer cochains]
\label{con:as-completed-artinian-spencer}
Retain the completed Artinian Cech--Spencer object
$\widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:as-completed-artinian-cech-spencer}.  We now construct the
completed object for a formal leaf rather than merely using notation for it.
For $r\geq0$ and Cech degree $n$, put
\[
    P_{A,\le r}^{[n]}
    :=
    F^0P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}/F^{r+1}P_A^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}.
\]
Stable scalar sections and normalization assemble these finite adic quotients,
as $A\to B$ varies over the Artinian test category of a formal leaf $F$, into
a cochain object
\[
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{Art},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \bigl(\operatorname{Sp}(\mathcal P_F)\bigr).
\]
Define the finite completed formal-leaf stage by Schlessinger localization
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
    :=
    L_F^{\mathrm{Sch,st}}
    \left(
        \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{Art},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
    \right)
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \bigl(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)\bigr).
}
\]
Finally define
\[
\boxed{
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    :=
    \lim_r
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
}
\]
in the stable cochain category
$\operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F))$.

The order of construction is deliberate: finite adic quotients are formed
first, then Schlessinger localization, and only then the inverse limit.  No
claim that the localization functor commutes with an arbitrary inverse limit
is required.
\end{construction}

\subsection{Cellwise CE comparison}

\begin{definition}[Rectified distribution-module bar]
\label{def:as-distribution-module-bar}
Let $A\to B$ be a rational Artinian cell and put
\[
    \mathbb D_A
    :=
    \mathbb D_{A/B}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin,dg}}.
\]
Rational rectification of
Construction~\ref{con:as-kernel-module-action} gives an action
$\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}$ of the rectified kernel category on
$\operatorname{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{dg}}_B$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-canonical-distribution-module} gives $B$ its
canonical one-sided $\mathbb D_A$-module structure.  Let
\[
    T_{\mathbb D_A}(M)
    :=
    \mathbb D_A\star_B^{\mathrm{mod}}M,
    \qquad
    \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D_A}
    :=
    \operatorname{Alg}_{T_{\mathbb D_A}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{dg}}_B
    \right).
\]
Define the simplicial free module bar
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B)_n
    :=
    T_{\mathbb D_A}^{\,n+1}(B),
    \qquad n\geq0.
}
\]
The nonterminal faces use multiplication of $\mathbb D_A$, the terminal face
uses the one-sided module action on $B$, and the degeneracies use the unit.
No algebra morphism $\mathbb D_A\to B$ in the two-sided kernel category is
used.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cech--distribution relative module-bar duality]
\label{lem:as-cech-distribution-bar-duality}
Let $A\to B$ be a rational Artinian cell.  There is a canonical filtered
equivalence of coherent cochain objects in $\operatorname{Mod}_R$
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    N^\bullet
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{
        \operatorname{Mod}^{\mathrm{src}}_{\mathbb D_A}}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B)_\bullet,
        B
    \right),
}
\]
where
$\widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}$
is the rational dg rectification of
Definition~\ref{def:as-completed-artinian-cech-spencer}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The integral statement is
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-cellwise-operator-action}.  Rational symmetric-monoidal
rectification preserves finite principal-parts quotients, relative tensor
products, the right-left pro-coherent completion, the kernel module action,
and the source-unit module map.  It therefore carries the integral free
module bar to
$\operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B)$ and carries the relative
$R$-linear enrichment and continuous source coduality to their dg
counterparts.  Rectifying the degreewise
equivalence and then applying stable normalization gives the displayed
filtered equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Cellwise CE--Spencer comparison map]
\label{con:as-cellwise-ce-spencer-map}
Let $A\to B$ be a rational Artinian cell in the dg representation range and
put
\[
    \mathfrak a_A:=D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(A).
\]
The cellwise PBW construction gives the natural filtered algebra morphism
\[
    \Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a_A)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbb D_A.
\]
Restriction of scalars carries the canonical one-sided $\mathbb D_A$-module
