$B$ to a $U(\mathfrak a_A)$-module.  By the weight-one anchor comparison this
restricted module is the usual $U(\mathfrak a_A)$-module $B$.

Functoriality of the free module construction gives a simplicial morphism of
free $U(\mathfrak a_A)$-modules
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{U(\mathfrak a_A)}(B)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Res}_{\Theta_A}
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B).
}
\]
There are therefore canonical maps of cosimplicial $R$-modules
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{\mathbb D_A\text{-}\mathrm{mod}}
\left(
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B),B
\right)\\
&\qquad\longrightarrow
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{U(\mathfrak a_A)\text{-}\mathrm{mod}}
\left(
    \operatorname{Res}_{\Theta_A}
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\mathbb D_A}(B),B
\right)\\
&\qquad\longrightarrow
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}^R_{U(\mathfrak a_A)\text{-}\mathrm{mod}}
\left(
    \operatorname{Bar}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{U(\mathfrak a_A)}(B),B
\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
The first map forgets $\mathbb D_A$-linearity to
$U(\mathfrak a_A)$-linearity and the second is precomposition with the
displayed free-bar morphism.  Apply stable normalization.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-distribution-bar-duality} the source is the completed
Cech Spencer object, while
Definition~\ref{def:as-ce-spencer-resolution} identifies the target with the
CE Spencer resolution.  Thus, after forgetting the Cartan action on the CE side, we obtain the
canonical filtered $R$-linear cochain map
\[
\boxed{
    \Phi_A:
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \longrightarrow
    U_R
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^{\bullet}
    \left(
        D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(A)
    \right),
}
\]
where $U_R$ forgets the dg Lie-algebroid representation to its underlying
$R$-linear cochain object.  On the PBW cell scope, the tangent-enveloping
representation on the source and the Cartan representation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation} make this the underlying
map of a representation morphism.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Cellwise complete bar reconstruction]
\label{thm:as-cellwise-ce-reconstruction}
Assume $L_{B/A}$ is perfect and the rectified cell lies in the ordinary dg
PBW and CE representation ranges.  Then
\[
\boxed{
    \Phi_A:
    \operatorname{Res}_R^B
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{A,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    U_R
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^{\bullet}
    \left(
        D_{B/R}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(A)
    \right)
}
\]
is a filtered equivalence of $R$-linear cochain objects.  With the source
endowed with the PBW-restricted tangent representation, it is the underlying
map of a filtered equivalence of dg Lie-algebroid representations.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer-cell},
\[
    \Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}:
    U(\mathfrak a_A)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb D_A
\]
is a filtered equivalence of bimodule algebras.  Restriction along an
equivalence identifies their one-sided module categories and the canonical
module $B$.  Hence the free module-bar morphism of
Construction~\ref{con:as-cellwise-ce-spencer-map} is a filtered equivalence
in every simplicial degree.  Relative $R$-linear internal Hom into $B$ preserves this equivalence.
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-cech-distribution-bar-duality} identifies the distribution
side with the restricted completed Cech Spencer object, and
Definition~\ref{def:as-ce-spencer-resolution} identifies the enveloping side
with the underlying relative CE Spencer resolution.  By
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-pbw-action-compatibility} the source representation
is the PBW restriction of the tangent-enveloping action, while
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation} identifies the target
representation as the Cartan Lie-derivative action.  The first-order anchor
and binary Lie--Rinehart relations agree by
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-binary-hurewicz-convolution}; hence the resulting
bar equivalence is equivariant.  Its underlying $R$-linear map is exactly
$\Phi_A$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Stable square-zero tangent density]
\label{lem:as-stable-square-zero-detection}
Let $F$ be a rational formal-moduli problem in the dg comparison range and put
\[
    \mathfrak a=\operatorname{Diff}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(F).
\]
Write
\[
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm,dg}}(F)
    :=
    \operatorname{Sp}
    \left(
        (\operatorname{FMP}^{\mathrm{dg}}_{B/R})_{/F}
    \right),
\]
and let
\[
    F_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}:
    \operatorname{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{dg}}_B
    \rightleftarrows
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a):
    U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
\]
be the rectified tangent free--forgetful adjunction.

For coherent $B$-modules $M$ and $k\in\mathbb Z$, put
\[
    G(M,k)
    :=
    F_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}(M^\vee[k]).
\]
Then:

\begin{enumerate}
    \item the objects $G(M,k)$ are compact and jointly generate
    $\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)$;

    \item if
    \[
        \mathcal G_{\mathfrak a}
        :=
        \operatorname{Thick}^{\natural}
        \left\{
            G(M,k)
        \right\}_{M,k}
    \]
    is their idempotent-complete finite stable closure, then
    \[
    \boxed{
        \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
        \simeq
        \operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal G_{\mathfrak a});
    }
    \]

    \item restricted spectral Yoneda
    \[
    \boxed{
        \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
        \longrightarrow
        \operatorname{Fun}
        \left(
            \mathcal G_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{op}},
            \operatorname{Sp}
        \right)
    }
    \]
    is fully faithful;

    \item a morphism in $\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)$ is an
    equivalence if and only if it induces equivalences on mapping spectra out
    of every $G(M,k)$.
\end{enumerate}

Transport through the tangent differentiation equivalence gives the same
statements in $\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm,dg}}(F)$ for
\[
\boxed{
    \left(D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}\right)^{-1}G(M,k).
}
\]
Under affine formal-moduli recognition these are the stabilized tangent
images of the coherent structured square-zero Schlessinger generators.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The pro-coherent coefficient category
$\operatorname{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{dg}}_B$ is compactly generated by the
continuous duals $M^\vee[k]$ of coherent modules and their shifts.  Tangent
monadicity makes
$U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}$ conservative and
sifted-colimit-preserving, hence filtered-colimit-preserving.

For a filtered diagram $(E_\lambda)$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Map}
\left(
    G(M,k),
    \operatorname*{colim}_\lambda E_\lambda
\right)
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}
\left(
    M^\vee[k],
    U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
    \operatorname*{colim}_\lambda E_\lambda
\right)\\
&\simeq
\operatorname{Map}
\left(
    M^\vee[k],
    \operatorname*{colim}_\lambda
    U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}E_\lambda
\right)\\
&\simeq
\operatorname*{colim}_\lambda
\operatorname{Map}
\left(
    M^\vee[k],
    U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}E_\lambda
\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus every $G(M,k)$ is compact.

Suppose $E$ is right-orthogonal to all $G(M,k)$.  By the free--forgetful
adjunction,
\[
    \operatorname{Map}
    \left(
        M^\vee[k],
        U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}E
    \right)
    \simeq0
\]
for every coherent $M$ and every $k$.  Since the $M^\vee[k]$ compactly
generate the pro-coherent coefficient category,
$U_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}E\simeq0$.  Conservativity gives
$E\simeq0$.  Hence the $G(M,k)$ are compact generators.

The standard compact-generation theorem now gives
\[
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a)
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal G_{\mathfrak a})
\]
and full faithfulness of restricted spectral Yoneda on the compact
idempotent-complete finite stable closure.  Joint detection of equivalences is
the same generator argument applied to the cofiber of a morphism.

Finally,
$D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}$ is an exact equivalence, so it transports compact
generators, their finite stable closure, restricted Yoneda, and detection.
The identification with stabilized structured square-zero generators is the
square-zero/free-cell calculation of affine formal-moduli recognition after
rational rectification and stabilization of the slice.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Rational CE--Spencer comparison by stabilized module Koszul duality]
\label{thm:as-rational-ce-spencer}
Let $F$ be a rational formal leaf in the coefficient, boundedness, and model
range of the dg Lie-algebroid representation theorem, and put
\[
    \mathfrak a
    :=
    \operatorname{Diff}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(F).
\]
Write
$\widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}$ for the rational
rectification of the completed formal-leaf object of
Construction~\ref{con:as-completed-artinian-spencer}, regarded as an object of
$\operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm,dg}}(F))$.
Then there is a canonical filtered equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
    \left(
        \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
    \right)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^{\bullet}
    \left(
        \mathfrak a
    \right).
}
\]
The equivalence is compatible with the dg algebroid action, the
primitive-weight filtration, and the universal bar-evaluation system.  On
every Artinian cell on which the ordinary PBW--Spencer comparison is an
equivalence, its restriction agrees, after forgetting to the underlying
relative $R$-linear cochain object, with the cellwise map $\Phi_A$ of
Construction~\ref{con:as-cellwise-ce-spencer-map}.  No perfectness or
perfect-cell cofinality condition is required for the controller-level
CE--Spencer equivalence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Rational rectification of the affine formal-moduli/partition-Lie equivalence
identifies the spectral and dg Artinian deformation contexts.  Slicing and
stabilization give the exact tangent equivalence
\[
    D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}:
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm,dg}}(F)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak a).
\]
On the stated dg representation range, the target is the category of
one-sided modules over the bimodule enveloping algebra $U(\mathfrak a)$.

Fix $r\geq0$.  Let
\[
    S_r
    :=
    D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
    \left(
        \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
    \right),
    \qquad
    C_r
    :=
    \mathbf{CE}^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak a,\le r}.
\]
Here the index $r$ on $S_r$ is finite adic/symmetric weight and the index on
$C_r$ is the matching CE primitive-word-length weight of
Definition~\ref{def:as-ce-spencer-resolution}; neither is a truncation in
cosimplicial degree.

We first construct a natural comparison on the compact tangent generators of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-stable-square-zero-detection}.  Let
\[
    G(M,k)
    =
    F_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}(M^\vee[k]).
\]
Under affine formal-moduli recognition, $G(M,k)$ is the tangent image of the
coherent structured square-zero generator with coefficient $M$ and shift
$k$.  Theorem~\ref{thm:as-spencer-colie-equals-colie} identifies the
Artinian Spencer comonad with the cotangent--square-zero coLie comonad as a
comonad, preserving counit, comultiplication, and anchor.  Rational
rectification preserves this comonad equivalence.  Hence, on every such
square-zero tangent generator, the source-fixed Spencer primitive and the dg
tangent primitive are identified together with every cobar coface and
codegeneracy.

The Cartan action of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation} is generated on the Lie
bar by the anchor action on scalars and the adjoint action on primitive
coefficients.  On a free tangent generator these are respectively the duals
of the relative anchor and of the binary cotangent cooperation, and the full
higher action is obtained from the same monad substitution maps.  The
Spencer--coLie/partition-Lie comparison preserves the anchor, binary
cooperation, and all PD substitution coherences.  Thus the primitive
comparison on every compact tangent generator is already natural in the dg
representation category, not merely after forgetting to $R$-modules.

The finite filtrations are compatible with this primitive comparison.  In
Cech degree $n$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-higher-cech-associated-graded} gives
\[
    \operatorname{Gr}
    F^\bullet P_{B_\alpha}^{[n],\mathrm{ad}}
    \simeq
    \operatorname{Sym}^{\mathrm{gr}}_B
    \left(
        L_{B/B_\alpha}^{\oplus n}(1)
    \right).
\]
On the rectified dg side, the cotangent--square-zero comonad gives the same
primitive coefficient and the same additive bar cooperation on
$L_{B/B_\alpha}^{\oplus n}$.  Therefore the maps between the finite
weight-$\le r$ stages have equivalent associated graded in every Cech degree
and commute with every face and degeneracy.  Since the filtration at stage
$r$ has only finitely many layers, induction through the successive
fibre/cofiber sequences gives an equivalence; no infinite complete-filtered
detection is required at this step.

Consequently, for every cochain degree $q\geq0$ and every $G(M,k)$ there is
a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Map}
    \left(G(M,k),S_r^q\right)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Map}
    \left(G(M,k),C_r^q\right),
}
\]
natural in $M$, $k$, and all morphisms between the free tangent cells:
under the free--forgetful adjunction such a morphism is adjoint to a
coefficient map into the tangent monad, and the rectified comonad/monad
comparison is natural in the coefficient and compatible with its unit and
multiplication.  Exactness extends this natural transformation from the free
generators to their idempotent-complete finite stable closure
$\mathcal G_{\mathfrak a}$.

By the full-faithfulness clause of
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-stable-square-zero-detection}, restricted spectral Yoneda
therefore reconstructs, for every $q$, a unique morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \Psi_r^q:S_r^q\longrightarrow C_r^q.
}
\]
The primitive comparison commutes with every Cech face and codegeneracy and
therefore with the normalized differential.  Hence, after mapping out of
every object of $\mathcal G_{\mathfrak a}$, the two composites
\[
    d_C\Psi_r^q,
    \qquad
    \Psi_r^{q+1}d_S:
    S_r^q\longrightarrow C_r^{q+1}
\]
agree.  Full faithfulness of restricted spectral Yoneda then identifies the
two composites themselves.  Thus the degreewise maps assemble to a unique
cochain morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \Psi_r^\bullet:
    S_r^\bullet\longrightarrow C_r^\bullet.
}
\]
For each $q$, the displayed mapping-spectrum comparison says that the cofiber
of $\Psi_r^q$ is right-orthogonal to every compact tangent generator.  The
generator clause of the same lemma forces that cofiber to vanish.  Hence every
$\Psi_r^q$ is an equivalence and $\Psi_r^\bullet$ is an equivalence of
cochain objects.

The cochain equivalences $\Psi_r^\bullet$ are compatible with $r\to r-1$: on the compact generators
this is functoriality of the adic quotient, primitive-word-length quotient,
and rectified comonad comparison; full faithfulness of restricted Yoneda then
gives compatibility globally.  Taking inverse limits and using that
$D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}$ is an equivalence, hence preserves limits, gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
\left(
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}
\right)
&\simeq
\lim_r
D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
\left(
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
\right)\\
&\xrightarrow{\sim}
\lim_r
\mathbf{CE}^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak a,\le r}\\
&\xrightarrow{\sim}
\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak a),
\end{aligned}
\]
where the final equivalence is
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-weight-complete}.

The order of construction remains finite adic quotient, then stabilized
Schlessinger localization, then inverse limit.  No commutation of the
localization functor with an arbitrary inverse limit is used.

Compatibility with the dg algebroid action and the bar-evaluation system is
already visible on the compact tangent generators: the comparison preserves
the comonad counit, comultiplication, anchor, and tangent action.  Density
extends that compatibility uniquely to the controller.  On an Artinian cell in the ordinary PBW range, finite Artinian cellular
induction reduces both comparisons to the same coherent square-zero
generators.  On those generators the comparison is the
restriction-of-scalars/free-module-bar map induced by
$\Theta_A^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}$, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-ce-cartan-representation} identifies its target
Cartan action.  Naturality of the attachment maps therefore identifies the
resulting representation map, after forgetting to relative $R$-linear
cochains, with $\Phi_A$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Raw Artinian CE comparison when formalization is trivial]
\label{cor:as-raw-rational-ce-spencer}
Assume
\[
    \ell_{\mathfrak G}:
    Q_{\mathfrak G}\xrightarrow{\sim}Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
\]
and let $F$ be a formal leaf in the dg representation range of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-ce-spencer}.  The raw relation then canonically
constructs finite-stage raw Artinian Spencer objects
\[
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
    \in
    \operatorname{CCh}_{\geq0}
    \left(\operatorname{Rep}^{\mathrm{fm}}(F)\right)
\]
and a completed raw Artinian object
\[
\boxed{
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}
    :=
    \lim_r
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}_{F,\le r}.
}
\]
There are canonical equivalences
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}_{F,\le r}
}
\]
for every $r$, hence
\[
\boxed{
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}.
}
\]
After rational rectification and tangent differentiation,
\[
\boxed{
    D_F^{\mathrm{Lie,lin}}
    \left(
        \widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F,\mathbb Q}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}
    \right)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathrm{CE}}^\bullet
    \left(\operatorname{Diff}^{\mathrm{Lie}}(F)\right).
}
\]
No CE functor is applied directly to the unlinearized raw parameterized
Cartan/Spencer object.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Because $\ell_{\mathfrak G}$ is an equivalence, pullback along it identifies
the Artinian mapping spaces into $Q_{\mathfrak G}$ and
$Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}$.  Equivalently, every Artinian point of the
formalized quotient has a contractible space of compatible raw lifts.  The
Artinian Cech test map of
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-artinian-cech-test-map} therefore factors
canonically through the raw relation, and postcomposition with
$\lambda^R_{\mathfrak G}$ recovers the formalized test map.

For an Artinian arrow $A\to B$, both raw and formalized test bars consequently
have the same represented Cech terms
\[
    P_A^{[n]}
    =
    \underbrace{B\otimes_A\cdots\otimes_AB}_{n+1}
\]
and the same faces and degeneracies.  Form the finite adic quotients in each
Cech degree, apply stable scalar sections and normalization, and then apply
the same stabilized Schlessinger reflector as in
Construction~\ref{con:as-completed-artinian-spencer}.  This defines
$\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{raw},\bullet}_{F,\le r}$ and gives the displayed
finite-stage equivalence.  Taking the inverse limit in the stable cochain
category gives the completed equivalence.  Rational rectification and the
exact tangent equivalence preserve it, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-ce-spencer} identifies the formalized side with
the displayed CE Spencer object.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Constructed Maurer--Cartan convolution completion}

The one-marked monadic bar itself is only exhaustively filtered.  The
canonical inverse-limit completion needed for an infinite transferred
Maurer--Cartan expression occurs after mapping out of that bar.  On the
rational dg comparison scope the rooted-tree decomposition of the same bar
also constructs the convolution $L_\infty$ operations in which the displayed
Maurer--Cartan series is evaluated.

\begin{definition}[Maurer--Cartan convolution object]
\label{def:as-mc-convolution}
For a representation $M$ of $\mathfrak a$, define the mapping spectrum in
the normalized chain category
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{
        \operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)}
    \left(
        \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M),
        M[0]
    \right).
}
\]
For $r\geq0$ put
\[
    \operatorname{Conv}_{\le r}^{\mathrm{Sp}}
    :=
    \operatorname{map}_{
        \operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)}
    \left(
        W_{\le r}
        \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M),
        M[0]
    \right)
\]
and define
\[
\boxed{
    F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r+1}
    :=
    \operatorname{fib}
    \left(
        \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
        \longrightarrow
        \operatorname{Conv}_{\le r}^{\mathrm{Sp}}
    \right).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Canonical completeness of Maurer--Cartan convolution]
\label{prop:as-mc-weight-complete}
There is a canonical equivalence
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \lim_r
    \operatorname{Conv}_{\le r}^{\mathrm{Sp}}.
}
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \lim_r
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)/
    F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r+1}.
}
\]
Every finite quotient sees only finitely many homogeneous transferred
operations.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-one-marked-bar-exhaustive} gives
\[
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \simeq
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    W_{\le r}
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M).
\]
Mapping out of a colimit converts it to a limit:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
&=
\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)}
\left(
    \operatorname*{colim}_r
    W_{\le r}\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}},
    M[0]
\right)\\
&\simeq
\lim_r
\operatorname{map}_{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee}_B)}
\left(
    W_{\le r}\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp}},
    M[0]
\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the first equivalence.  The second is the fibre definition of
$F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r+1}$.  By construction, a weight-$r$ truncation contains
only operations with at most $r$ unmarked positive inputs.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Rational rooted-tree convolution $L_\infty$ structure]
\label{con:as-rational-convolution-linfty}
Work after rational dg rectification on the coefficient/model range of the dg
representation theorem.  Let
$\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)$ denote the rectified
normalized chain object.  The mapping spectrum of
Definition~\ref{def:as-mc-convolution} rectifies to the dg mapping complex
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    :=
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{
        \operatorname{Ch}_{\geq0}
        (\operatorname{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{dg}}_B)}
    \left(
        \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M),
        M[0]
    \right).
}
\]
The manuscript uses homological dg grading.  For Maurer--Cartan degree
conventions write
\[
\boxed{
    \left(
        \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp,MC}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \right)^n
    :=
    \left(
        \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \right)_{-n}
}
\]
for the cohomological regrading of the rectified convolution complex.  The
weight filtration $F_{\mathrm{MC}}^\bullet$ is transported unchanged through
this regrading.

Every reduced one-marked rooted tree has a unique path from the distinguished
coefficient leaf to the root; call it the \emph{marked spine}.  An admissible
$n$-spinal cut is a choice of $n$ pairwise disjoint connected marked
components whose intersections with the marked spine are nonempty consecutive
segments.  Each selected component inherits a distinguished incoming marked
edge and a distinguished outgoing edge and is therefore again one-marked.
Every component disjoint from the marked spine is unmarked and is evaluated
by the fixed $\mathfrak a$-algebra structure.

The rooted-tree expansion of the one-marked tangent bar carries canonical cut
cooperations over these admissible spinal cuts.  For homogeneous
\[
    f_1,\ldots,f_n
    \in
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp,MC}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
\]
define
\[
\boxed{
    \ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}(f_1,\ldots,f_n)
}
\]
by evaluating the $n$ selected marked components by
$f_1,\ldots,f_n$, evaluating every off-spine component by the fixed
algebroid operations, and grafting the resulting outputs through the residual
outer marked tree.  Sum over the symmetric admissible spinal cuts with the
Koszul signs supplied by the dg suspension convention.  The unary operation
is the convolution differential.

The operation preserves the Maurer--Cartan filtration in the form
\[
\boxed{
    \ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}
    \left(
        F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r_1},\ldots,F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r_n}
    \right)
    \subseteq
    F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r_1+\cdots+r_n}.
}
\]
Hence every $\ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}$ extends uniquely to the inverse-limit
completion of Proposition~\ref{prop:as-mc-weight-complete}.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Convolution identities and strict dg comparison]
\label{prop:as-convolution-linfty-identities}
The operations
$\{\ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}\}_{n\ge1}$ satisfy the $L_\infty$ identities.  On
the CE--Spencer comparison scope of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-ce-spencer}, this complete convolution
$L_\infty$ algebra is canonically identified with the usual convolution
$L_\infty$ algebra of the dg Lie-algebroid bar and the given representation.
In a strict dg Lie model the only primitive Lie operations are the
differential and binary bracket, so the twisting equation has the strict form
\[
    d\theta+\frac12[\theta,\theta]=0.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A two-stage admissible spinal cut can be formed by cutting the inner marked
components first and then the residual marked tree, or in the opposite order.
The two resulting decompositions are identified by coassociativity of the
rooted-tree cut cooperations and associativity of PD substitution.  Components
disjoint from the marked spine are evaluated by the fixed algebra structure
in both decompositions, so they introduce no untyped convolution input.
Stable Dold--Kan and the dg suspension convention supply the Koszul-signed
differential terms.  The signed sum of the two-stage spinal decompositions is
therefore zero in every arity, which is precisely the family of $L_\infty$
identities.

Rational rectification carries the spectral partition-Lie operation system to
the shifted dg Lie-algebroid operation system.  The CE--Spencer comparison is
constructed from the same primitive cotangent and substitution maps and hence
intertwines admissible spinal cuts with the ordinary dg Lie-bar cut
cooperations.  Applying the representation action identifies the displayed
operations with the standard dg convolution operations.  For a strict dg Lie
model all higher primitive brackets vanish, leaving $\ell_1=d$ and
$\ell_2=[-,-]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{construction}[Canonical suspended-corolla convolution twisting element]
\label{con:as-convolution-twisting-element}
After rational rectification, retain the rooted-tree expansion used in
Construction~\ref{con:as-one-marked-tangent-filtration}.  Put
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal C_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk},+}(M)
    :=
    \bigoplus_{r\ge1}C_r(\mathfrak a;M),
}
\]
the positive-weight one-vertex marked-corolla coefficient.  A full
representation evaluates these corollas by a canonical map
\[
    a_{\mathrm{cor}}:
    \mathcal C_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk},+}(M)
    \longrightarrow M.
\]
Let
\[
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg},+}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    :=
    \operatorname{cofib}
    \left(
        W_{\le0}\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
        \longrightarrow
        \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \right).
\]
In the rational dg rooted-tree model, let $U_{\mathrm{gr}}$ denote the
forgetful functor from normalized dg chain objects to their underlying graded
objects.  Forget the normalized chain differential and retain that graded
object.  It is the sum over positive-weight reduced one-marked trees and
therefore has a canonical projection onto the one-vertex summand
\[
\boxed{
    q_{\mathrm{cor}}:
    U_{\mathrm{gr}}
    \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg},+}_{\mathfrak a}(M)
    \longrightarrow
    s\mathcal C_{\mathfrak a}^{\mathrm{mk},+}(M)
}
\]
in graded pro-coherent dg coefficients, where $s$ denotes homological bar
suspension.  This projection is a morphism of underlying graded objects; it
is not asserted to commute with the normalized chain differential.

Let
$\pi_+:\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}\to
\overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg},+}$ be the quotient.  Define the homological
twisting element to be the degree $-1$ graded morphism
\[
\boxed{
    \theta_{\mathfrak a,M}^{\mathrm{hom}}
    :=
    s^{-1}a_{\mathrm{cor}}
    \circ q_{\mathrm{cor}}
    \circ U_{\mathrm{gr}}(\pi_+)
    \in
    \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{
        \operatorname{Gr}(\operatorname{QC}^{\vee,\mathrm{dg}}_B)}
    \left(
        U_{\mathrm{gr}}
        \overline{\mathbb B}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M),
        U_{\mathrm{gr}}M
    \right)_{-1}.
}
\]
The underlying graded object of the dg mapping complex
$\operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)$ consists of precisely
such homogeneous graded maps.  Under the cohomological regrading of
Construction~\ref{con:as-rational-convolution-linfty}, the preceding element
therefore defines a canonical degree-one element
\[
\boxed{
    \theta_{\mathfrak a,M}
    \in
    \operatorname{Conv}^{\mathrm{Sp,MC}}_{\mathfrak a}(M)^1.
}
\]
The normalized monadic bar augmentation
$\tau_{\mathfrak a,M}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$ constructed integrally above is not
identified with $\theta_{\mathfrak a,M}$.  Rather, it is the coherent
iterated evaluation of arbitrary rooted trees generated by the corolla action
whose suspended corestriction is $\theta_{\mathfrak a,M}$.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Rational twisting cochain and Maurer--Cartan formula]
\label{thm:as-rational-mc-formula}
After rational dg rectification, the integral monadic Spencer bar evaluation
is the iterated relative dg bar evaluation generated by the canonical
suspended-corolla twisting cochain
$\theta=\theta_{\mathfrak a,M}$.  On the CE comparison scope of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-ce-spencer}, $\theta$ is the ordinary dg
Lie-algebroid twisting cochain.  In a strict dg Lie model it satisfies
\[
\boxed{
    d\theta+\frac12[\theta,\theta]=0.
}
\]
After homotopy transfer to an $L_\infty$ model, the complete convolution
object contains the canonically defined inverse-limit element
\[
\boxed{
    d\theta+
    \sum_{n\ge2}
    \frac1{n!}
    \ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}(\theta,\ldots,\theta),
}
\]
and the transferred twisting identity says that this element is zero.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
In the rational rooted-tree model, the differential of the normalized
one-marked bar is the sum of the internal dg differential and the edge
contractions induced by PD substitution and the representation action.  Its graded corestriction to the suspended one-vertex marked-corolla summand
is exactly $\theta^{\mathrm{hom}}$.  Because $q_{\mathrm{cor}}$ is only a
graded projection, the mapping-complex differential of
$\theta^{\mathrm{hom}}$ records precisely its failure to commute with the bar
differential; this is the $d\theta$ term.  The identity that the bar
differential squares to zero, projected to the one-corolla target and
decomposed by the number of marked spinal components, is therefore the
convolution twisting equation.
After the cohomological regrading this gives
\[
    d\theta+
    \sum_{n\ge2}\frac1{n!}
    \ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}(\theta,\ldots,\theta)=0
\]
at every finite weight.  The same rooted-tree evaluation reconstructs the
integral normalized bar augmentation
$\tau_{\mathfrak a,M}^{\mathrm{Sp}}$ by iterating the corolla action, so no
identification of that degree-zero bar evaluation with the degree-one twisting
cochain is made.

On the CE--Spencer comparison scope,
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-convolution-linfty-identities} identifies the
rooted-tree bar, its corolla corestriction, and its cut cooperations with the
ordinary dg Lie-algebroid bar data.  Hence $\theta$ becomes the ordinary dg
Lie-algebroid twisting cochain.  In a strict dg Lie model only the differential
and binary bracket contribute, giving
\[
    d\theta+\frac12[\theta,\theta]=0.
\]

After homotopy transfer the strict binary operation is replaced by the
transferred operations $\ell_n^{\mathrm{conv}}$, and characteristic-zero
symmetric normalization gives $1/n!$.  Modulo
$F_{\mathrm{MC}}^{r+1}$ only finitely many rooted-tree weights and hence
finitely many arities contribute.  The finite Maurer--Cartan equations are
compatible under restriction.  Proposition~\ref{prop:as-mc-weight-complete}
identifies their inverse limit with a unique element of the constructed
complete convolution object.  Since every finite truncation of the bar
identity vanishes, the inverse-limit element vanishes.  No pronilpotence datum
on the algebroid or representation is imposed.
\end{proof}

\section{Isotropy-exactness and Atiyah sequences}
\label{sec:as-isotropy-exactness}

The preceding chapter constructed
\[
    \gamma_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{st}},
    \qquad
    \gamma_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty},
    \qquad
    \gamma_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Lie}}
\]
from differentiated actual isotropy to the corresponding homotopy anchor
fibres.

\begin{definition}[Isotropy-exactness]
\label{def:as-isotropy-exact}
A formal Lie groupoid is \emph{isotropy-exact} in a specified coefficient
theory when its constructed actual-isotropy-to-anchor-fibre morphism is an
equivalence.  This is a property of a constructed map, not additional
structure.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Classical smooth isotropy calculation]
\label{prop:as-classical-isotropy-exact}
For an ordinary smooth Lie groupoid,
actual differentiated isotropy at a point is the kernel of the tangent anchor
on that fibre.  If this kernel is locally free, the fibrewise kernels glue to
the ordinary isotropy vector bundle.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The ordinary Lie algebroid is
$\ker(ds)|_{u(X)}$ and its anchor is the restriction of $dt$.  Tangent
vectors to the isotropy group are precisely those annihilated by both $ds$
and $dt$.  Hence they form the anchor kernel.  Local freeness makes these
fibrewise kernels a vector bundle and ordinary descent glues the
identification.
\end{proof}

\section{One-object formal groups}
\label{sec:as-one-object}

Let $G/S$ be a group object and let
\[
    \mu:=\mu_{G/S}^{\mathrm{mod}}
\]
be its formal identity group.  Put
$\mathfrak G=B_\bullet\mu$.

\begin{theorem}[One-object reduction]
\label{thm:as-one-object-reduction}
For $B_\bullet\mu$:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the raw relation is the reduced bar nerve of $\mu$;
    \item the raw scalar geometric Cech object is the scalar reduced-bar
    cochain object;
    \item the Cartan and cofree cobars are the corresponding translation
    cobars;
    \item formal isotropy is the whole group $\mu$;
    \item
    \[
        \mathfrak l_{B\mu}^{\mathrm{sp}}
        =
        \operatorname{Prim}^{\mathrm{der}}
        \left(
            \Sigma^\infty_{+,S}\mu
        \right)
    \]
    remains the independently constructed Hopf-derived primitive object;
    \item the raw exhaustive singleton-marked cells map to their geometric
    formalizations inside the support-marked closure; independently, the
    directional Artinian square-zero cross-effects and exact multiweight
    cotangent primitives identify with the complete operation system of
    $\mathbb A_{B\mu}^{\pi,E_\infty}$;
    \item nonlinear conjugation descends to the raw adjoint group; right
    direct image to the formalized quotient constructs the formalized adjoint
    representation and its comparison to the actual formal-isotropy stable
    coefficient;
    \item the complete translation cocycle restricts from formalized Cech
    transport to raw transport, and rationally the suspended positive-corolla
    corestriction of its monadic bar evaluation is the ordinary Lie-bar
    twisting cochain on the stated dg comparison scope.
\end{enumerate}
No equivalence between
$\mathfrak l_{B\mu}^{\mathrm{sp}}$ and
$\mathbb A_{B\mu}^{\pi,E_\infty}$, nor between the formalized adjoint
coefficient and the independently differentiated actual-isotropy controller,
is asserted without a separate comparison theorem.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Cech nerve of a one-object groupoid is the reduced bar nerve.  Apply the
geometric scalar Cech construction, the Cartan-cobar endpoint
correspondence, and the separated and support-marked constructions for the
nonlinear branch.  Apply the directional Artinian construction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-support-multiweight-identification}, the comonadic
Hurewicz comparison, and the multiweight cotangent theorem for the algebraic
operation branch.  Conjugation is the one-object specialization of the formal
adjoint transport, so
Construction~\ref{con:as-formalized-adjoint-group} applies.  The
Hopf-derived primitive object is independent by construction in the
preceding chapter.
\end{proof}

\section{Action groupoids}
\label{sec:as-action}

Let $\mu$ act on $X$ and put
\[
    \mathfrak G=\mu\ltimes X.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Action Atiyah--Spencer calculus]
\label{thm:as-action-spencer}
The raw relation nerve is the action bar construction.  The chapter
constructions specialize to:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item action scalar Cech cochains and their complete
    partial-totalization filtration;
    \item Cartan and cofree action cobars;
    \item raw and formalized support-marked Cech systems and their separated
    exhaustive marked-word sectors; independently, the directional Artinian
    square-zero cross-effects, comonadic scalar-diagonal comparison, and
    multiweight cotangent operation system;
    \item the spectral partition-Lie action-algebroid controller obtained from
    the Artinian cotangent primitive system;
    \item the universal formalized scalar Spencer representation and global
    tangent enveloping action;
    \item the formalized adjoint representation obtained from the descended
    stabilizer-conjugation coefficient, together with its constructed
    comparison to actual stabilizer isotropy at the nonlinear stable level;
    \item the right-normalized mixed stabilizer commutator comparison through
    the independently differentiated actual-isotropy controller;
    \item the coefficient-connection obstruction tower and monadic twisting
    system.
\end{enumerate}
After rational dg rectification, the formalized package is the ordinary dg
action Lie-algebroid Spencer calculus on the explicit dg comparison scope.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The nerve of an action groupoid is the action bar.  Every assertion is the
corresponding specialization of the constructions already proved.
\end{proof}

\section{Gauge groupoids and Atiyah--Spencer geometry}
\label{sec:as-gauge}

Let
\[
    P_\chi\longrightarrow X
\]
be a principal right $G$-object and let
\[
    \mathfrak G
    :=
    \operatorname{Gauge}^{\mathrm{for}}(P_\chi).
\]
Retain from the preceding chapter the effective-image factorization
\[
    X\twoheadrightarrow I_\chi\hookrightarrow BG
\]
and the formal gauge quotient
\[
    X\twoheadrightarrow\widehat I_\chi.
\]

\begin{definition}[Raw gauge Spencer objects]
\label{def:as-gauge-spencer}
Define the raw gauge scalar Spencer cochains by
\[
    \operatorname{Sp}_{P_\chi,\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{geom},\bullet}
    :=
    \operatorname{Sp}_{\mathfrak G,\mathcal O}^{\mathrm{geom},\bullet},
\]
and use the corresponding Cartan and cofree gauge cobars for descent and
co-Kleisli operators.
\end{definition}

There are two different connection constructions.

\begin{definition}[Coefficient connection]
\label{def:as-gauge-coefficient-connection}
For a representation coefficient $M$ of the spectral gauge controller, a
coefficient connection is a point of
\[
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi}}^\pi(M).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Anchor-splitting connection]
\label{def:as-anchor-splitting-connection}
On the represented smooth coefficient sector, let
\[
    \rho_{\mathfrak G}:
    \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathfrak t_{X/S}
\]
be the represented underlying tangent anchor in
$\operatorname{QCoh}(X)$.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Conn}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{anch}}
    :=
    \operatorname{Map}_{(\operatorname{QCoh}(X))_{/\mathfrak t_{X/S}}}
    \left(
        \mathfrak t_{X/S},
        \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}
    \right).
}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Terminal identity-anchor algebroid on the gauge coefficient sector]
\label{con:as-terminal-identity-anchor}
On every coherent affine-etale pair
$(U,V)=(\operatorname{Spec}B,\operatorname{Spec}R)$, the preceding chapter
constructs the terminal spectral partition-Lie algebroid
\[
\boxed{
    \mathbb T_{B/R}
    :=
    \left(
        \mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1]
        \xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}
        \mathbb T_{B/R}^{\mathrm{pc}}[1]
    \right).
}
\]
Its algebra structure is constructed by terminality of the underlying
anchored object: there is a unique anchored morphism from the value of the
relative partition-Lie monad back to that terminal object, and the unit and
associativity identities hold by uniqueness.  Cotangent spectral-etale base
change identifies the terminal anchored objects on overlaps, and uniqueness
of the terminal algebra structures makes these identifications coherently
cocartesian.  They therefore descend to a global terminal identity-anchor
algebroid, denoted
\[
    \mathbb T_{X/S}.
\]
On the represented smooth coefficient sector, ordinary finite-locally-free
duality identifies its underlying tangent coefficient with
$\mathfrak t_{X/S}$.  Thus an ordinary anchor splitting
$h:\mathfrak t_{X/S}\to\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}$ is, under the represented
first-order identification, the underlying anchored morphism
\[
    U\mathbb T_{X/S}
    \longrightarrow
    U\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}.
\]
This construction uses the terminal algebraic algebroid itself and does not
identify the differentiated maximal formal relation with it.
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Anchor curvature by the morphism obstruction tower]
\label{con:as-anchor-curvature}
Let
\[
    \rho:\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}\longrightarrow\mathfrak t_{X/S}
\]
be the represented underlying anchor and let
$h:\mathfrak t_{X/S}\to\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}$ be a splitting of the
underlying coefficient map.  By
Construction~\ref{con:as-terminal-identity-anchor}, regard $h$ as the
underlying anchored map from the canonically constructed terminal
identity-anchor algebroid $\mathbb T_{X/S}$ to
$\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$.  Apply
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-morphism-obstruction-tower} to this
morphism-extension problem.  The anchor is the underlying map of the unique
algebroid morphism
$\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}\to\mathbb T_{X/S}$, and the composite
with $h$ is the identity of the terminal algebroid because
$\rho h=\operatorname{id}_{\mathfrak t_{X/S}}$.  Naturality of the morphism
obstruction tower therefore makes the weight-two obstruction map to zero
after applying $\rho$.  It consequently factors canonically through the
constructed homotopy anchor fibre.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal F_h^{\mathrm{anch}}
}
\]
to be this weight-two factorization.  The next weight of the universal
morphism-defect syzygy of
Construction~\ref{con:as-morphism-defect-syzygy} supplies its Bianchi
nullhomotopy without a flatness assumption.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Classical form of anchor curvature]
\label{prop:as-anchor-curvature-classical-form}
After rational dg rectification, and in particular for an ordinary smooth
Lie algebroid,
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal F_h^{\mathrm{anch}}(v,w)
    =
    [h(v),h(w)]-h([v,w]).
}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
In the dg rectification the relative operation monad is the ordinary
Lie-algebroid monad.  Its weight-two morphism relation is preservation of the
binary bracket and anchor.  Since $h$ already splits the anchor, the failure
of that relation is the displayed difference and lies in the anchor kernel.
The next weight of the morphism-defect syzygy is the ordinary Bianchi
identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Gauge Atiyah--Spencer package]
\label{thm:as-gauge-package}
The formal gauge groupoid canonically determines:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item raw and formalized gauge Cech/Cartan/cofree Spencer objects;
    \item raw-to-formalized marked Cech-cell maps and their aritywise
    discrepancies;
    \item the directional Artinian square-zero cross-effects, exact scalar
    diagonal Hurewicz comparison, cotangent cobar, and multiweight gauge
    operation system;
    \item the Artinian-formalized gauge controller, canonically equivalent to
    the spectral partition-Lie gauge controller;
    \item the formalized gauge adjoint representation together with the
    formalized-to-actual stable isotropy comparison;
    \item coefficient-connection spaces, curvature, Bianchi, and their
    complete weight obstruction towers;
    \item anchor-splitting connection spaces, anchor curvature, and Bianchi
    coherence wherever the represented anchor is available;
    \item the finite-order two-sided gauge distribution kernel algebra and
    complete raw/formalized scalar Cartan transport;
    \item the integral monadic Spencer twisting system and its canonically
    complete Maurer--Cartan convolution target.
\end{enumerate}

For an ordinary smooth principal bundle $P\to X$,
\[
    A(\operatorname{Gauge}(P))
    \simeq
    \operatorname{At}(P)
    =
    T_{P/S}/G
\]
and actual differentiated isotropy is
\[
    \operatorname{ad}(P)
    =
    P\times^G\mathfrak g.
\]
The anchor-splitting space is the classical principal-connection space.  For
a splitting
$\nabla:T_{X/S}\to\operatorname{At}(P)$,
\[
\boxed{
    \mathcal F_\nabla(v,w)
    =
    [\nabla v,\nabla w]
    -
    \nabla[v,w]
    \in
    \operatorname{ad}(P),
}
\]
and the next coherence is the classical Bianchi identity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The gauge controller and its actual isotropy are those of the preceding
chapter.  The raw/formalized Spencer, cotangent primitive, tangent
representation, formalized adjoint, and kernel-operator constructions apply
functorially to the formal gauge groupoid.

For the classical specialization, the preceding chapter identifies
\[
    \operatorname{At}(P)\longrightarrow T_{X/S}
\]
as the directly differentiated gauge anchor and its ordinary kernel with
$\operatorname{ad}(P)$.  Hence
Definition~\ref{def:as-anchor-splitting-connection} is exactly the classical
section space.  Construction~\ref{con:as-anchor-curvature} specializes to
the displayed failure of the splitting to preserve the binary bracket, and
its value lies in the anchor kernel.  The Bianchi theorem gives the usual
classical Bianchi identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Coefficient, adjoint, and principal connections]
A coefficient connection is a first-weight action on a representation
coefficient.  A principal connection is an anchor splitting.  The
formalized adjoint representation is a third construction, obtained from the
descended nonlinear adjoint group after quotient formalization.  None of
these spaces is identified by definition.  In classical situations the
ordinary adjoint representation on $\operatorname{ad}(P)$ can also be
constructed directly from the Atiyah bracket.
\end{remark}

\section{Ordinary smooth Lie groupoids}
\label{sec:as-classical}

Let
\[
    \mathcal H_1\rightrightarrows X
\]
be an ordinary smooth Lie groupoid over a smooth characteristic-zero base,
and let
$\widehat{\mathcal H}^{\,\mathrm{dR}}$
be its unitwise formalization.  The preceding chapter constructed
\[
    A(\mathcal H)
    =
    \ker(ds)|_{u(X)},
    \qquad
    \rho
    =
    dt|_{\ker(ds)},
\]
and the bracket from right-invariant source-vertical vector fields.

\begin{construction}[Right-invariant raw first-order Spencer operator]
\label{con:as-right-invariant-spencer-operator}
For a local section $\alpha$ of $A(\mathcal H)$, let
$\widetilde\alpha$ be the associated right-invariant source-vertical vector
field and let
\[
    \delta_{\widetilde\alpha}:
    \mathcal O_{\mathcal H_1}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal O_{\mathcal H_1}
\]
be its source-linear derivation.  On a represented local chart let
$\mathcal I$ be the ideal of the unit section.  If $a,b\in\mathcal I$, then
\[
    \delta_{\widetilde\alpha}(ab)
    =
    a\,\delta_{\widetilde\alpha}(b)
    +
    b\,\delta_{\widetilde\alpha}(a)
    \in
    \mathcal I.
\]
Hence restriction to the unit kills
$\delta_{\widetilde\alpha}(\mathcal I^2)$, so the composite with unit
restriction factors canonically through the first structured neighbourhood.
Using the represented identification of that neighbourhood with first
principal parts, define
\[
\boxed{
    D_\alpha:
    P^1_{\mathcal H}(\mathcal O_X)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal O_X
}
\]
to be this factorization.  Equivalently, locally it is induced by
\[
    \mathcal O_{\mathcal H_1}/\mathcal I^2
    \xrightarrow{\ \delta_{\widetilde\alpha}\ }
    \mathcal O_{\mathcal H_1}/\mathcal I
    \simeq
    \mathcal O_X.
\]
In particular,
\[
    D_\alpha(t^*f)=\rho(\alpha)(f)
\]
for local scalar functions $f$ on $X$.
\end{construction}

\begin{construction}[Right-invariant first-order commutator symbol]
\label{con:as-classical-commutator-symbol}
The maps
$D_\alpha,D_\beta:P^1_{\mathcal H}(\mathcal O_X)\to\mathcal O_X$ are
first-principal-parts symbols and are not composable as endomorphisms of a
single object.  Define
\[
\boxed{
    [D_\alpha,D_\beta]_{\mathrm{ri}}
}
\]
to be the first-principal-parts factorization of the unit restriction of the
ambient graded commutator
\[
    [\delta_{\widetilde\alpha},
      \delta_{\widetilde\beta}]:
    \mathcal O_{\mathcal H_1}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal O_{\mathcal H_1}.
\]
The ambient commutator is again a source-linear derivation.  Therefore its
unit restriction annihilates $\mathcal I^2$ by the same calculation used in
Construction~\ref{con:as-right-invariant-spencer-operator}, so the displayed
first-order factorization exists canonically.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Right-invariant operators recover the direct bracket]
\label{prop:as-classical-right-invariant-operator}
For local sections $\alpha,\beta$,
\[
\boxed{
    [D_\alpha,D_\beta]_{\mathrm{ri}}
    =
    D_{[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}}.
}
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On a represented local chart, $D_\alpha$ and $D_\beta$ are the unit
restrictions of the source-linear derivations
$\delta_{\widetilde\alpha}$ and $\delta_{\widetilde\beta}$ after passage to
the first structured neighbourhood.  Cech correspondence composition is
induced by groupoid multiplication, so convolution of the corresponding
first-order distributions is the successive action of the two
right-invariant derivations.  Their graded commutator is therefore induced by
\[
    [\delta_{\widetilde\alpha},\delta_{\widetilde\beta}]
    =
    \delta_{[\widetilde\alpha,\widetilde\beta]}.
\]
Right-invariant source-vertical vector fields are closed under commutator, and
restriction of
$[\widetilde\alpha,\widetilde\beta]$ to the unit is the definition of
$[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}$.  Passing through the same first-neighbourhood factorization is exactly the
definition of
$[D_\alpha,D_\beta]_{\mathrm{ri}}$ in
Construction~\ref{con:as-classical-commutator-symbol}, and gives
$[D_\alpha,D_\beta]_{\mathrm{ri}}=D_{[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Faithfulness of the first-principal-parts symbol]
\label{lem:as-classical-first-symbol-faithful}
For a smooth represented groupoid, the map
\[
    \alpha\longmapsto D_\alpha
\]
from local sections of $A(\mathcal H)$ to first-order principal-parts
operators is faithful.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Smoothness makes
\[
    \mathfrak a_{\mathcal H}^{\vee}
    =
    u^*L_{\mathcal H_1/X,s}
\]
finite locally free, with dual
$A(\mathcal H)$.  The first-principal-parts sequence for the scalar
coefficient is
\[
    \mathfrak a_{\mathcal H}^{\vee}
    \longrightarrow
    P^1_{\mathcal H}(\mathcal O_X)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal O_X.
\]
Restriction of $D_\alpha$ to the kernel is the evaluation pairing
\[
    \mathfrak a_{\mathcal H}^{\vee}
    \longrightarrow
    \mathcal O_X,
    \qquad
    \lambda\longmapsto\langle\lambda,\alpha\rangle.
\]
If $D_\alpha=D_\beta$, these evaluation maps agree.  Finite local freeness
identifies
$A(\mathcal H)\simeq(\mathfrak a_{\mathcal H}^{\vee})^\vee$, so
$\alpha=\beta$.  Notice that no faithfulness of the anchor action on
functions on $X$ is asserted; anchor-kernel elements may act trivially on
base functions.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Classical right-invariant bracket and represented formalized controller]
\label{thm:as-classical-bracket-comparison}
Assume, in addition to the standing smooth characteristic-zero hypotheses,
that the effective quotient of the unitwise formalized groupoid
\[
    Q_{\widehat{\mathcal H}^{\,\mathrm{dR}}}
\]
is represented in the locally coherent rational dg comparison range.  Then
for local sections $\alpha,\beta$ of $A(\mathcal H)$, the raw right-invariant
first-order Spencer symbols satisfy
\[
    [D_\alpha,D_\beta]_{\mathrm{ri}}
    =
    D_{[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}}.
\]
The represented quotient comparison identifies the first structured
coefficient with
$L_{X/Q_{\widehat{\mathcal H}^{\,\mathrm{dR}}}}$ and identifies its
continuous dual with the weight-one coefficient of the rationally rectified
formal-moduli controller
\[
    \mathbb A_{\widehat{\mathcal H}^{\,\mathrm{dR}}}^{\mathrm{Lie}}.
\]
The exact two-direction cotangent primitive identifies the first-order symbol
of the commutator of the corresponding controller operators with the binary
controller bracket.  Consequently
\[
\boxed{
    [\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}
    =
    [\alpha,\beta]_{\mathrm{controller}}
}
\]
under this represented effective-quotient first-order identification.  The
anchor comparison is the represented quotient first-order anchor theorem of
the preceding chapter.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The direct operator-symbol identity is
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-classical-right-invariant-operator} and requires no
quotient representability.  Under the additional hypothesis of the theorem,
the preceding chapter's represented quotient comparison applies to
$\widehat{\mathcal H}^{\,\mathrm{dR}}$: deformation formalization does not
change the represented effective quotient, and the underlying controller
coefficient is the continuous dual of cotangent transitivity
\[
    L_{X/S}
    \longrightarrow
    L_{X/Q_{\widehat{\mathcal H}^{\,\mathrm{dR}}}}.
\]
The first structured unit neighbourhood of the original smooth arrow geometry
survives unitwise formalization, so the first-principal-parts symbol attached
to a section $\alpha$ maps to the same represented first-order controller
coefficient.

Polarize the two first-order symbols by two independent square-zero
directions.  The commutator of their completed Cech convolution products has
order at most one: on associated graded the order-two symbols lie in the
graded-commutative symmetric algebra and cancel.  Its exact directional
bidegree $(1,1)$ is therefore its first-order symbol.  By
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-relative-operadic-bar-comparison},
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-bar-level-hurewicz}, and
Corollary~\ref{cor:as-integral-pd-arity}, rational rectification identifies
that exact scalar-diagonal symbol with the binary dg Lie-algebroid operation
of the represented controller.  On the raw smooth side,
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-classical-right-invariant-operator} identifies the
same first-order commutator symbol with
$D_{[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}}$.

Thus the first-principal-parts operators associated to
$[\alpha,\beta]_{\mathcal H}$ and to the controller bracket have the same
first-order symbol.  Smoothness makes the first cotangent coefficient finite
locally free, so
Lemma~\ref{lem:as-classical-first-symbol-faithful} implies equality of the
sections.  The anchor statement is the represented effective-quotient
first-order comparison of the preceding chapter.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Classical Jacobi and Leibniz]
\label{cor:as-classical-jacobi-leibniz}
The rational image of the arity-three Artinian Spencer coherence restricts
to the Jacobi identity for right-invariant vector fields, and
Corollary~\ref{cor:as-spencer-leibniz}
restricts to the ordinary Lie-algebroid Leibniz rule.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[PBW compatibility in the smooth range]
Whenever the explicit hypotheses of the rational PBW--Spencer theorem are
satisfied, the preceding bracket comparison is also the degree-one
commutator relation under
$\Theta_{\mathbb A^{\mathrm{Lie}}}^{\mathrm{Sp,dg}}$.  The bracket comparison
itself does not depend on that additional PBW theorem.
\end{remark}

\section{Functoriality and base change}
\label{sec:as-functoriality}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-object functoriality]
\label{thm:as-fixed-object-functoriality}
For fixed $X/S$, a morphism
\[
    F:\mathfrak G\longrightarrow\mathfrak H
\]
induces coherently functorial maps with the following variances.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item The relation nerves are covariant,
    \[
        \mathfrak G_\bullet\longrightarrow\mathfrak H_\bullet,
    \]
    while the right-correspondence jet comonads are contravariant,
    \[
        J_{\mathfrak H}\longrightarrow J_{\mathfrak G}.
    \]

    \item Geometric scalar Cech objects, Cartan cobars, cofree cobars, Spencer
    normalizations, and complete partial-totalization filtrations carry the
    induced contravariant coefficient maps.  Co-Kleisli operator rings carry
    the corresponding covariant maps obtained by precomposition with the jet
    comonad morphisms.

    \item Quotient formalization targets and formalized relation nerves are
    covariant; formalized jet comonads are contravariant.  Scalar restriction
    and formalized-to-raw Cartan-cobar comparison commute with these maps.

    \item Affine formal leaves, stable Artinian section linearizations,
    tangent representation categories, scalar Spencer cochain
    representations, formalized adjoint representations, and tangent
    enveloping monads carry the functoriality induced by formal-moduli
    differentiation.

    \item Raw and formalized support-marked Cech systems, their separated
    singleton-marked sectors, and geometric discrepancies are functorial.
    Directional Artinian square-zero cubes, support scalar Artinian bars,
    comonadic scalar-diagonal bars, primitive cotangent cobars, multi-filtered
    diagonal cotangent primitives, multiweight coLie operations, and PD
    substitution maps are separately functorial.

    \item The bar--cotangent, Spencer coLie, partition-Lie controller, and
    global integral comparison morphisms are natural.

    \item The filtered tangent monad, coefficient-connection spaces, rooted
    latching objects, curvature maps, Bianchi coherences, and flatness towers
    are natural.

    \item Principal-parts filtrations, finite-order pro-coherent kernel
    algebras, their left-Kan-extension reconstructions, and their actions on
    formalized Spencer cochains are natural.

    \item Raw/formalized Cartan transport, the normalized monadic twisting
    systems, and the Maurer--Cartan convolution completion are natural.

    \item The represented first-order and anchor-splitting constructions are
    functorial wherever their represented coefficients are defined.
\end{enumerate}
Every comparison theorem of the chapter is natural for the corresponding
variance.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The effective quotient functor is covariant in fixed-object formal-groupoid
morphisms.  A quotient map
$Q_{\mathfrak G}\to Q_{\mathfrak H}$ induces the relation map by fibre
product, while the counit of the intermediate dependent adjunction gives the
opposite jet-comonad map.  The scalar Cech and cobar variances are the
functoriality of dependent pullback/product and source-fixed decalage.

The preceding chapter proves functoriality of quotient formalization, formal
leaves, descended adjoint groups, and spectral differentiation.  Stable
Artinian sections, Schlessinger localization, slicing, stabilization,
multi-filtered square-zero formation, cotangent, support collapse,
normalization, rooted-tree latching, adic filtration, continuous duality,
kernel convolution, and left Kan extension are functorial.  The remaining
constructions are composites of these functors and canonical adjunction
mates, so identity, composition, and higher coherence are inherited from the
corresponding functoriality and mate-pasting coherences.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Arbitrary spectral base change of raw object-level constructions]
\label{thm:as-arbitrary-base-change}
For an arbitrary spectral base change $S'\to S$, pullback carries the
following raw \emph{objects and structure maps} to the corresponding
constructions for the base-changed formal groupoid by canonical equivalences:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the raw relation nerve and jet comonad;
    \item the geometric scalar Cech object;
    \item Cartan and cofree cobars;
    \item their Spencer normalizations and complete partial-totalization
    filtrations;
    \item complete Cartan transport;
    \item the raw support-marked Cech systems and bi-reduced commutator.
\end{enumerate}
On the represented first-order range, pullback also carries the first
structured neighbourhood, Atiyah extension, and principal-parts sequence to
their base changes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The formal-groupoid chapter proves arbitrary base change for the effective
relation and jet comonad.  Parameterized pullback is exact, strong symmetric
monoidal, and preserves the limits and colimits used in the displayed
object-level constructions.  It therefore commutes with Cech formation,
stable normalization, partial and complete totalizations, finite
support-collapse cubes, pointed smash products, and Cartan transport.  The
first-order statement is the represented structured-neighbourhood
base-change theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Base change of external co-Kleisli mapping spectra]
\label{prop:as-cokleisli-base-change-map}
For an arbitrary spectral base change $c:S'\to S$, there is a canonical map
\[
\boxed{
    \operatorname{Diff}_{\mathfrak G}(E,F)
    \longrightarrow
    \operatorname{Diff}_{\mathfrak G'}
    (c_X^*E,c_X^*F).
}
\]
For $E=F$ it is an $E_1$ morphism.  No equivalence is asserted for arbitrary
base change without a separate full-faithfulness or internal-Hom
base-change theorem.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The jet object-level base-change equivalence gives
\[
    c_X^*J_{\mathfrak G}E
    \simeq
    J_{\mathfrak G'}c_X^*E.
\]
Applying $c_X^*$ to a map $J_{\mathfrak G}E\to F$ gives a map from the left
side to $c_X^*F$, hence the displayed mapping-spectrum morphism.
Compatibility with co-Kleisli multiplication follows because the jet
base-change equivalence intertwines comultiplication and counit.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Formalized base-change strength]
The formalized package has the base-change strength of the global
Artinian-deformation reflector and formal-leaf coefficient system.  For the
spectral-etale variance proved in the preceding chapter the comparison maps
are equivalences.  For a general base change the universal properties still
supply canonical mate maps; no stronger equivalence is inferred from the raw
object-level base-change theorem.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Spectral-etale varying-object functoriality]
\label{thm:as-etale-varying-object}
Let
\[
    F:(\mathfrak G,X)\longrightarrow(\mathfrak H,Y)
\]
cover a spectral-etale map $f:X\to Y$.  On the locally coherent coefficient
sector there are compatible transported morphisms on
\[
    \mathbf{Sp}_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet},
    \qquad
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}},
    \qquad
    \mathbb U^\pi_{\mathbb A^\pi_{\mathfrak G}},
    \qquad
    \mathbb D_{\mathbb A^\pi_{\mathfrak G}}^{\mathrm{Sp,fin}},
\]
together with the formalized adjoint representation, formalized support-marked
Cech cells, directional Artinian square-zero cubes, comonadic scalar-diagonal
and cotangent primitive systems, multiweight operations, filtered tangent
monad, rooted obstruction tower, kernel distributions, and all
comparison/twisting maps.  They commute with
\[
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \longrightarrow
    f^*\mathbb A_{\mathfrak H}^{\pi,E_\infty}.
\]
No arbitrary varying-object partition-Lie pullback is asserted beyond this
spectral-etale variance.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the global formal-leaf functoriality and two-variable etale base-change
theorem of the preceding chapter.  Propositions~\ref{prop:as-representation-etale-descent} and~\ref{prop:as-stable-schlessinger-exists} give representation and stable
Artinian transport.  Cotangent, multi-filtered square-zero formation,
support-marked cells, adic principal parts, and finite continuous duals
commute with the same finite-Tor base change.  Proposition~\ref{prop:as-procoherent-kernel-convolution} gives monoidal base change for
kernel distributions, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-cellular-operator-independence} gives the
left-Kan-extension Beck--Chevalley equivalence.  The global tangent monad is
defined from these same cocartesian affine adjunctions.  All compatibility
maps therefore use the same transported adjunctions and satisfy the same
higher pasting coherence.
\end{proof}

\section{Etale descent and rational rectification}
\label{sec:as-descent-rectification}

\begin{theorem}[Etale descent for the formalized Spencer package]
\label{thm:as-spencer-etale-descent}
On the locally coherent qcqs spectral coefficient sector, the affine
assignments of:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item formal leaves and stabilized Schlessinger Spencer cochain
    coefficients;
    \item Artinian Spencer coLie coalgebras and Spencer partition-Lie
    algebroids;
    \item tangent representation categories, scalar Spencer representations,
    and formalized adjoint representations;
    \item affine tangent enveloping monads, their descended global monad,
    finite weight pieces, and connection/curvature/Bianchi obstruction
    towers;
    \item reduced support scalar Artinian bars, directional Artinian
    cross-effects, comonadic scalar-diagonal bars, primitive cotangent cobars,
    multi-filtered diagonal cotangent primitives, multi-filtered Artinian
    operation systems, and formalized support-marked Cech cells;
    \item finite-order pro-coherent kernel distribution algebras and normalized
    monadic Spencer twisting systems
\end{enumerate}
satisfy coherent spectral-etale descent.  The affine integral comparisons
glue to
\[
    \Xi_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Art}}:
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Sp,Art}}.
\]
The raw marked Cech maps form a compatible morphism into the descended
geometric-formalized marked Cech system.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The preceding chapter gives coherent etale descent for the pro-coherent and
partition-Lie coefficient stacks and etale naturality of affine
differentiation.  Proposition~\ref{prop:as-representation-etale-descent}
gives descent after slicing and stabilization.  The Schlessinger stable
reflector commutes with the same Artinian transport by
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-stable-schlessinger-exists}.  Cotangent and
multi-filtered square-zero base change identify the Artinian Spencer cells,
while continuous pro-coherent duality satisfies finite-Tor
Beck--Chevalley.  Support-marked Cech cells use exact pullback, finite total
cofibres, and normalization.  The formalized adjoint group is obtained by
right direct image and pullback, so its transition maps are the same
dependent-adjoint Beck--Chevalley maps.  Principal parts and finite
continuous duals satisfy finite-Tor base change, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:as-procoherent-kernel-convolution} makes that
base-change monoidal.  Controller-level distribution objects descend by left
Kan extension.  The tangent monad descends by
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-global-tangent-monad}; its normalized bar and rooted
latching diagrams are functorial in the descended monad.  Hence all affine
transition maps and their higher pasting data agree.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Rational rectification of the formalized Spencer package]
\label{thm:as-rational-spencer-rectification}
On rational coherent affine charts:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the bar--cotangent equivalence and Artinian Spencer coLie comonad
    rectify to their dg counterparts;
    \item the Spencer partition-Lie monad and multiweight PD cooperation
    system rectify to the shifted pro-coherent dg Lie-algebroid monad and its
    ordinary symmetric operation system;
    \item tangent representations and tangent enveloping monads rectify to dg
    Lie-algebroid representations and, on the explicit dg representation
    range, the ordinary bimodule enveloping action;
    \item completed principal-parts filtrations, pro-coherent kernel
    distribution algebras, the one-sided kernel action, and continuous source
    coduality rectify termwise to their dg analogues;
    \item the normalized one-marked monadic Spencer bar rectifies to the dg
    representation bar;
    \item the directional Artinian cross-effect and the comonadic
    scalar-diagonal Hurewicz comparison rectify termwise; the scalar Hurewicz
    map remains a comparison, while the diagonal cotangent primitive
    equivalence and primitive cotangent cobar rectify to the corresponding dg
    primitive operation comparison;
    \item the finite-stage construction of
    $\widehat{\mathbf{Sp}}_{F}^{\mathrm{fm},\bullet}$ rectifies termwise, and
    on the dg representation scope stabilized module Koszul duality together with
    the constructed Cartan Lie-derivative action gives the dg CE--Spencer
    equivalence; on the ordinary PBW cell scope the relative $R$-linear
    Cech--distribution bar comparison agrees with its underlying cochain
    equivalence.
\end{enumerate}
On the additional explicit perfectness, dg-model, and perfect-cell cofinality
scope of Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-pbw-spencer}, the ordinary
PBW--Spencer equivalence follows.  The CE--Spencer equivalence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:as-rational-ce-spencer} requires only its stated dg
representation scope and no perfect-cell cofinality.  These comparisons
descend spectral-etale locally.
Rationality alone does not force raw support-marked formalization
discrepancies, scalar-Hurewicz discrepancies, or formalized-adjoint/actual
isotropy comparisons to vanish.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Rational symmetric-monoidal rectification preserves cotangent complexes,
structured square-zero extensions, complete almost-finite objects,
pro-coherent coefficients, Artinian tests, and the coLie comonad by the
preceding chapter.  It carries multi-filtered Day convolution to rational dg
multi-filtration.  Finite-group Tate corrections in the spectral
partition-Lie operation system vanish rationally, giving ordinary symmetric
dg Lie operations.  Slicing and stabilization commute with rectification,
hence so do tangent representations and tangent monads.

Adic filtration is characterized by its adjunction and therefore rectifies;
finite principal-parts quotients and their two $B$-actions rectify termwise.
The pro-coherent kernel category is generated from continuous duals of the
finite-diagonal, bi-almost-perfect two-sided kernels by the same right-left
completion relations, so it also rectifies termwise.  The one-sided kernel
action and continuous source codual are defined on the same dense
almost-perfect generators by relative tensor product and internal Hom and
therefore rectify with the same right-left universal property.  Finally the
normalized monadic bar is built
from the monad unit, multiplication, and representation action and therefore
rectifies to the dg bar of the rectified representation.

The perfectness, dg-model, and perfect-cell cofinality conditions used for the
ordinary PBW comparison are separate theorem hypotheses exactly where the
ordinary tensor-dual and controller-level PBW formulas are invoked.  The
CE--Spencer comparison instead uses the stated dg representation range and
the stabilized module Koszul-duality construction above.  Nothing in
rational rectification makes the raw quotient formalization map, the scalar
Hurewicz map, or the adjoint counit comparison an equivalence.
\end{proof}

\section{Audit of constructions and theorem scopes}
\label{sec:as-audit}

The chapter contains raw intrinsic constructions, represented first-order
refinements, Artinian-formalized spectral constructions, and rational dg
comparisons.  Their separation records theorem strength rather than competing
axiomatic packages.

\subsection{Unconditional raw intrinsic constructions}

For every brave-new formal Lie groupoid the chapter constructs the iterated
jet endpoint correspondences; the geometric scalar Cech object; Cartan and
cofree cobars; the correctly indexed source-fixed decalage and extra
codegeneracy splitting; stable normalization and complete partial-totalization
filtrations; the co-Kleisli operator category and its cofree Spencer action;
separated singleton-marked cells; the face-closed support-marked simplicial
Cech system in the common-base stable category; the raw-to-formalized marked
comparison; strong monoidality of reduced stabilization; the bi-reduced
nonlinear isotropy commutator; and complete raw Cartan transport.

No Cartan coaction is attached to an arbitrary coefficient, no missing cobar
coface or codegeneracy is manufactured by convention, and no raw geometric
marked system is declared to be a PD operad.

\subsection{Represented first-order constructions}

On the represented first-order range the chapter constructs
$J_{\mathfrak G}^{(1)}$, the Atiyah extension and its section space,
quasi-coherent first principal parts, the sequence
\[
    \mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^\vee\otimes M
    \longrightarrow
    P^1_{\mathfrak G}(M)
    \longrightarrow
    M,
\]
the represented quotient identification
$\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak G}^\vee\simeq L_{X/Q_{\mathfrak G}}$, scalar
first-order linearization, the first differential of inversion, the
right-normalized mixed square-zero commutator coefficient, the faithful
first-principal-parts symbol in the smooth range, and represented
anchor-splitting spaces.  Connection comparison is proved by the common
square-zero lifting functor, not by silently dualizing an arbitrary
principal-parts extension.

\subsection{Artinian-formalized spectral constructions}

The chapter constructs the formalized relation and opposite-variance jet
comparison; formalized-to-raw scalar and Cartan-cobar restriction; relative
stable Schlessinger localization in the slice; explicit universal stable
Artinian sections; tangent representation categories; correctly typed
formalized scalar Spencer cochain representations; stabilized sliced
monadicity; affine and global tangent enveloping monads; the Cech diagonal
complete-almost-finite theorem; and the bar--cotangent equivalence.

The Artinian Spencer coLie endofunctor is
\[
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}
    =
    \operatorname{cot}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}
    \operatorname{sqz}_{B/R}.
\]
Its comonad structure is transported through the natural bar--cotangent
equivalence
\[
    G^{\check C}_{B/R}
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{cot}_{B/R}\operatorname{sqz}_{B/R}.
\]
The comonad counit is therefore the transported actual adjunction counit.  The
Cech-unit primitive map is proved, by naturality, to be exactly the transported
comultiplication.  No Cech degeneracy is used as a substitute for a map from a
cofree cotangent coefficient back to its generator.

On the adjoint side, the descended nonlinear adjoint group
$K_{\mathfrak G}\to Q_{\mathfrak G}$ is sent by right direct image to
\[
    K_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}
    =
    \Pi_{\ell_{\mathfrak G}}K_{\mathfrak G}
    \longrightarrow
    Q_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{fm}}.
\]
Pullback gives the formalized adjoint group on $X$ and the adjunction counit
gives
\[
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{Ad,fm}}
    \longrightarrow
    I_{\mathfrak G}^{\mathrm{for}}.
\]
Stable Artinian linearization of the formalized adjoint coefficient gives an
ambient $\mathbb A_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$-representation.  The chapter
does \emph{not} identify that representation coefficient with the independently
constructed actual-isotropy controller
$\mathfrak i_{\mathfrak G}^{\pi,E_\infty}$.

For higher operations the chapter first keeps the nonlinear geometric and
Artinian algebraic layers distinct.  The support-marked formalized relation
gives a stable object in $\mathcal E_S$; internal Hom into
$\mathcal O_S^{\mathrm{add}}$ gives its contravariant scalar dual, and
restriction along the canonical Artinian Cech test map gives the support
scalar bar $\mathbf{RedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A/B}(I)$.  This support cube is
not used to compute the multi-derivative.

The actual multi-derivative is the directional Artinian square-zero cube
$J\mapsto A_J$, whose total fibre is canonically the all-positive multiweight
part of the source-fixed Cech primitive:
\[
    \mathbf{DirRedBar}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{A_I/B}(I)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    N^\bullet F^{\mathbf1_I}\mathsf{CechPrim}_{A_I/B}^{\bullet}.
\]
Its degree-zero all-positive scalar coefficient maps canonically to the exact
multiweight scalar diagonal.

For finite $I$, the chapter constructs the unrestricted multi-filtered
cotangent--square-zero adjunction and its comonad
\[
    G_I
    =
    \operatorname{cot}^{I}_{B/R}
    \operatorname{sqz}^{I}_{B/R}.
\]
The canonical cotangent--square-zero adjunction cobar of $A_I$ gives the scalar
diagonal cobar $\mathsf{ScCotCobar}^{\bullet}$ and the cofree cotangent cobar.
The relative Hurewicz morphism and diagonal cotangent equivalence assemble to
\[
    h_I^{\mathrm{comon}}:
    \mathbf{ScCotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \longrightarrow
    \mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I).
\]
The cotangent cobar is correctly indexed by
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I^{n+2}(D_I)$ in degree $n$ and its
cosimplicial totalization is
$\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbf1_I}G_I(D_I)$.  Hence
\[
    \operatorname{Tot}_{\mathrm{DK}}\mathbf{CotBar}_{B/R}(I;D_I)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{CotPrim}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-)
    \xrightarrow{\sim}
    \operatorname{coOp}^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{B/R}(I;-).
\]
The Cech and comonadic cosimplicial directions meet only at the exact scalar
diagonal and are not identified.

The anchor-weight filtration is obtained from the unrestricted filtered
cotangent--square-zero adjunction and the preceding chapter's theorem that the
actual filtered cotangent object is connective, almost perfect, and complete.
Tail connectivity and continuous duality prove exhaustivity of the dual
partition-Lie filtration.  No pronilpotence or exhaustivity datum is added.
The full partition-Lie filtration obeys the arity-resolved substitution law;
the additive $F_{\le r+s}$ law is asserted only for the one-marked tangent
monad.
